# Cele 697 de capabilități Hermes — status cod

[Statusul sprintului](../HERMES_STATUS.md) · [Reguli și livrări](HERMES_SPRINT.md)

ID-urile H001–H697 sunt poziții în inventarul înghețat, validate prin hash. Folosește căutarea paginii pentru nume/ID sau comanda `show` pentru cerința originală completă. «Audit 07.09» înseamnă moștenit, nu verificat din nou. Dovezile legate sunt fișiere inspectate; simpla lor existență nu dovedește întregul flux și nu afirmă că testele au fost rulate în această livrare.

| <a id="h002"></a>H002 | Run one scripted agent turn from a pipe and get a machine-readable cost report | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | H002 whole-contract review: \`nerva chat -z\` preserves final-answer-only stdout, bounded piped/file/argument input, nonzero refusal/failure/usage/auth/unreachable/interrupt exits, confirmed versus requested session attribution, and an atomic nerva.chat.usage.v1 receipt on ordinary success and failure paths. It never approves a queued action; pending IDs remain queued and force an incomplete result. Interactive output/exit compatibility remains while its optional receipt can report incomplete. The hub supplies request-owned bounded generation usage on JSON chat replies and SSE end events. Accepted owned generation dispatch attempts are observed after quota, physical data-handling and host/protocol checks. Retries, specialists, synchronous response-driving auxiliary generation, streaming and synthesis are included; control probes, embeddings/audio and explicitly detached title/review/cache jobs are excluded. Task-owned pending attempts prevent cross-task usage attribution; nested scopes preserve provenance, inactive inherited scopes cannot authenticate a model, unknown POSTs during generation make totals unknown, and closed/bounded collectors reject late or overflowing observations. Tokens require complete valid provider counters for every attempt; failed/partial retries cannot hide behind later success. Local zero needs positive provider/route/host proof and complete counters. Exact requested models confirmed against the wire can use dated repository estimates; unsupported cache-write premiums, Gemini Pro long-context tiers, unknown models and uncertain provenance stay unpriced. Mixed identities receive a bounded breakdown. The provider field denotes the backend adapter, not an attestation of remote weights or an invoice. Source-assigned runtime stops survive synthesis independently of prose: settled tool-loop exits, exact thinking exhaustion, terminal agent failures/timeouts, policy/continuation refusal and terminal synthesis errors make one-shot execution incomplete. Stop metadata is finite and bounded; malformed present metadata fails closed. Legacy hubs retain text-fallback limits. Successful deterministic synthesis fallback may still return complete specialist answers; a failed observed attempt keeps its usage unknown. Authorization and early owner/session preflight errors retain their HTTP failure codes and can yield unavailable telemetry while still producing a failure receipt. CLI validators reject malformed, missing, negative, boolean, nonfinite, oversized or inconsistent telemetry without crashing or changing the answer verdict; error objects carry only bounded projected telemetry. Verification includes real owned HTTP MockTransport calls through actual backend adapters, actual ASGI JSON/SSE, concurrent/nested/late task behavior, runtime-stop to synthesis to HTTP to CLI, malformed receipts, pending approvals, transport failures, real isolated FastAPI lifespan/CLI smoke and the frozen backend milestone with its explicitly recorded outcome. No live-provider, native Windows/device or invoice claim is made. Unrelated Hermes flags, invocation IDs and writing through a symlink are outside the frozen acceptance; the atomic replace behavior is retained. Interrupt review and regressions verify exit 130 before POST and after decoded response/output, preserving observed session, usage, stops and pending IDs; already emitted terminal text cannot be retracted. The full frozen run was not green (32 failed cases); all 32 original nodes pass after fixture/status repairs, and the isolated interrupt fix passes integrated CLI coverage. | <a id="h015"></a>H015 | Create and manage scheduled jobs, and see why past runs failed | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Whole frozen H015 contract reviewed, batch 06 (2026-10-10). CLI/API support list/create/edit/pause/resume/run/remove/status/tick, durable runs, incidents, independent KV notes and read-only doctor. List provides global scheduler status; status provides job/request outcomes. A completed one-shot rearms through edit to a distinct future time and may then run immediately under the same gate/reservation. Direct actions reserve one pending run before execution and finish that row atomically with counters/output and any failure incident. Cancellation/orphan recovery yields unknown without replay, only while the execution gate proves no cooperating executor is live. Scripts reserve one one-shot/repeat slot in their own CAS transaction, freeze input notes and reconcile once. Deleted configuration cannot deliver a later model result; history and acknowledged incidents survive reopening. Terminal history is bounded at 200 rows/job and 10,000 globally with active references protected. Incidents group normalized redacted failures by job/signature, expose numeric IDs and detected/closed state, acknowledge idempotently, preserve the three-failure pause and never invent an alerted transition. Closed matching signatures remain closed. Diagnostic redaction covers known environment/settings/catalogue secrets before cuts and fails closed. Notes are separate from previous model output: 128 printable characters/key, 16 KiB/value, 64 KiB/job and 256 keys, enforced transactionally. Continuity defaults on; disabling omits previous output but keeps KV and saves the latest bounded output. Both enter shared direct/script asks as JSON-escaped fenced untrusted data. Additive continuity edits merge atomically without replacing other options. Detailed model failures become failed attempts before delivery; legitimate queued-approval answers and silent suppression retain their meanings. Existing script/URL monitors skip the model when unchanged. Native no_agent executes a bounded approved Python command rather than introducing a shell. Scripts need fresh risk-3 ASK per attempt; URL hops remain plugin.egress. Task jobs preserve kind, payload, risk floor and host-bound inbound:job identity through the live governed worker as ASK, with no raw-queue fallback or standing grant. Existing action gates remain on direct ask/delivery paths. All five new routes are admin guarded. Core 476/476, surface 137 passed/3 unavailable-Fish skips, 43 route/doctor guards and actual disposable lifespan CLI smoke passed. Full backend milestone is separately required before assessment update. H451 watchdog/recovery, H452 classifier/alerting/preflight, H454 cross-job context and new dedicated HUD/mobile controls remain separate. No live-provider/native-host claim. | <a id="h067"></a>H067 | Pending-input interception (clarify answers, three-way confirm, destructive-command confirm, ephemeral notices) | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Whole frozen H067 contract re-reviewed, batch09 (2026-10-10). A fresh app now registers the existing clarify ToolRPC after the initial effective settings load on the same live server, preserving default-off, restart-to-apply, existing specs and handler-time disabling/revocation. No executor rebuild, raw-setting authority bypass or new permission is introduced. The initial actual app regression exposed the missing registration before repair. Per-chat/channel/route/verified-sender pending identity supports next-message numeric/exact-label/multiselect answers and free text through open-ended or Other prompts. Valid choices still canonicalize after Other, matching the frozen donor; only unmatched text falls back to raw Other input, and a mixed invalid multiselect is not partially accepted. Whole-reply parsing, bounds, command fallthrough, first-writer resolution and cancellation remain. Native prompts bind the delivered bot message, current sender/route, revision and transport generation; pairing and prompt interception run before occupied model lanes. Wrong-principal, stale, replayed, expired and undelivered replies cannot resolve a wait. Prompts are ephemeral across restart with bounded cleanup and human-wait credit only after actual delivery. Typed/native Once/Always/Cancel confirmations accept the documented aliases. Destructive new/reset/undo paths acquire current session leases and recheck owner, session generation, transcript identity/revision, clock and transport generation after the wait. New/reset preserves saved history; undo removes only the last exchange via durable rewind. Always queues the narrowly scoped permission.grant selector through governed intake and reports pending until a real approved worker decision installs the grant. No in-memory standing consent, forged grant or file rollback is substituted; revocation, expiry and restart remain fenced. EphemeralReply carries validated TTL through signed delivery; default TTL zero matches the donor. Only exact successfully acknowledged own-bot messages may be deleted on supported Telegram/Slack/Discord transports, with bounded owned cleanup and no claim that remote I/O can be forcibly undone. Unsupported transports retain notices. ntfy has the documented authenticated text-only mapping. Native channels, session consent/kernel/history and receipt behavior are covered by offline integration suites. Optional SDK dependencies were isolated under scratch, allowing all previously-skipped Slack/Discord cases to run without modifying the other batch environment. Actual fresh-app HTTP/model/ToolRPC discovery and answer resumes the same conversation with auth, invalid-choice, cross-principal and replay refusals; model responses are offline synthetic HTTP, with no live-account/provider/native-host claim. The two tool-loop generations and one auxiliary naming response are explicit. An unrelated pre-existing H673 nonstream context-anchor warning remains open; H002 usage is recorded before that callback and is not lost. Wider CLI/email extension contracts are separate. Frozen full backend milestone completed 25,951 cases: 25,906 passed, five failures and 40 skipped/xfail; all 5,718 inputs were verified unchanged before repair. All original failures pass after three timing/collision fixture repairs and an honest optional TLS-import provenance annotation; affected complete modules pass 245+130 cases, and both provenance guards also pass without SDKs. This is not a green-full-run claim. Whole clauses were independently re-reviewed and the original Other finding resolved. Frontend/mobile code did not change; prior completed 2,181/359 results are reused, not rerun. Current source references: agents/core/orchestrator.py:1226-1234; agents/core/channels/pending_input.py:115-167. | <a id="h117"></a>H117 | Message batching and debounce (treat a burst as one turn) | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Message batching remains per (chat, sender), in arrival order, with the existing 0.35-second rolling window, 1-second cap and 32-part bound (agents/core/channels/batching.py:45-47). Telegram keys the batch by (chat, sender) and, inside a forum topic, also by the topic id (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:1306-1308); Discord keys by (channel, author) (agents/core/channels/discord.py:156) and Slack by (channel, member, thread) (agents/core/channels/slack.py:649-650). Ordinary Telegram callbacks flush held batches and enter the chat lane; ordinary messages, attachment descriptions, command and reason ordering and merged-text injection scanning retain their existing paths: a command flushes what was held and is delivered alone (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:1309-1314), and the Gateway scans the merged turn normalised (agents/core/channels/gateway.py:275-278). Reserved autc reusable-consent callbacks are an explicit exception: they intercept before the normal batch flush. A registered current callback uses a separate bounded 32-task lane to release its waiting owner/model turn; stale, malformed, replayed, unregistered or overflowing reserved callbacks refuse promptly without entering the occupied ordinary lane. The existing live aut1 owner-once callback retains its separate post-flush lane. This is deliberate collateral behavior review with existing batching/reason/owner-once regressions, not a new batching feature or renewed mutation receipt. Batch collateral review (2026-10-04, base 9bfa085c): Native reusable-consent \`/deny\` is now a reserved direct reply to the delivered card. It dispatches outside the occupied chat lane and consumes stale or malformed \`/deny\` without forwarding it as a model turn. Ordinary batching, callback, command and reason paths retain their existing ordering. This is a scoped lane exception, not new batching equivalence or live Telegram proof. Owner-once denial collateral review (2026-10-04, base 22005244): an original current \`/deny \[reason\]\` reply to the delivered owner-once card is now another reserved pre-flush exception. Telegram selects exactly one current owner-once or reusable-consent registry, dispatches the selected reply outside the occupied ordinary chat lane, and consumes stale, ambiguous and malformed \`/deny\` without forwarding text to the model. The (chat, sender) batching window, cap, part bound and ordinary callback/command/reason paths are unchanged. The new native owner-once test exercises the occupied-lane reply and refusal paths; this preserves the prior batching verdict without a live Bot API or new mutation claim. Pending-input drift re-read (2026-10-11, PR #1249 head): the h067 pending-input work added two more reserved pre-batch exceptions and does not change the batching contract. (1) A \`h067:\` callback is dispatched on its own bounded 32-task lane before the consent/owner-once callback handling and before any batch flush (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:882-889); with no hook, a bad id or a full lane it is answered "Not applied." without entering the ordinary lane. (2) A fresh, directly authored, group-admitted text with no attachment that is not a claimed decision reason is first offered to the Gateway's pending handler (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:1045-1047; agents/core/channels/gateway.py:77-78 runs it ahead of the rate limit, pairing and the merged-text scan), and only a consumed clarification answer skips batching; anything not consumed continues down the unchanged reason/batch path. Discord offers original human messages the same way before the batch submit (agents/core/channels/discord.py:156). The held Telegram batch now carries session metadata, and coalescing keeps a pending_input_eligible=False mark sticky across the merged pieces (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:1315-1318). The topic-scoped key and the sticky mark are not exercised by the pinned regressions; the 0.35 s / 1.0 s / 32-part contract, the command and reason ordering, the Discord and Slack bursts and the shutdown discard are, and all 301 pinned tests pass at this head. Slack's wake coalescing moved into _enqueue_event_locked with the same effect, and the H677 test drift is warm-up fixtures plus the ephemeral-delete handle in the Telegram stop test, not batching. [d1](../agents/core/channels/batching.py) [d2](../agents/core/channels/telegram.py) [d3](../agents/core/channels/discord.py) |

DEBUG under ALL and CRITICAL under ERROR remain colored; the component menu reflects the bounded scan. Backend log regressions passed in the focused H049 selection; all 14 existing Logs panel tests and frontend TypeScript passed in this batch. No browser/native-host/deployment claim. | <a id="h156"></a>H156 | Edit an agent's persona and description from the UI | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | An agent's persona can now be edited in the UI and is live on its next turn. That is Hermes' persona editor, over the version history Nerva already kept. Until now the history was a side record: a commit or rollback in the Prompts panel changed a JSON file while the agent kept its old persona. agents/core/soul_edit.py holds the one path an edit takes, apply_soul (:152). (1) Refusals. An agent the hub has not loaded is refused (404; agent_folder, :100, returns an enumerated folder name in the id alphabet, so no request value reaches a path). So is text over MAX_SOUL_BYTES (256 KiB of UTF-8, :40; 413), safe mode (:171; 409, because overlays are not read there, H275) and a persona the H387 guard would drop (:174; 422 with the verdict). guard_verdict (:60) runs the same scan and cap as Agent._load_soul, and GET /api/agents/{id}/soul now reports it through the same function. A line the guard only quarantines is applied, and the verdict names it. (2) Nothing live is lost. The first edit keeps the file on disk as v1 ("on disk before the first edit"). A file changed on disk since the last version, such as a hand edit, is kept as its own version ("on disk, changed outside the editor") before it is replaced (:185). An agent with no persona file has nothing to keep. (3) Written where the model reads it. The text goes to the owner's overlay (overlay_path, :115): souls/\<id\>/SOUL.local.md in the data home, else the repo-local SOUL.local.md, never the shipped SOUL.md. The write is atomic (_write_atomically, :126: temporary file, fsync, replace; a failure leaves no temporary file and records no new version). (4) Versioned, then live. The text is committed (:195; the same text twice is one version). The agent re-reads its persona (_load_soul, :197), and the result says whether the running agent now reads the written file. (5) Audited without the text. A SETTINGS_CHANGE row names the agent, the version and its hash. A plain commit and a history-only rollback are audited the same way (record_version, :224; agents/core/routers/admin.py:805, :860). Surfaces. PUT /api/admin/agents/{id}/soul {content, message} (agents/core/routers/agents_api.py:132, admin). POST /api/admin/prompts/{id}/rollback applies the version through the same path for a loaded agent (agents/core/routers/admin.py:846); the shared contract _identity (served from IDENTITY.local.md) and keys that are no loaded agent keep the history-only rollback. Description. A description: field in the SOUL front-matter is the agent's one-line description (description_of, :84, and description_in, :89: one line, at most 400 characters). It is listed on GET /agents and the SOUL read (agents/core/routers/agents_api.py:179, :124) and is never part of the prompt. POST /api/admin/agents/{id}/description/draft (agents/core/routers/agents_api.py:150; draft_description, :238) asks the strict-local model for one paragraph from the live persona and saves nothing. With no local model it is a 503, never a cloud call (:255-258). HUD. The Dossier has an Edit persona button that opens the live SOUL in frontend/src/soul-edit.tsx (SoulEditor, :49, mounted at frontend/src/modes.tsx:96). It offers Preview against the live text, Apply, Draft description with Use it (withDescription, :15, writes the front-matter line), and the hub's refusal in words (refusalText, :32). The Dossier also shows the description. The Prompts panel (PromptsPanel in frontend/src/gap.tsx, not pinned: high-churn) gets apply beside commit, through the same PUT, and says when a rollback is live. Two survivors of the first-edit-only seed led to keeping hand edits as versions; that code's own five mutants are caught. The fsync is a crash-only property. HUD contrast collateral review (2026-10-09): foreground-only changes preserve this capability and its authority; changed source/test pins reviewed by gpt-6-sol/high, with full HUD and focused follow-up tests. Scope and browser evidence: docs/hud-muted-text-contrast-20261009.md. Independent #1233 review (historical scope): An agent's persona can now be edited in the UI and is live on its next turn. That is Hermes' persona editor, over the version history Nerva already kept. Until now the history was a side record: a commit or rollback in the Prompts panel changed a JSON file while the agent kept its old persona. agents/core/soul_edit.py holds the one path an edit takes, apply_soul (:152). (1) Refusals. An agent the hub has not loaded is refused (404; agent_folder, :100, returns an enumerated folder name in the id alphabet, so no request value reaches a path). So is text over MAX_SOUL_BYTES (256 KiB of UTF-8, :40; 413), safe mode (:171; 409, because overlays are not read there, H275) and a persona the H387 guard would drop (:174; 422 with the verdict). guard_verdict (:60) runs the same scan and cap as Agent._load_soul, and GET /api/agents/{id}/soul now reports it through the same function. A line the guard only quarantines is applied, and the verdict names it. (2) Nothing live is lost. The first edit keeps the file on disk as v1 ("on disk before the first edit"). A file changed on disk since the last version, such as a hand edit, is kept as its own version ("on disk, changed outside the editor") before it is replaced (:185). An agent with no persona file has nothing to keep. (3) Written where the model reads it. The text goes to the owner's overlay (overlay_path, :115): souls/\<id\>/SOUL.local.md in the data home, else the repo-local SOUL.local.md, never the shipped SOUL.md. The write is atomic (_write_atomically, :126: temporary file, fsync, replace; a failure leaves no temporary file and records no new version). (4) Versioned, then live. The text is committed (:195; the same text twice is one version). The agent re-reads its persona (_load_soul, :197), and the result says whether the running agent now reads the written file. (5) Audited without the text. A SETTINGS_CHANGE row names the agent, the version and its hash. A plain commit and a history-only rollback are audited the same way (record_version, :224; agents/core/routers/admin.py:805, :860). Surfaces. PUT /api/admin/agents/{id}/soul {content, message} (agents/core/routers/agents_api.py:132, admin). POST /api/admin/prompts/{id}/rollback applies the version through the same path for a loaded agent (agents/core/routers/admin.py:846); the shared contract _identity (served from IDENTITY.local.md) and keys that are no loaded agent keep the history-only rollback. Description. A description: field in the SOUL front-matter is the agent's one-line description (description_of, :84, and description_in, :89: one line, at most 400 characters). It is listed on GET /agents and the SOUL read (agents/core/routers/agents_api.py:179, :124) and is never part of the prompt. POST /api/admin/agents/{id}/description/draft (agents/core/routers/agents_api.py:150; draft_description, :238) asks the strict-local model for one paragraph from the live persona and saves nothing. With no local model it is a 503, never a cloud call (:255-258). HUD. The Dossier also shows the description. | <a id="h157"></a>H157 | Edit every configuration key from the UI | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Every configuration key is reachable and searchable from the UI, and every one but a secret or a route-only row (settings_db.ROUTE_ONLY: the skill switches, the voice command providers, the security data-handling acknowledgments) is portable and resettable. The backend it builds on: \`GET /api/admin/settings\` (agents/core/routers/admin.py:73) returns every category, and the per-category PUT (agents/core/routers/admin.py:194) validates with \`validate_category\` and answers 422 before any write, auditing the changed key names and the new value of a choice only (\`_audited_values\`, agents/core/routers/admin.py:124). New in H157: \`GET /api/admin/settings/export\` (agents/core/routers/admin.py:78, registered before the \`/{category}\` read so it is never taken for one) returns the declared settings as a \`nerva-settings/1\` document with an attachment disposition, leaving out every secret (encrypted at rest, or named like a token, secret, password, client id or API key), \`mcp.servers\` (it holds credentials by design) any URL that carries a password (\`http://user:pw@host\`, in a list too), and any free-form value the H495 secret scanner would mask (or every free-form value, when the scanner cannot run), and naming each left-out setting with its reason (export_settings, agents/core/settings_db.py:1317; is_secret_setting, agents/core/settings_db.py:1263; _looks_like_credential, agents/core/settings_db.py:1296; _url_password, agents/core/settings_db.py:1280). \`POST /api/admin/settings/import\` (agents/core/routers/admin.py:325) takes such a document (at most 1 MB and 1,000 keys, the body counted as it streams in, so a chunked upload is bounded too: _bounded_body, agents/core/routers/admin.py:312). The import and the reset take only an \`application/json\` request (415 otherwise, a type a browser must pre-check before sending) and refuse one the browser marks cross-site (403), since the admin guard trusts loopback on a default install (_refuse_cross_site_write, agents/core/routers/admin.py:298); every reply is no-store, and only a JSON \`true\` makes a dry run. A NaN, an Infinity or nesting too deep to store is refused (422), never stored, an \`mcp.servers\` entry needs a name (validate_category, agents/core/settings_db.py:1112, via _mcp_servers_problem, agents/core/settings_db.py:1184; the loader skips a nameless entry too, MCPManager.load_from_config, agents/core/mcp/client.py:814), and 2000 and 2000.0 are the same number, so no change (_same_value, agents/core/settings_db.py:1405). In the document every key must be declared and passes the same \`validate_category\` a single write does, so a posture flag or an out-of-range value is refused like any other; one refusal and nothing is written (422 with every reason) (plan_import, agents/core/settings_db.py:1349). Only the keys whose value differs are written, in one sqlite transaction, all or nothing, secrets encrypted as a single write encrypts them (apply_import, agents/core/settings_db.py:1439), with one audit row naming the settings (and the new value of a choice); \`"dry_run": true\` answers what would change, a secret's values shown as \`(secret)\` and a credential-shaped free value as \`(hidden)\`, so the preview never echoes one (describe_changes, agents/core/settings_db.py:1413). \`POST /api/admin/settings/{category}/reset\` (agents/core/routers/admin.py:391) puts one category back to its declared values and names the keys that moved, with an audit row; it keeps the category's secrets and its route-only rows (the skill switches, the voice command providers, the security data-handling acknowledgments) and names them (kept, reset_kept, agents/core/settings_db.py:1457; the HUD counts every kept row as a secret) and names the settings the product posture still forces (overridden, posture_overridden, agents/core/settings_db.py:1466), where the global reseed (agents/core/routers/admin.py:430) was the only reset (reset_category, agents/core/settings_db.py:1564). The v2 console SettingsPanel (frontend/src/gap.tsx:2754; widgets typed by settingsField, frontend/src/gap.tsx:2731) now has a search across every category matching a setting's category.key or label, with a match count and an honest no-match line (settingMatches, frontend/src/panels/settings-tools.tsx:37); a reset per category heading (only one with declared rows) that asks first, by a second click that names how many settings go back and moves the focus to itself, drops the category's unsaved edits once it runs and names what was kept and what the posture still forces (ResetCategory, frontend/src/panels/settings-tools.tsx:92); edits hidden by the search are counted beside the save; and a MOVE A CONFIGURATION section (SettingsTransfer, frontend/src/panels/settings-tools.tsx:187) that downloads the export and names what the hub left out, and imports a pasted or chosen file by showing the hub's dry run (each change old → new) and applying it only on a second step, listing every reason on a refusal; a pasted \`dry_run\` is dropped so the apply step is never a dry run, the text is held while the hub answers, and the file picker is reachable from the keyboard. The standalone /admin app's GlobalConfigPage (agents/web/static/admin.js:447) is unchanged, and \`nerva config list\|get\|set\|check\` (cmd_config in agents/cli/nerva.py; the file is high-churn and no longer pinned by this row) still edits single keys offline. tests/test_settings_transfer.py (28 cases) covers the export (every declared key either exported or named as left out, secrets, client ids, mcp.servers, a token-shaped value, a scanner that cannot run, the stored value), the import (an export imports back as no change, valid changes, every refusal a single write makes, nothing written on one refusal, one transaction, the posture flag, a secret encrypted and never echoed, the bound), the per-category reset, and the routes (admin only, the export not read as a category, preview then apply with one audit row, every reason on a refusal, no row when nothing changes, reset and 404); tests/test_h157b_settings_review.py (30, the first review) covers the JSON-only and cross-site refusals, URL passwords, a credential in a list, the hidden preview, NaN, Infinity and deep nesting, nameless MCP servers, number equality, secrets kept on a reset, the posture's settings named, the change listeners, the 1 MB bound for a sized and a chunked body and the JSON-only dry run; frontend/src/test/settings-tools.test.tsx (12) covers the search, the two-step reset, the export note, the dry-run preview and the second-step apply with the admin credential, a refusal's reasons, text that is not JSON, an import that changes nothing, a reset that drops unsaved edits and names kept secrets, edits hidden by the search, a pasted dry run never applied and the keyboard-reachable file button. 25 backend mutants, all caught (one after its case was added: a scanner that cannot run). The first adversarial review (review-H157: 2 major, 7 minor, 12 nit) found that a page the owner visited could post a plain-text import to a loopback hub, and that a URL password was exported; all are fixed, and its 18 mutants were all caught but one equivalent (two after their cases were pinned). Test manual: PNB-171, PNB-172; the API sweep lists the three routes. Adapted from Hermes: no raw YAML editor (the typed widgets and the JSON import cover it, and a free-form editor would bypass the per-key widgets), and export/import are JSON documents rather than a Form/YAML toggle. | <a id="h165"></a>H165 | In-app documentation | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Built on this branch. Hermes has a Documentation page inside its dashboard with an "open in a new tab" affordance. Nerva's written material (the user guide, FLAGS.md on what each posture flag costs, and the privacy and camera-privacy notes) was only in the repository. The Dockerfile copied it into the image, but nothing served it. (1) The route. agents/core/routers/help_docs.py is registered on the app in agents/web.py (not pinned: a high-churn file; the pinned route tests drive web.app) and user-guarded on the router (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:27). - GET /api/help/docs (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:103) lists the allowlist: slug, title, whether the file shipped, and each document's sections. - GET /api/help/docs/{slug} (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:113) returns the Markdown text, capped at MAX_DOC_CHARS with a truncated flag (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:121). - The allowlist is the fixed DOCS table (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:31): user-guide, flags, privacy, camera-privacy. A slug is only ever a key into it, never joined into a path, so an unknown slug, '../' or encoded traversal is 404. - A file must be a regular file under the install root; a symlink is refused (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:85). A file missing from an install reads as "not available", not an error. - Responses are no-store. (2) The page. DocsPanel (frontend/src/panels/docs.tsx:37) is registered in the console's Start group (frontend/src/console-routes.ts:6), in PANEL_COMPONENTS (frontend/src/gap.tsx:5277) and in the desktop app's route allowlist (desktop/src-tauri/hud-routes.json:49). - It lists the documents, disabling one not shipped, and renders the chosen one through the new shared Markdown component (frontend/src/markdown.tsx:101). - That component emits React elements only (no innerHTML path): headings with anchors, code fences, pipe tables, lists, quotes and rules, plus bold, italic, inline code and links. A link renders only for http(s) or a same-origin path, in a new tab with rel=noopener noreferrer (safeHref, frontend/src/markdown.tsx:25). Raw HTML in a document stays literal text. There is no regex lookbehind, so older Safari can parse the bundle. (3) Open in a new tab. Every document is a standalone deep link, /v2/console/docs?doc=\<slug\>\[#section\] (docHref, frontend/src/panels/docs.tsx:15). The panel offers it as "open in a new tab ↗" with rel=noopener noreferrer (frontend/src/panels/docs.tsx:67), and a #section is scrolled into view (frontend/src/panels/docs.tsx:49). (4) The flag's cost where it is flipped, per the row rationale. - Each document's sections carry the names their headings document. The hub computes them with heading_anchor (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:44), which one shared fixture pins character for character to the renderer's headingAnchor (frontend/src/markdown.tsx:94). - The Settings panel links every setting FLAGS.md documents to its section with a "cost ↗" beside the toggle (sectionIndex, frontend/src/panels/docs.tsx:20; frontend/src/gap.tsx:2757, frontend/src/gap.tsx:2849). - The security posture panel links to the flags document (frontend/src/gap.tsx:1034). The route/auth/OpenAPI snapshots, the HUD parity rules, the API sweep chapter and frontend/src/api/schema.gen.ts are regenerated. Tests: - tests/test_help_docs_route.py, 25 cases: - the list and each allowlisted document (tests/test_help_docs_route.py:35, tests/test_help_docs_route.py:49); - seven off-list and traversal URLs, all 404 (tests/test_help_docs_route.py:67); - the guard (tests/test_help_docs_route.py:71); - a missing file, the cap and a symlink (tests/test_help_docs_route.py:80, tests/test_help_docs_route.py:88, tests/test_help_docs_route.py:94); - the shared anchor fixture and the section index (tests/test_help_docs_route.py:109, tests/test_help_docs_route.py:114, tests/test_help_docs_route.py:122). - frontend/src/test/markdown.test.tsx: structure, snake_case kept as text, hostile HTML inert, safe links only, no raw-HTML sink and no lookbehind in the source, the same anchor fixture, heading ids. - frontend/src/test/docs-panel.test.tsx: registration, rendering, switching and truncation, deep links to a doc and to a section, and the Settings cost link. | <a id="h168"></a>H168 | Shared UI primitives the pages lean on (markdown, destructive-action confirmation, schema-driven fields) | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The HUD now has one confirmation for every action that needs one, graded the way the approval queue grades risk, and agent text renders as Markdown wherever it is shown. Both halves of Hermes' confirm-and-render behaviour are covered. (1) One confirmation, graded by RiskTier. frontend/src/confirm.tsx declares RISK_TIER (frontend/src/confirm.tsx:19) with the names and values of agents/core/autonomy/policy.py:26 RiskTier, and tests/test_confirm_tiers.py:22 ties the two lists together. confirmStyle (frontend/src/confirm.tsx:23) gives READ_ONLY one click, REVERSIBLE two steps (the button arms, a second button confirms) and EXTERNAL and IRREVERSIBLE_OR_MONEY a phrase typed exactly (frontend/src/confirm.tsx:143); a tier it does not know takes the most. ConfirmAction (frontend/src/confirm.tsx:74) moves focus to the control that replaced the one pressed. Escape cancels, and a cancelled phrase is not remembered (frontend/src/confirm.tsx:95). Enter confirms only on a match. An action that returns a promise keeps the confirmation open, with both buttons held, until it lands; one that answers false keeps it armed to retry (fire, frontend/src/confirm.tsx:97). A page can open and close it itself (armed/onCancel, frontend/src/confirm.tsx:114, taking focus as it opens, :110) or keep a typed one always open (open: no trigger, no cancel). (2) Every hand-rolled site uses it. The kill switch's HALT ALL stays one click on purpose (READ_ONLY, frontend/src/gap.tsx:779), because an emergency stop must never wait for a second click; disengage is typed (DISENGAGE, frontend/src/gap.tsx:777, :778). Forget-me types FORGET (frontend/src/gap.tsx:3655) and the acquisition purge its phrase (frontend/src/gap.tsx:4590). Trust-ops rotation types the scope in an always-open confirmation that a refusal leaves filled and a rotation clears (frontend/src/panels/trust-ops.tsx:397). The marketplace uninstall types the folder it deletes, because the rmtree has no undo on this box; a changed folder is typed again, and a refusal keeps it armed (frontend/src/panels/marketplace-admin.tsx:313, confirmRemove :207). The webhook delete is two steps, opened by the row and held while that row's switch is pending (frontend/src/panels/webhooks.tsx:420, remove :285). The ESTOP pause is two steps (frontend/src/modes3.tsx:216). tests/test_confirm_tiers.py:26 checks that each of these files imports and uses ConfirmAction and has no window.confirm. (3) Markdown wherever agent text is shown. Chat bubbles, for agent and vision replies (frontend/src/cockpit.tsx:79, :96), and decision cards (frontend/src/shell.tsx:144) render through Bubble (frontend/src/cockpit.tsx:130). Bubble is memoised on the text, so a streamed token re-parses one bubble. It uses the shared React-only Markdown (frontend/src/markdown.tsx:101): lists, fences, tables and http(s) or same-origin links render, while raw HTML and any other link stay text. Canvas Markdown artifacts go through the same renderer (MarkdownBody, frontend/src/artifacts.tsx:78) and are bounded to MARKDOWN_LIMIT (4,000) code points when shown as well as when saved. A new breaks option (frontend/src/markdown.tsx:171) keeps a single newline as a line break for chat and artifacts, and documents keep paragraph joining. The owner's own turns are shown exactly as typed. Test-manual gap G22 (collapsed newlines, fences as prose) is closed. The kill-switch, marketplace, trust-ops and webhooks tests each gain cases, and the purge, marketplace and artifact tests follow the new steps. tests/test_confirm_tiers.py has 2. | <a id="h182"></a>H182 | Keep the machine awake through a turn, and be aware of power/battery state | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | Windows keep-awake state can be falsely reported active: _windows_set_state ignores SetThreadExecutionState return value (zero means failure), while KeepAwake._start unconditionally sets _win_held=True (agents/core/power.py:173-177, :280-283). SetThreadExecutionState is per calling thread, but acquire/release may execute on different turn/shutdown threads; release on another thread may not clear the original assertion. Use one owning thread or a host-supported process-level mechanism, validate return and release on that owner; cover failure and cross-thread behavior. Host-bound systemd/macOS/Windows and browser background behavior has no live acceptance in this review; H182 cannot be claimed fully equivalent from mocks alone. | <a id="h200"></a>H200 | Webhook subscription management surface | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Built in #1207 together with H153 (critic note 7: one spec, one panel), then fixed after an adversarial review. The v2 console has a Webhooks panel, WebhooksPanel (frontend/src/panels/webhooks.tsx:187), registered in the Interop group (frontend/src/console-routes.ts:50; the entry is asserted at frontend/src/test/webhooks-panel.test.tsx:71). Interop mode's WEBHOOKS list links to it by an accessible name (frontend/src/modes2.tsx:343). Every panel call sends the admin credential. The backend stays admin-only CRUD: list, create, switch and delete all carry Depends(admin_guard) (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:175, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:181, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:194, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:221). Interop mode is built from its four sources alone (hydrateInterop, frontend/src/api/live.ts:126). All four (A2A peers, MCP servers, widgets, webhooks) are admin-only routes and are fetched with the admin credential, and so is the webhooks settings category: while the receiver switch is off every webhook row reads "receiver off", and a switch it could not read is said under WEBHOOKS (frontend/src/api/live.ts:119, frontend/src/modes2.tsx:344). A section whose source failed is empty and says "not connected", and an empty real list says "none" (Section, frontend/src/modes2.tsx:304); the demo seed's rows are never shown under the LIVE chip. When no source answers nothing is overwritten, so the mode stays "Not connected" and DEMO keeps its seed. The review found that fetching only the webhooks list with the credential had marked the whole mode LIVE over seeded peers, MCP servers and widgets. The five accepted parts: (1) Create dialog: name, agent or workflow target, target id and an HMAC-signed toggle, and, since H153, a description, an event list, a prompt template, where the result goes and deliver only, posted to POST /api/webhooks. A refusal shows the hub's own reason, and an empty target is refused before any request. The create button is held while the call is pending and while a new hook's credentials are on screen, so a second hook cannot replace a secret before it was saved. (2) Created result, shown once (Reveal, frontend/src/panels/webhooks.tsx:145): the absolute trigger URL (triggerUrl, frontend/src/panels/webhooks.tsx:52: the page origin, the base path and the encoded id), and the token, or the signing secret for a signed hook, each with a copy button that says "copied" in a polite live region, plus the header the sender must send. Only the warning sentence is an alert, so a screen reader does not read the secret aloud. On a loopback origin it says a sender elsewhere needs an address it can reach. The secret lives only in the panel's state and is dropped on "I have saved it" (focus returns to the create form) or unmount. The list never carries it: WebhookStore masks the token to a 4-character hint (agents/core/webhooks.py:635). (3) Detail pane: clicking a hook expands its trigger URL, auth header (with the token hint), calls, last call and creation time, the deliveries its event list skipped and the last event skipped, and an editor for its event list and prompt template (H153). The row shows target_type:target, an HMAC or token badge, an on/off badge and an event-count badge. (4) Delete with confirmation: "delete…" arms an inline confirmation that explains the sender's credential dies with the hook and moves focus to "delete for good", which sends DELETE; "keep it" returns focus to "delete…". Each row button's accessible name includes the hook's name. A row's buttons are held while its call is pending, and every switch or delete reloads the list whatever the hub answered: a hook already deleted elsewhere is reported as gone ("ci was already deleted", "ci no longer exists") and its row disappears, with focus moved to the create form; a refused delete keeps the confirmation and says why. A per-hook on/off switch (PATCH, H153) is offered as the reversible alternative. (5) Governance: creating, switching and deleting a hook each write a SecurityEvent (_audit_webhook, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:143, called at agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:190, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:215 and agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:227). It names the hook id, the target as a JSON string (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:137), so a target cannot pass its own text off as the row's id or flags, and the signed and enabled flags as the store reads them, never the token or signing secret. A delete of an unknown id writes no webhook audit row (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:226) and answers 404 "webhook not found" (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:229). Since #1247 the admin guard's own auth telemetry (AUTH_SUCCESS and AUTH_FAILURE rows) lands in the same audit log, so the backend tests set those rows aside before counting webhook rows (_action_events, tests/test_h10_8_webhooks.py:266); an unknown switch adds none (tests/test_h10_8_webhooks.py:322) and a repeated delete adds none (tests/test_h10_8_webhooks.py:331). The receiver-disabled banner: when the receiver switch (H153, the setting webhooks.receiver_enabled) is off, the panel's receiver row says every delivery is refused until it is switched back on (frontend/src/panels/webhooks.tsx:352). The restart-gateway banner is not needed: a delivery sees the switch at most a second old (at once after a write in this process), and hook CRUD is served live; the receiver row says so. Not built, and not part of this row: a \`nerva webhooks\` CLI verb (critic note 7 places it with H409, as \`nerva webhooks inbound\|outbound\`) and a mobile screen. Tests: frontend/src/test/webhooks-panel.test.tsx has 56 cases. By describe block, 23 are this row's (the panel, the trigger URL and the review round; one of them, an open delete confirmation held while the same row switches, was added by H168), and 13 of those failed on the pre-review panel: the pending hold, the reload and the ghost row after a switch or delete, the one-POST create and the create held while a secret shows, the hook-named buttons, focus, the alert without the secret, the "copied" live region, the loopback hint and an unencodable id. The other 33 are H153's: the subscriptions, the receiver row and banner, the event list, the template, Deliver to, focus after a hook is gone, the push note and a proxy's 404. frontend/src/test/interop-live-honesty.test.tsx (14 cases, 5 of them H153's receiver read) drives the real useLiveModes against admin-guarded mock routes and renders InteropMode. The backend audit and DELETE guards are pinned in tests/test_h10_8_webhooks.py (tests/test_h10_8_webhooks.py:367, tests/test_h10_8_webhooks.py:377, tests/test_h10_8_webhooks.py:386); the file has 35 cases. Mutation: 60 mutants of the review round's guards (11 backend, 27 panel, 15 Interop, 7 v1) and 16 of the H153 panel additions were each run against their own suite, and all were caught; before the review round, 12 of the reviewer's 42 mutants had survived. | <a id="h209"></a>H209 | Keyboard shortcut customisation with a recorder and conflict detection | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The HUD's keyboard shortcuts live in one registry, as in Hermes. frontend/src/shortcuts.ts defines every action once (ACTIONS: id, category, label, default chord, scope): the ten mode keys, World (w), the Console (\`), Ambient (a), Cinema (m) and its three stages (o, n, b, scope cinema), focus the message box (/), the command palette (mod+k) and the Keyboard Shortcuts panel (mod+/). app.tsx (World's key included: WorldAwareApp passes onWorld and shares the live overrides through useShortcutOverrides) and the cinema overlay in shell.tsx dispatch through matchAction over bindings(overrides) instead of literal key maps, and every key hint (palette, top bar, World, Console, cinema stages) is read from the registry (chordOf, displayChord). A matched action takes the key from the browser (preventDefault); Shift+letter falls back to the bare letter unless it is bound itself. frontend/src/shortcuts-panel.tsx is the panel mod+/ opens: categories Navigation, View, Composer and Session with search (searchBindings); click a chord to record a new one ("Press a key…", Esc cancels, a lone modifier keeps listening; chordFromEvent, rebind); the palette and the panel take only a chord held with Ctrl/⌘ or Alt (acceptsChord), fire in an input only then (firesInInputs), and the palette always has a Keyboard shortcuts entry, so no rebinding can lock the panel out; Space is spelled "space", and a chord with no key is refused on record and on read; Esc closes the panel without closing what lies beneath; per-binding reset and Reset all (resetBinding); an "Also bound to X" note when two actions share a chord (conflictsFor). Overrides persist per viewer in localStorage under hud.shortcuts, validated on read (readOverrides, loadOverrides, saveOverrides), and survive storage that is off. Governance: defineActions refuses any action whose id, category or label names an approval, a decision, an accept, reject, deny or consent (FORBIDDEN_TARGET), so no chord can reach a Decision Inbox action. Tests: frontend/src/test/shortcuts.test.tsx (25), app-routing.test.tsx (+14: panel, rebinding, persistence, World, composer focus, the Space and Ctrl+Space lockout, the palette entry, the typing guard, preventDefault, World behind the panel, storage off, live hints, Esc beneath, Cinema), cinema.test.tsx (+1: rebound stage keys); voice-orb and briefing-wall expect the registry's stage titles; 38 mutants, 36 killed, 2 equivalent; the review round's 19 mutants, all killed (4 by tests added after a first pass). Re-review on 2026-10-02: The current app diff only passes the active agent into the image composer; keyboard shortcut behavior remains unchanged. Selected-main image collateral review (2026-10-02, 2fce39ca): The source diff adds optional bounded image provenance and a new reviewed conversation-image path. Existing no-media memory, text turns, previous composer route, and unrelated orchestrator/HUD behavior retain their prior branches; focused H277/H513/composer/session tests, 1,872 frontend tests, TypeScript and HUD build pass. Existing verdict preserved; this note does not claim live-provider acceptance or a new Hermes capability. HUD interruption collateral review (2026-10-05): reviewed exact preimage delta; separate text streams preserve existing pending, voice, vision and demo behavior. Complete frontend run: 1926 passed; typecheck/build passed. Other retained claims and limitations unchanged. Re-review on 2026-10-11 (PR #1249 restamp): the drift since the pins is cosmetic or unrelated. frontend/src/shortcuts-panel.tsx and frontend/src/shell.tsx changed only the colour token --ink-3 to --ink-2; frontend/src/app.tsx gained chat-session plumbing (session_id, /sessions/resume, selectAgent, turn outcome) in hunks that leave the H209 hotkey block untouched (useShortcutOverrides, matchAction over bindings, firesInInputs, preventDefault, onWorld, ShortcutsPanel). frontend/src/shortcuts.ts, frontend/src/world_app.tsx and both pinned tests are unchanged. Re-run: shortcuts.test.tsx 25, app-routing.test.tsx 23 (14 H209), cinema.test.tsx 8; 56 passed. | <a id="h222"></a>H222 | Always-visible microphone / wake-word state indicator | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | The frozen dedicated, independently visible wake-indicator window is absent. The current native surface is a tray icon whose title appears on macOS/Linux, while Windows has no tray title and only a tooltip visible on hover (desktop/src-tauri/src/indicator.rs:1-8, :27-37; main.rs:174-188). This does not supply an always-visible listening cue regardless of focused window on Windows. The native tray state depends on a HUD webview reading the stream and invoking desktop_listening; its cleanup sends off (frontend/src/listening.ts:74-80). Prove/fix visibility when windows are hidden/closed or background-suspended; native shell should own the cue independently if needed. | <a id="h242"></a>H242 | Workspace/project model, repo discovery, strict setup verification and diagnostics bundle | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva copies Hermes's setup.runtime_check into the owner's terminal. The hub resolves the route with the same llm_router.select_backend call a Jarvis turn makes (agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:296) and names its verdict: a local provider/model pair must be resident, and a cloud route (Gemini, Claude, or the cloud-compatible adapter set by llm.compatible_provider) counts only when the router handed back the very backend that route names (agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:145, agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:334-343), and (H380) unless its provider refuses the key that route would use; a provider that says nothing about the key reads unknown, and a 429 stays runnable. A real HybridRouter pins this for the local, cloud-flash, claude and both cloud-compatible paths (tests/test_first_run_command_center.py:536). \`nerva doctor\` (and \`python scripts/doctor.py\`) adds the advisory runtime_resolves row: one GET of /api/onboarding/command-center at the hub \`nerva status\` reads (scripts/doctor.py:283), run only after /readyz answered at that same address and otherwise reported as skip, never ok (scripts/doctor.py:432-436). It sends one credential, the user token when both are set and the admin token only to a hub on this machine, decided by its address (scripts/doctor.py:400-407, scripts/doctor.py:353), never prints it, and is ok only on a ready verdict that names the route, provider and model (scripts/doctor.py:483-487). A refused token, a reply cut off mid-body, a listener that does not speak HTTP, a bad URL or an odd resident list is a named warn, not a traceback (scripts/doctor.py:463-468; tests/test_doctor.py:433, tests/test_doctor.py:458). \`nerva status\` now labels the old any-model state 'resident' and adds a runnable: line from the same verdict (agents/cli/nerva.py:715, agents/cli/nerva.py:890; tests/test_nerva_cli.py:272); a timeout or reset on that read shows 'unknown — hub_unreachable' and the rest of the status still prints (agents/cli/nerva.py:723-728; tests/test_nerva_cli.py:336). A reply cut off mid-body or a garbled status line on any hub read is HubUnavailable, so \`nerva status\` reports an unreachable hub instead of a traceback (agents/cli/client.py:187, tests/test_nerva_cli.py:190). Both tools dial a wildcard bind (0.0.0.0, ::) over loopback (agents/cli/client.py:61). An end-to-end test drives both terminal checks against the real app (tests/test_first_run_command_center.py:593). Diagnostics stay local: the redacted bundle is served over one admin GET (agents/core/routers/support.py:21), and neither module imports a network client (tests/test_support_bundle.py:67), so the share_nous upload stays refused. The projects/workspaces model was skipped by decision. Known limits the row does not require: the verdict is for the route a short Jarvis chat takes; and a cloud route is judged by the router's own selection, not by a live call. Image-recovery collateral review (2026-10-02): changes to shared CLI/image-turn or HUD local-panel disclosure are outside this row's claimed symbols. Complete base evidence pins and unchanged text anchors verified; existing capability/status preserved. Claim-specific source inspection is recorded in the bounded image source review. Independent #1233 review (historical scope): Nerva copies Hermes's setup.runtime_check into the owner's terminal. The hub resolves the route with the same llm_router.select_backend call a Jarvis turn makes (agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:296) and names its verdict: a local provider/model pair must be resident, and a cloud route (Gemini, Claude, or the cloud-compatible adapter set by llm.compatible_provider) counts only when the router handed back the very backend that route names (agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:145, agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:334-343), and (H380) unless its provider refuses the key that route would use; a provider that says nothing about the key reads unknown, and a 429 stays runnable. A real HybridRouter pins this for the local, cloud-flash, claude and both cloud-compatible paths (tests/test_first_run_command_center.py:536). \`nerva doctor\` (and \`python scripts/doctor.py\`) adds the advisory runtime_resolves row: one GET of /api/onboarding/command-center at the hub \`nerva status\` reads (scripts/doctor.py:283), run only after /readyz answered at that same address and otherwise reported as skip, never ok (scripts/doctor.py:432-436). It sends one credential, the user token when both are set and the admin token only to a hub on this machine, decided by its address (scripts/doctor.py:400-407, scripts/doctor.py:353), never prints it, and is ok only on a ready verdict that names the route, provider and model (scripts/doctor.py:483-487). A refused token, a reply cut off mid-body, a listener that does not speak HTTP, a bad URL or an odd resident list is a named warn, not a traceback (scripts/doctor.py:463-468; tests/test_doctor.py:433, tests/test_doctor.py:458). \`nerva status\` now labels the old any-model state 'resident' and adds a runnable: line from the same verdict (agents/cli/nerva.py:715, agents/cli/nerva.py:890); a timeout or reset on that read shows 'unknown — hub_unreachable' and the rest of the status still prints (agents/cli/nerva.py:723-728). A reply cut off mid-body or a garbled status line on any hub read is HubUnavailable, so \`nerva status\` reports an unreachable hub instead of a traceback (agents/cli/client.py:187, tests/test_nerva_cli.py:190). Both tools dial a wildcard bind (0.0.0.0, ::) over loopback (agents/cli/client.py:61). An end-to-end test drives both terminal checks against the real app (tests/test_first_run_command_center.py:593). Diagnostics stay local: the redacted bundle is served over one admin GET (agents/core/routers/support.py:21), and neither module imports a network client (tests/test_support_bundle.py:67), so the share_nous upload stays refused. The projects/workspaces model was skipped by decision. Known limits the row does not require: the verdict is for the route a short Jarvis chat takes; and a cloud route is judged by the router's own selection, not by a live call. | <a id="h247"></a>H247 | Wake word arming per surface, push-to-talk capture, TTS, and liveness/config primitives | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva now knows which surface holds each microphone. A surface arms only if the owner allowed its kind; one device's microphone has one holder at a time; and arming, pausing, resuming and stopping carry the surface's identity and are audited. This is the adaptation's missing concept, on top of the voice primitives that already existed (wake word, push-to-talk and hands-free capture, TTS, STT, capabilities, and H222's listening indicator). The lease table is agents/core/voice/mic.py (MicArbiter, agents/core/voice/mic.py:93; one process-wide ARBITER, agents/core/voice/mic.py:232). (1) Surfaces and devices. A surface is kind:client: host:hub (the hub's own wake-word pipeline), hud:\<tab\> or mobile:\<device\> (ARMABLE, agents/core/voice/mic.py:39). A Wyoming satellite has its own microphone and is not armed here. A lease is for one device. host is the machine the hub runs on, and a browser that the route sees on loopback shares it. The route decides that from the address a trusted proxy vouches for (_mic_device, agents/core/routers/voice.py:527), so a LAN client cannot claim to be local. Otherwise a browser gets remote:hud:\<tab\>, and every phone gets mobile:\<device\>. (2) One holder per device. A second surface arming a held device is refused, naming the holder (MicRefused mic_busy, agents/core/voice/mic.py:49; HTTP 409). With take_over, it pauses the holder and tells it (on_change, agents/core/voice/mic.py:144). A pause frees the device (agents/core/voice/mic.py:199). A resume asks again, both for consent and for the device's holder (agents/core/voice/mic.py:212). A stop releases the lease (agents/core/voice/mic.py:221). (3) Consent. A kind arms only if the setting voice.mic_surfaces allows it (allowed_kinds, agents/core/voice/mic.py:59). The setting is a declared tags setting whose values are validated against host, hud and mobile (agents/core/settings_db.py:254, agents/core/settings_db.py:1153). The default is hud and mobile (DEFAULT_ALLOWED, agents/core/voice/mic.py:40): surfaces someone uses on purpose. The hub's always-on listener needs host added. So VoiceChannel.start (agents/core/channels/voice.py:29) opens the host microphone only with a lease. Before this, it started at boot with no check. It stops the pipeline when taken over and starts it again when given back (_on_lease, agents/core/channels/voice.py:48). Withdrawn consent ends a lease at its next renewal. (4) Leases lapse. A browser or phone lease lapses LEASE_SECONDS (45, agents/core/voice/mic.py:42) after its last renewal (_expire_locked, agents/core/voice/mic.py:108). The host pipeline's lease does not lapse. The table holds at most 32 leases. (5) Audit. Every arm, take-over, pause, stop and refusal is one audit row naming the surface and the device. A renewal is not. (6) Routes, all user-guarded. GET /api/voice/mic answers who holds each device, the paused leases and the allowed kinds (agents/core/routers/voice.py:548). POST /api/voice/mic/arm (agents/core/routers/voice.py:557) takes hud or mobile and answers 403 or 409. /pause, /resume and /stop act on a lease by name (agents/core/routers/voice.py:576, agents/core/routers/voice.py:587, agents/core/routers/voice.py:601). (7) The HUD. The voice loop arms its lease before getUserMedia (frontend/src/voice.ts:414; armMic, frontend/src/mic-lease.ts:47) and renews it every 20 s (frontend/src/voice.ts:434). It releases the lease on every end of the loop (frontend/src/voice.ts:135). If a renewal is refused, it stops and says who took the microphone. Only a hub refusal blocks: a hub that cannot answer does not. The loop offers take over (takeOver, frontend/src/voice.ts:454; the voice pill, frontend/src/cockpit.tsx:256). The voice settings list the holders, with pause, resume and stop (MicLeases, frontend/src/mic-leases.tsx:17). Tests: tests/test_h247_mic_lease.py has 27. They cover consent, kinds, client and device names, withdrawn consent, a conflict and a take-over, renewal, lapse, pause and resume, stop, the bound, status, the numbers, the setting and its reading, the voice channel's lease, loopback against LAN against a phone, the routes and their guard. frontend/src/test/mic-lease.test.tsx has 10: arm before the mic, a named holder and take-over, no consent, an unreachable hub, renewal and loss, a cancelled start, an unmount, a click handler, the pill and the settings list. 61 mutants: 60 caught, eleven of them after survivors got cases. One of those survivors exposed a device name that was too long for a 64-character client id; it is fixed. The last was a redundant check, removed. Not in this row: no install path installs a voice engine. The named STT/TTS command-provider change touched only the voice router's provider catalog, intake and selector fields; the mic lease routes and the consent check are unchanged, and both test files above pass. Re-review on 2026-10-02: InputBar now passes its active agent into the image hook; voice arming, capture and liveness controls remain unchanged. Collateral active-image review (2026-10-03, a30603e0): cockpit InputBar now treats an explicitly selected prior image like a fresh image for submit readiness. Wake-word, microphone, push-to-talk and voice-control branches are untouched; pinned citations remain unchanged. The complete frontend suite passes 1,881 cases; existing equivalent verdict preserved. Fresh-store owner-authority collateral review (2026-10-03, base ce34558179a7fc267555952f8a3856b40c3317f6): The fresh owner read and JSON-field rendering do not alter microphone arming, capture, liveness or voice control. This row’s cited settings, defaults, validation and claimed consumer behavior are unchanged; no new capability or live evidence is claimed. Every other pinned file holds. tests/test_h247_mic_lease.py 27 passed and frontend/src/test/mic-lease.test.tsx 10 passed in this session. | <a id="h259"></a>H259 | Reset to defaults, scoped and previewed, plus export/import of the whole configuration | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | A settings reset now shows what it will change, never touches a secret and can be undone, and the HUD marks every setting that differs from its default. Hermes' config-reset and defaults surface is covered, on top of H157's search, secret-free export, dry-run import and per-category reset. (1) Defaults. Every declared row that is not a secret carries its declared default (agents/core/settings_db.py:739, in _mark_overlay :731, used by GET /api/admin/settings and each category read). A secret carries none. The Settings panel marks a row whose value, or staged edit, differs from its default ("changed", with the default in its tooltip); a ↺ button stages the default and the usual save writes it through the validated PUT (frontend/src/gap.tsx:2856). (2) A preview before any reset. plan_reset (agents/core/settings_db.py:1493) computes what a reset of one category, or of every category, would write: each declared setting that is neither a secret nor a route-only row (settings_db.ROUTE_ONLY: the skill switches, the voice command providers, the security data-handling acknowledgments) and whose stored value is not its default. POST /api/admin/settings/{category}/reset and POST /api/admin/settings/reseed take {"dry_run": true} and answer the changes as current → default without writing anything (agents/core/routers/admin.py:391, :430; _dry_run_asked, :379; only a JSON true, in a body of at most 4 KB). The changes use describe_changes, so a credential-shaped value shows as (hidden). Arming the reset in the HUD asks for that preview and lists it, and counts the settings kept (secrets and route-only rows, all labelled secrets) and names the settings the posture still forces (ResetPreview, frontend/src/panels/settings-tools.tsx:64; ResetCategory, :92). (3) The reseed no longer deletes the store. It used to run DELETE FROM settings, wiping every setting, secrets included, with no preview and no way back. It is now the per-category reset over every category: secrets and route-only rows are kept (and named), rows nothing declares are left, it takes a JSON request from this origin only (415/403 otherwise, as H157's writes), and it is audited with the settings that moved (agents/core/routers/admin.py:430). The v1 admin page sends it as JSON (agents/web/static/admin.js:1520). The HUD gets "reset every category…" with the same preview (ResetAll, frontend/src/panels/settings-tools.tsx:115). (4) Undo. reset_settings (agents/core/settings_db.py:1533) writes the defaults and records what it replaced in settings_resets (:120) in the same transaction, keeping the last 20 (RESETS_KEPT, :1516). POST /api/admin/settings/undo (agents/core/routers/admin.py:461; undo_last_reset, agents/core/settings_db.py:1590) puts back the latest reset not yet undone. A setting changed since that reset, one its declaration no longer accepts, or one no longer declared is left as it is and named in skipped. The undo is audited, and the listeners hear every value a reset or an undo writes, so the webhook receiver switch follows it. GET /api/admin/settings/resets (agents/core/routers/admin.py:91; list_resets, agents/core/settings_db.py:1577) lists the resets, newest first, naming settings but never their values. The HUD shows "last reset: \<scope\> · N settings · \<time\>" with "undo…" (UndoReset, frontend/src/panels/settings-tools.tsx:135). Every reset and undo in the HUD is a two-step ConfirmAction at the REVERSIBLE tier (H168). Adapted from the row's ask: the reseed does not go through the approval queue's irreversible tier, because it is no longer irreversible. It keeps secrets, previews, and can be undone. Routing it through the queue would need a new kernel action kind for it, and none is declared (agents/core/kernel/registry.py's one settings kind is H613's settings.voice_command). Tests: tests/test_settings_defaults_undo.py has 24: defaults on rows and none for secrets; the plan (what moves, never a secret); a recorded reset and its undo; nothing moved means nothing recorded; the listeners; a setting changed since, no longer valid or no longer declared left as it is; latest first; the list keeps 20 and names no values; everything reset with secrets and undeclared rows kept; and the routes (admin-only, dry run, a hidden credential, JSON only with a 4 KB bound, the reseed's preview, audit and undo, nothing to undo 404, and /resets not taken for a category). frontend/src/test/settings-reset-undo.test.tsx has 7. The H157 reset test, the H153b reseed test (a JSON body) and the settings row-shape test (default) follow. Of 54 mutants, all 54 are caught, ten of them after survivors got cases. The full backend run first stopped at the expected stale Hermes generated report, with no behavior failure before that point. | <a id="h285"></a>H285 | Choose which skills, plugins and MCP servers load at startup | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The owner declares which skills, plugins and MCP servers load at boot, and a plugin toggle survives a restart. Six declared settings rows (agents/core/settings_db.py:447-452), category \`loadset\`, hold comma lists: skills_disabled/skills_only, plugins_disabled/plugins_only, mcp_disabled/mcp_only, editable from the settings page and \`nerva config set\` (which says a loadset.\* key or a plugin switch applies at the next start). A disabled name does not load; a non-empty only-list loads only what it names; the disabled list wins (permits, agents/core/load_set.py:96). A list is read at call time (declared, agents/core/load_set.py:74), trimmed, de-duplicated and bounded to 256 names of at most 128 characters without control characters (parse_names, agents/core/load_set.py:43), and an unreadable store narrows nothing. For plugins the settings page's existing per-plugin \`plugins.\<id\>\` switches, which nothing used to read, count too: an explicit off is off (agents/core/load_set.py:84). SkillLoader.discover reads the lists once per pass (agents/core/skills/loader.py:654-655) and a skill named by its registry name or its folder is not registered at all, before any quarantine row (agents/core/skills/loader.py:733); a registration from an earlier pass is taken out when a later pass denies it, the per-pass snapshot is cleared in a finally, and in safe mode (the owner folder unread) no declared name is reported as unknown. PermissionGate switches off the plugins the lists name when it is built (apply_load_set, agents/core/plugin_gate.py:704), and PluginManager.build drops them from orch.plugins before binding oracle_bridge and Argus (_drop_switched_off, agents/core/plugin_manager.py:198), so the probes, the dashboard and WorldView never reach a switched-off plugin. Each gate holds its own copies of the built-in manifests (agents/core/plugin_gate.py:698), so switching one off never reaches the module table or another gate, and the readers of a plugin's enabled state ask the live gate: CloudImageRuntime is wired with orch.permission_gate (_available, agents/core/cloud_image_runtime.py:542) and the capability board reads the gate's manifests (_plugin_records, agents/core/observability/capability_registry.py:127). The plugin toggle (toggle_plugin, agents/core/routers/plugins.py:99) keeps the choice in one place, the plugin's plugins.\<id\> settings switch when it has one and otherwise plugins_disabled; switched on, it leaves plugins_disabled and joins a declared plugins_only (persist_plugin, agents/core/load_set.py:140). It reads the lists fail-closed (read_setting), so an unreadable store is not overwritten, and answers \`persisted\` (false when the store refused or could not be read, the live toggle standing). It writes a SETTINGS_CHANGE audit row naming the keys, and a re-enable that cannot be audited is switched back off and answers 503 audit_failed. The MCP load registers only the permitted saved servers (agents/web.py:1967-1974); a switched-off one is held (_MCP_HELD, agents/web.py:1944) and written back unchanged by every save, so the saved configuration never loses it, and adding a server of that name or removing it answers 409 \`switched_off\` naming the list (_held_refusal, agents/core/routers/mcp.py:159). The lists only narrow: nothing is installed, approved, registered or written to the approval store, and a declared name that matches nothing installed is reported as unknown (finish, agents/core/load_set.py:121). The status rows carry it: /skills (agents/core/routers/skills.py:76), /plugins (agents/core/routers/plugins.py:88) and /api/admin/mcp (agents/core/routers/mcp.py:132) return \`load_set\` with the declared lists, what was switched off and what is unknown (status, agents/core/load_set.py:132). Safe mode (H275) applies first; the load set narrows further. tests/test_h285_load_set.py (49 cases, plus one in tests/test_cloud_image.py) covers the parsing and its bounds, the six declared rows, the permit table, an unreadable store, a disabled skill and an only-list with their status rows and unknown names, a skill named by its folder, a named-but-absent skill installing or approving nothing (asserted on a private approval store), the skills route, MCP servers switched off and still saved, an MCP only-list, the add and remove refusals for a held server, adding and removing other servers keeping the held one, the MCP list route, the gate's disabled and only lists, the settings-page switch and that only an explicit off counts, that the lists never enable or register, the toggle kept across a restart both ways, a plugin without a settings switch, enabling under an only-list, a toggle the store refuses, and the plugins route. 33 mutants over the parser, the permit table, the persistence, the three loaders, the refusals and the status rows: all caught (two after their cases were added: an explicit off, a refused write). Test manual: ENV-169, ENV-170. Adapted from Hermes: the lists live in the settings store Nerva already persists and edits rather than a YAML file, the plugin toggle writes them, and an MCP server switched off is kept in the saved list rather than dropped. MCP transport audit collateral review (2026-10-09): the route adds only fixed-field authentication telemetry at the existing RPC gate. This row's admin/listing, validation or transport-auth behavior and remaining limitations are unchanged; its previously current mcp.py pin and explicit coordinates were reviewed. | <a id="h298"></a>H298 | Keep a huge tool result out of context without losing it | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Pinned/owner/MCP/declaration/default threshold precedence, window-scaled result/turn spill budgets, retained full-result files, bounded previews and actual file_read paging are wired through the tool loop. Paging recipes require current registration, job/turn offer and exact path reachability; read-back stays tainted. Shared byte/line/line-length limits have real code/terminal consumers. Four focused modules pass. file_read is intentionally pinned against recursive spill, so repeated bounded pages can exceed the nominal spill allowance; request-context checks remain separate. Whole-result capture, capped 5 MB streamed capture, retention expiry and bounded lossy I/O fallback are distinguished; this does not promise permanent retention after storage failure.
