| <a id="h314"></a>H314 | Let the model decide what to remember | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The model now writes its own long-term memory during a turn, through a tool that is atomic, guarded, recorded and undoable. \`memory\` (register_memory_tool, agents/core/memory_tool.py:413; registered ungated at agents/core/memory_tool.py:519 and wired from the autonomy coordinator's H314 block, whose \`_living_memory\` getter and \`register_memory_tool(\` call tests/test_h314_memory_tool.py:544-546 reads back from the coordinator's source) takes \`operations\`, each an add, a replace of the one fact \`old_text\` matches, or a remove of it, against target \`memory\` (the LivingMemory \`core\` ring of agent notes) or \`user\` (the \`user_core\` profile ring) (TARGETS, agents/core/memory_tool.py:61); a call with no operations reads both. The rings are reached only when cognition memory is on: the coordinator's \`_living_memory\` getter hands the tool the LivingMemory only when \`cog.sub_enabled("memory_enabled")\` holds (pinned by content at tests/test_h314_memory_tool.py:544-546), and a getter that yields nothing — memory off, safe mode, or a getter that fails (agents/core/memory_tool.py:429-441) — is refused \`memory_disabled\` (agents/core/memory_tool.py:469-471). Both rings come from one LivingMemory, so the enabled set is both or none: when memory is off the tool's offer says so through the H296 schema hook (OFF_DESCRIPTION, agents/core/memory_tool.py:459-460) and every call is refused \`memory_disabled\`, rather than the target enum being narrowed to an empty list. Every operation is checked on a working copy before anything is written (plan, agents/core/memory_tool.py:156): one line of at most 300 characters, the core block's own cut (MAX_FACT_CHARS, agents/core/memory_tool.py:63), with no control, format, private-use, surrogate or other invisible character (the quarantine.strip_format_chars set: variation selectors, Hangul fillers, U+034F, U+2800) (_clean_text, agents/core/memory_tool.py:112), scanned with the normalised injection scanner (_scan, agents/core/memory_tool.py:135), an old_text that matches exactly one fact, compared against the whitespace-normalised fact as the prompt shows it (_match, agents/core/memory_tool.py:144), no duplicate, and a full ring refusing an add instead of silently dropping its oldest fact; one refusal changes nothing. The rings are then written by a new compare-and-set (CoreMemory.compare_and_set, agents/core/cognition/memory.py:314) under the lock a forget clears them with, so a forget or another write that ran in between wins and nothing forgotten is written back; a second ring that fails puts the first back (_commit, agents/core/memory_tool.py:320). A compare-and-set that lost is refused \`memory_changed_meanwhile\`; a ring that cannot be saved is refused \`memory_write_failed\`. Only a clean owner turn at the operator surface writes: the resolver offers the tool to operator/owner alone, whatever llm.guest_tools says (OWNER_OPERATOR_TOOLS, agents/core/tool_profiles.py:102 and :243), the handler refuses any other posture (agents/core/memory_tool.py:449) and a turn whose origin is untrusted, an inbound message, web content or a flagged recall (agents/core/memory_tool.py:452), so an injected instruction cannot make itself permanent. Every write is committed and then recorded as an intent-log \`memory.write\` entry carrying the actor, the undo ref, the targets and per operation its target and action — never the text nor a hash of it, since an unsalted hash of a short fact is guessed back and the intent log survives a forget (agents/core/memory_tool.py:497). A record that cannot be made puts the rings back and records the put-back as \`memory.write_reverted\` (undo: \`memory.undo_reverted\`) with the same ref, naming exactly the rings it restored, because the intent log keeps a record whose save failed and writes it with its next one; a put-back that cannot be made (a ring moved again) is logged as an error and not recorded as one (_revert, agents/core/memory_tool.py:344; _record_put_back, agents/core/memory_tool.py:307). With no intent log nothing is written (_record, agents/core/memory_tool.py:299). The rings' previous contents are kept under the ref in a bounded undo store beside the rings (UndoStore, agents/core/memory_tool.py:228), which a forget deletes with them and which POST /api/admin/forget empties together with the live rings (clear_live_memory, agents/core/data_purge.py:429), so an undo during a forget cannot write the facts back. undo (agents/core/memory_tool.py:363) restores a write while the rings still hold what it wrote, by the same compare-and-set, recorded as \`memory.undo\`. The owner reads the rings and the writes undo would still restore — only those whose rings hold what they wrote (UndoStore.recent, agents/core/memory_tool.py:263) — at GET /api/memory/core (agents/core/routers/memory_kg.py:872) and undoes one at POST /api/memory/core/undo, admin-only (agents/core/routers/memory_kg.py:886). The Console's Long-Term Memory panel shows both rings and the recent writes with an undo each, and says when an undo is refused (LongTermMemoryPanel, frontend/src/panels/long-term-memory.tsx). A write that changes a ring leaves a \`memory_updated\` trail row with the changed targets and the actions, never text; a call that changes nothing leaves none (_record_event, agents/core/memory_tool.py:394). The prompt's core block stays frozen per session, so a write reaches the system prompt at the next session and the tool answers with both rings as they now are. tests/test_h314_memory_tool.py (72 cases) covers the plan and every refusal (malformed, injected, invisible-character, ambiguous, duplicate or over-full operations), the compare-and-set and a ring that cannot be saved, an owner write of both rings with its record and its undo, a read, an add already saved, the five postures and untrusted origins, a failing getter, memory switched off and its offer, a broken memory module, an unknown argument, no intent log or one without a record method, a record that fails and its recorded put-back, an undo whose put-back also fails claiming none, a forget between the read and the write, a forget that empties the undo store, a second ring that fails, a full ring, undo after the ring moved, undo of a replace and a remove, undo without memory, an undo that cannot be recorded, the undo store's bound and its listing of only restorable writes, the trail row, the profile offer and the routes; frontend/src/test/long-term-memory-panel.test.tsx pins the panel. 47 mutants: 33 at the build (32 caught, four after their cases were added: a raw over-long padded fact, an audit sink without record, an empty operation list, the undo listing's limit; one equivalent, rewriting a ring the call did not change) and 14 in the review round (13 caught, one after its case was added: an undo whose put-back also fails; one equivalent, widening _moved to rings it is never given). Test manual: MEM-204, MEM-205. Adapted from Hermes: its memory tool's add/replace/remove on the two injected stores, with the writes restricted to the owner's clean turns, committed by compare-and-set, recorded in Nerva's intent log without their text and undoable by the owner. | <a id="h318"></a>H318 | List, read and author the agent's own skills | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Built in #1207, with H340 in the same change (critic note 21: one skill_view), and fixed after seven adversarial reviews. The first (review-H318: two majors, six minors, nine nits) found an unkeyed signature counted as a vouch and approved changes that never landed. The second (review-H318b: two majors, seven minors, seven nits) found that an approved change disabled the skill it improved (a bundled skill unbundled, a signed or approved one losing its standing) and that the owner still approved without seeing the change. The third (review-H318c: one records major, eight minors, seven nits) found a write between the standing read and the renewal could ride into the vouch, a renewal failure reported as applied, a keyed signature overwritten while its key was missing, a publish that silently dropped a linked file, and cards that could be lost or left pending. The fourth (review-H318d: one major, five minors, six nits) found that text-mode SKILL.md writes made every vouched apply fail on Windows, a partial renewal left a new signature over the old text, publish still followed a link planted after its check, and cards beyond the route's page were called inert. The fifth (review-H318e: two minors, seven nits) found the old SKILL.sig read after the write with no rollback behind it, text-mode test fixtures on the Windows lane, a signature that could not be put back reported as the old text staying, a truncated write, the review superseding by the record's origin, a /v1 card approvable without its diff, and publish describing another read of SKILL.md. The sixth (review-H318f: one minor, four nits) found the whole-file write staging its temporary file inside the skill, where one a crash left would void the signature. The seventh (review-H318g: two minors, five nits) found a standing that could not be read for a moment read as unsigned, and the crash-safety claim wider than the code. All are fixed or stated below; two nits are stated by decision below. Hermes gives the agent skills_list, skill_view (a skill's SKILL.md or one of its linked files) and skill_manage (create, patch and delete). Nerva now offers all three powers under its governance: the two reads as ungated read-only ToolRPC tools, and authoring as a proposal the owner approves. The three tools are in agents/core/skills/tools.py; register_skill_tools (agents/core/skills/tools.py:192) registers them on the orchestrator's ToolRPC server in AutonomyCoordinator, next to the todo and memory tools; tests/test_h318_skill_tools.py:400 drives all three through the live hub's own server. The trust gate: - SkillLoader.catalog_gate (agents/core/skills/loader.py:1365) is the prompt catalog's gate, factored out and now shared by the catalog, skills_list and skill_view. - It says why a skill is not shown: sandboxed (quarantined included), untrusted (a signature that does not verify here), or declared for other agents. prompt_catalog applies it unchanged, and its suite passes as before. skills_list {query?, offset?, limit?}: - It returns name, a one-line description and the command names for every skill the gate passes for the calling agent (agents/core/skills/tools.py:267). - A row whose text is injection-flagged is left out, as the catalog leaves it out. The query matches name or description, case-insensitively. Pages hold at most 50 rows, with total and next_offset. - Unknown fields and malformed paging are refused by name. skill_view {name, file?} (agents/core/skills/tools.py:307): - It returns the SKILL.md body (frontmatter removed; the heading dialect whole) with its template variables rendered (H340), plus the list of the skill's other files, at most 3 KiB of names as the answer carries them (escapes counted) with files_more counting the rest; a file or body is served up to 64 KiB on disk, as always, and refused only when its escapes pass 128 KiB (MAX_ENCODED_BYTES), and the description is cut to 2 KiB escaped (agents/core/skills/tools.py:87-96). With file, it returns one of those files, listed or not. - It serves the bytes the trust checks ran on at load, kept as Skill.view_files (agents/core/skills/loader.py:334): each file up to 64 KiB as bytes, at most 1 MiB of a skill, any other file only as its size (listed, refused by name). An edit on disk after the signature check is not what the model reads, and a skill's assets are not held for the process's life. Signing a skill loads it again, so the view follows the signed bytes. - A path can only name a file of that snapshot. It must be relative, with no '..', leading '/' or backslash, and it is normalised (agents/core/skills/tools.py:182). The snapshot refuses links when it is taken. - Only text is served (a NUL or bad UTF-8 is refused, a BOM dropped), at most 64 KiB (MAX_FILE_BYTES, counted in bytes) after the template variables render and 128 KiB once escaped. The tool declares the escaped ceiling, the file list's and the description's bounds and 4 KiB of envelope as its own result budget. - An unknown skill and one this agent is not shown answer alike (skill_unknown). - The answer is tainted, and carries a warning sentence saying why next to the content, in two cases (agents/core/skills/tools.py:250): - the body or the description is injection-flagged; - the skill comes from outside the product and the owner has not vouched for it. - Skill.owner_vouched (agents/core/skills/loader.py:791) is true for a bundled skill, or for an outside one with a keyed signature or the owner's approval of these exact bytes. That is the same rule that decides whether its code may load in-process. - An unkeyed SKILL.sig is a sha256 anyone can compute. It verifies as integrity-only, but it is no vouch, so a self-signed import stays data (review-H318 M-1, SEC-B2). - The loop fences a tainted answer as DATA and raises the turn's taint. - A body view that succeeds for the owner's own turn also declares the skill's required_env names to the code-environment registry for that actor and session, with a validator the registry re-runs before the names are honoured (the same loader, skill, vouch, snapshot and gate), and a tainted view revokes them; a declaration that fails never fails the view (agents/core/skills/tools.py:392, agents/core/code_env.py:161, agents/core/code_env.py:222). skill_propose (agents/core/skills/tools.py:423): - {name, content} proposes a new SKILL.md for an existing skill that this agent is shown. It becomes a pending SkillProposalStore proposal with origin agent:\<agent\>, plus one skill.patch_proposal approval card bound to it in the ledger (queue_card, agents/core/skills/proposals.py:213). The background review and /refine queue theirs the same way. - A bundled skill is refused (skill_propose_bundled): it is product source, and an edit would unbundle it and, with its code, stop it loading. A change that would rename the skill is refused (skill_propose_rename). - What the owner reviews comes from the ledger, never from the card. GET /api/skills/proposals (admin, agents/core/routers/skills.py:766) serves the pending proposals, a page of 20 with more counting the rest, each with its whole diff against the live SKILL.md, whether the skill drifted since, and flags for a rename or a bundled skill (describe, agents/core/skills/proposals.py:272); a proposal from before cards were bound, or whose card is gone from the queue, gets its card there, every pending proposal's, not only the page's, and the answer names every pending proposal's card (cards). The /v1 console's panel holds every skill-change card it has no diff for (one beyond the page, or one no proposal names) with Approve disabled, offering Reject (review-H318e n-4), re-reads the action list once the proposals answer, and says the change is loading, or that it could not be read, and holds Approve until it knows; a change the hub will refuse (a bundled skill, a rename) offers reject only in both consoles. The HUD's Decision Inbox shows these under SKILL CHANGES, whole, with approve and reject on the proposal's own card (frontend/src/panels/skill-changes.tsx), and the /v1 console's action panel shows the same diff under a skill-change card. - Only the proposal's own card decides it (agents/core/skills/curator.py:217): a card anyone with a user token queued naming a proposal id changes nothing, and the decide route says so. - Re-sending the same text, from any origin, makes no second proposal and no second card; a card that failed to queue is queued by the retry. A newer proposal from the same origin (the caller's own, even when the text re-sent was another origin's) for the same skill supersedes its older pending one (marked superseded, its card withdrawn), whether the tool, the background review or /refine proposed it (each passes its own origin, review-H318e n-2); binding a card withdraws the unbound cards naming the proposal, and a proposal superseded while its card was queued gets that card withdrawn, and a process takes at most 10 proposals a day, new skills included (skill_propose_limit). - The owner's decision applies it at once: POST /api/actions/{id}/decide on the proposal's card runs SkillCurator.apply_decisions (agents/core/routers/actions.py:147, agents/core/skills/curator.py:154), one pass at a time, the nightly pass included, not gated by the learning loop's flag or the night window. The apply is hash-checked against drift and backed up under the skill's directory name with a unique suffix. - The applied bytes keep the skill's standing (agents/core/skills/loader.py:1297, agents/core/skills/loader.py:1328): judged on one snapshot of the tree read before the write; after the write, the tree must be that snapshot with only SKILL.md replaced by the approved text, and the SKILL.sig that verified or the owner's approval is renewed over exactly those checked bytes, never a later read. SKILL.md is read and written as bytes, never in text mode, so the line ends on disk are the ones checked, on Windows too, and written whole: staged beside the skill's folder where it can be (a file a crash leaves inside the folder counts as a member and fails the signature closed; where beside it cannot be, another filesystem or a root the hub cannot write, the folder itself is used: review-H318g n-1), created with the old file's mode, flushed to disk and renamed over it, so a write cut short leaves the old text (review-H318e n-3, review-H318f m-1). The old SKILL.sig is read with SKILL.md, before anything is written (review-H318e m-1); a read of it that fails changes nothing and is tried again at the next decision (unreadable_signature, review-H318f n-1), and so does a standing that could not be read for a moment, which no longer reads as unsigned (review-H318g m-1); a signature that stays unreadable is warned about once per proposal. The apply is not crash-proof: a crash between the rename and the renewal leaves the skill sandboxed (untrusted, never trusted) and the next decision reports it as drifted, and on Windows a SKILL.md another process holds open cannot be replaced, so the change is retried as write_failed. Otherwise, or if the renewal fails, the old SKILL.md and the old SKILL.sig are put back byte for byte, where the old signature and approval still hold, the outcome says changed_during_apply or standing_not_renewed (rollback_failed, with the backup, if the old text could not be put back; signature_not_restored if the old text is back but its SKILL.sig could not be, which leaves the skill untrusted until it is signed again; when the old text could not be put back, its signature is not either, so what was renewed is not undone over the text that stays), and the proposal is marked stale with that reason rather than retried every pass. A standing that cannot be read (a SKILL.sig that is not UTF-8) reads as unsigned, and one proposal that fails never stops the pass. A keyed SKILL.sig whose key is not configured is not overwritten: the change waits (signing_key_missing). A shipped skill is known by its load or by where it lives (a shipped name directly under the product's skills tree), so one whose bytes drifted is still refused, by the tool, the background review and the apply. A skill nothing vouched for gains no vouch, a signature that no longer verifies is not renewed, and code changed on disk since the approval does not ride the patch. The answer reports each outcome with the state the skill is left in (shown, sandboxed, untrusted, hidden) or why it was not applied (bundled_skill, renames_skill, drifted_since_proposal, write_failed). A write that failed stays approved and is retried at the next decision, leaving no backup behind for the attempt. Publishing builds the package from one snapshot of the source, the bytes it read, and its registry row describes that snapshot's SKILL.md (review-H318e n-5): a skill whose folder is a link, a member that is a link or not a regular file, or a file that changed while read is refused (422, skill_source_refused: a link, a special file, or a file that could not be read stably), and a link planted after the check is never followed. - {description, steps} asks for a new skill, which SkillLoader.generate_skill writes into CDX-8 quarantine (PENDING_REVIEW, never executed until the owner approves). - Nothing writes a live SKILL.md: the live file is byte-identical after a proposal. - Only an owner's turn that has read nothing untrusted may propose. A guest, a household member, a job, an inbound channel or a turn that read a web page (the action origin a read raises) is refused by name, and so is a turn whose origin cannot be read. Injection-flagged text is refused, as are mixed or malformed arguments. Differences from Hermes, by decision: - Authoring is a proposal, never a write. Deleting a skill and adding supporting files stay owner actions. - skill_propose is offered wherever an ungated tool is, and refuses every turn that is not the owner's clean one; it is not withheld from the offer (a nit both reviews left). - The daily limit is per process and follows local time: a restart resets it. - Publishing a skill to the marketplace signs a staged copy, never the owner's tree, so sharing a skill is not vouching for it (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:391). - All three tools are ungated, so an inbound guest is offered them only when the owner names them in llm.guest_tools (critic note 22). tests/_snapshots/tool_profiles.json is regenerated. Tests: tests/test_h318_skill_tools.py (43 cases), tests/test_h318c_skill_review.py (22, the second review, and one H350 case), tests/test_h318d_skill_review.py (22, the third review), tests/test_h318e_skill_review.py (21, the fourth review), tests/test_h318f_skill_review.py (10, the fifth review), tests/test_h318g_skill_review.py (5, the sixth review), tests/test_h318h_skill_review.py (7, the seventh review), vitest files for the HUD's JSON setting field (3 in frontend/src/test/settings-json-field.test.tsx, beside one H329 case and two Telegram owner-list cases) and the Decision Inbox's skill changes (frontend/src/test/skill-changes.test.tsx, 12), and the /v1 action panel's skill-change cards, a refused change's included (tests/frontend/tools.test.js), with the live orchestrator's pinned tool list in tests/test_agent_runtime_v2.py. They cover: - the list against the catalog, query, paging and refusals, and hidden skills; - view of a bundled body, unknown and hidden skills, linked files and every path refusal, binary and oversized files, and load-time bytes; - taint and warning for an unsigned outsider and for injected text; - the template variables; - patch and new proposals, the posture and turn refusals, and the malformed cases; - the review's cases: a self-signed outsider stays tainted; a keyed signature and a bundled skill are vouched; signing reloads the view; the rendered bound; large assets kept as sizes and the per-skill total; NUL, BOM, a one-lined description and the declared budget; an injected description; the list's page end and command cap; a hidden skill cannot be proposed to; an unreadable origin is untrusted; supersede and the daily limit; the card retry; an approval through the real route applies the change; - registration, the inbound-guest default and the live hub answering all three; - the second review's cases: a generated-and-approved skill with code, a keyed signature, an integrity-only one, an unvouched outsider, a tampered signature and code changed on disk after the approval, each through the real apply; a bundled skill refused at propose and at apply; a rename; the route's whole diff, flags, drift and old-proposal card; a card that is not the proposal's own; supersede per origin and per skill; one card per proposal; a new skill counting toward the limit; the byte-counted render bound; a file of exactly 64 KiB; 2,000 files within the budget; the backup name; a keyed publish; two overlapping decisions. - the third review's cases: code changed during the apply, and after the check but before the renewal, for an approval and a signature; a failed renewal; a keyed signature with its key missing and back; the answer-byte bounds; a lost card, legacy cards, a proposal superseded mid-bind and the route's rebind; a linked publish; a bundled skill and a gone skill in describe; pending only; the background review's bundled and shipped skip and its supersede; the nightly lock; the route's page; a rename behind a blank line; a drifted shipped skill; the HUD's reject-only, count and outcome texts. Mutation: 41 mutants of the new guards (the template-variable ones shared with H340). 33 were caught at once, and of the six survivors: - four were caught after cases were added or corrected (one test was vacuous: two fixtures pointed at different skill roots); - one was malformed and, rewritten, was caught; - one survived because its guard was redundant with the proposal store's own no-change check, and the guard was removed. Two mutants added afterwards were caught. The review's own 55 mutants left 15 survivors; its seven real ones (a proposal to a hidden skill, an origin error failing open, a NUL served, the next_offset edge, the description, the declared budget, a BOM kept) are now caught, and the rest change only a refusal reason or are equivalent. The first round's 25 mutants were all caught, two after a case was added. The second review's 44 mutants left six real survivors (the daily limit for new skills, supersede across origins and across skills, the diff cut, the render bound in characters, the 64 KiB boundary); each has a case now, and the diff is no longer cut. That round's 31 mutants (26 Python, 5 HUD) were all caught, one after the tampered-signature case was added. The third review's 60 mutants left four real survivors (a bundled flag in describe, a gone skill, the pending-only filter, the review's bundled skip); each has a case now. The third round's 28 mutants (25 Python, 3 HUD) were all caught, four after cases were added. The fourth review's 43 mutants left five real survivors (a SKILL.md swapped after the write, an integrity-only signature with a key configured, a re-bind touching other proposals' cards, the publish route's 422, a body's escapes); each has a case now. That round's 17 mutants were all caught. The fifth review's 35 mutants left four real survivors (a description cut to nothing, the escaped ceiling's boundary, two HUD reason texts) and an untested /v1 panel; each has a case now. That round's 16 mutants were all caught. The sixth review's 25 mutants left three real survivors (a signature restored over text that stayed, /v1's reject-only for a refused change, a weak one); the first two have cases now. That round's 7 mutants were all caught. The seventh review's 17 mutants left three real survivors (a removed flush, a removed cleanup, /v1's reject-only for a rename); the write no longer buffers, and the other two have cases. This round's 8 mutants were all caught, one after it was rebuilt (it had set the flag back itself). Every pinned suite passed again: 43, 23, 22, 21, 10, 5 and 7 backend cases, 6 and 12 vitest cases, and the 40 /v1 console cases. | <a id="h327"></a>H327 | SKILL.md frontmatter contract (agentskills.io-compatible) | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The SKILL.md frontmatter contract Hermes parses (agent/skill_utils.parse_frontmatter and tools/skills_tool_plugin.py) now lives in one shared module, agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py, used by both the loader and the importer. Parsing. split_frontmatter (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:47): - drops a leading UTF-8 BOM before the fence check (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:56); - takes the closing fence at column 0 only, as Hermes' \`\\n---\\s\*\\n\` does, so an indented \`---\` inside a block scalar is content (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:63); - falls back to key: value lines for a block that is not valid YAML (_naive_key_values, agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:83), instead of demoting the file to the heading dialect. The fallback is stricter than Hermes' line split: - only column-0 keys count, so a nested \`name:\` cannot replace the skill's own; - a value wrapped in matching quotes loses them; - a fallback that finds neither a name nor a description counts as no frontmatter (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:77), so a heading-dialect file framed by fences keeps its heading parse. Identity decisions pass lenient=False (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:72), so a name only the fallback can find never decides one: the Hermes pin gate (agents/core/skills/importer.py:271) and the migration and rescan slugs (agents/core/skills/importer.py:1192, agents/core/skills/importer.py:1376). SkillLoader._parse_manifest decodes with utf-8-sig on both the snapshot and the path read (agents/core/skills/loader.py:857, agents/core/skills/loader.py:859). A manifest the normaliser cannot read registers under the directory name instead of failing (agents/core/skills/loader.py:874). The manifest: - The name is capped at 64 characters and stripped after the cut (agents/core/skills/loader.py:893; the caps are at agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:28-29). - The description is capped at 1024 characters with an ellipsis, quotes kept (cap_description, agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:351). When none is declared, it is the first non-heading body line, as Hermes' listing reads it (agents/core/skills/loader.py:895-896). - A list-valued author or license is joined (agents/core/skills/loader.py:898). - agents and requires read a YAML list, \`\[a, b\]\` or \`a, b\` as plain strings (agents/core/skills/loader.py:885-886). - Command entries are reduced to JSON types (agents/core/skills/loader.py:970). contract_fields (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:359), merged into every frontmatter manifest (agents/core/skills/loader.py:905), keeps the keys Hermes recognises: - homepage and compatibility; - platforms, read as Hermes matches them (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:204): a list, or one string as one name. Each name maps through PLATFORM_MAP and matches the OS whose sys.platform starts with it, so darwin and win count and osx and win64 do not. An unmatched name is kept, so a gate refuses it everywhere, as Hermes hides it; - environments and requires_apps, where a string is one item (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:224, agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:234); - triggers and dependencies; - tags and related_skills, where a declared metadata.hermes value wins over the top-level key (skill_view's \`hermes_meta.get(k) or frontmatter.get(k)\`); - setup, prerequisites and required_credential_files; - required_environment_variables (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:256), which matches Hermes' _get_required_environment_variables: it merges the declared list, setup.collect_secrets and the legacy prerequisites.env_vars, first entry wins, names are validated, and \`optional\` is read for truth; - metadata.hermes.{tags, category, upstream_skill, supersedes, session_platforms, requires_toolsets, requires_tools, fallback_for_toolsets, fallback_for_tools, config} (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:340). A config entry needs a key and a description. Skill exposes platforms, environments, requires_apps, required_env (names only; no value is read) and hermes_meta, and to_dict carries them (agents/core/skills/loader.py:478, agents/core/skills/loader.py:526, agents/core/skills/loader.py:534, agents/core/skills/loader.py:538, agents/core/skills/loader.py:544). Imported frontmatter is third-party text: - json_safe (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:128) bounds depth, width, string length and total nodes, with a 2,048-node budget per value (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:134). A 2 KB YAML alias bomb that would expand to 64^6 nodes parses in about 0.02 s. - Dates become ISO strings, and NaN, infinities and integers past 64 bits become null or "". A hex integer past Python's str() limit no longer raises (_scalar_text, agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:112), so the security-posture route serialises Skill.to_dict with allow_nan=False. - discover() skips a skill whose load raises and keeps the rest, while the signing misconfiguration still stops discovery (agents/core/skills/loader.py:664-669). - A second skill taking an occupied registry name is logged: a warning within one tree (e.g. two names sharing their first 64 characters), info for a user skill replacing a bundled one (agents/core/skills/loader.py:681). Deliberate differences from Hermes: - A closing fence at end of file with no trailing newline is accepted, as Nerva always did. - The registry is last-writer-wins with that log line; Hermes' listing keeps the first. Tests: tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py has 51 cases, including the alias bomb (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:286), a hex integer in seven fields (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:306), NaN and huge numbers through to_dict (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:315), one broken skill among others (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:328), the fallback rules (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:354), Hermes platform matching (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:406), the strict identity parse (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:434) and the snapshot BOM path (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:482). A mutation pass over 25 mutants (first-wins, precedence, the depth, width and node caps, both utf-8-sig decodes, the fence, the fallback rules, optional, the strict gate, NaN and big ints, the discover guard, the collision log, the platform string, author join, quotes, the body-line fallback, name strip, command sanitising, requires_apps) killed all of them. 454 tests across 28 skill, import, marketplace, migration and posture files pass. Gating on these fields is not this row: H328 (visibility) and the setup rows consume them. | <a id="h344"></a>H344 | Reading skills from directories shared with other AI tools | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Import-once stays the only way a Hermes / OpenClaw / Claude Code skill enters Nerva's skills tree (no live-scanned external_dirs tier; SkillLoader.discover() is unchanged), and a re-run of \`python scripts/nerva_import.py\` now notices upstream movement instead of reporting a blanket skipped/exists. Each imported skill is re-detected against the absolute source_path and sha256 recorded in its manifest.json (importer.py:955, importer.py:1000): unchanged; changed, with the old and new digests plus a bounded line diff shown by --json; moved to another folder of the same install and followed (importer.py:934); removed; renamed; unreadable; or source_untrusted when a record names a file outside the install, which is then never read (importer.py:1290). The default run is a dry run. \`--apply --update\` re-imports only changed skills that this source imported (importer.py:1065): the current copy is backed up under data/imports/skill_backups, PENDING_REVIEW is written before the new bytes land, SKILL.sig and the legacy approval marker are cleared, and the owner's approval is revoked. A linked control file, an unusable backup dir or a refused save fails closed, and the write is bound to the probed digest. Every import and re-import crosses skill.install through the real Action Kernel at the shipped CLI (nerva_import.py:577, bound at nerva_import.py:1044 and as the ImportRunner default at nerva_import.py:682), behind SKILL_IMPORT_CONTRACT (nerva_import.py:145). The persisted kill switch is re-read on every decision, so an engaged halt refuses an import or re-import before a byte moves. The external origin keeps a policy GRANT at QUEUE, and only GRANT or QUEUE writes (nerva_import.py:764), so every write lands quarantined. The human summary names a local edit that --update replaces (with its backup path), a failed revoke, and moved, untrusted and refused rows (nerva_import.py:938). Tests in test_nerva_import.py: 44 passed and 1 live-gated skip under python 3.11 and 3.12. They include test_cli_skill_writes_cross_the_real_kernel_and_honour_the_kill_switch, test_runner_default_authorizer_is_the_kernel_for_skills_and_queue_for_memory, test_source_moved_within_the_install_is_followed_not_reported_removed, test_apply_update_reimports_changed_skill_into_quarantine_and_revokes_approval, test_rescan_never_follows_a_record_outside_the_install and test_reimport_refuses_a_linked_control_file_and_never_writes_through_it. Two known limits sit outside the accepted requirement. (1) The CLI binds no durable audit sink, so each kernel decision is recorded only on its report row (kernel/kernel_reason) and in the in-process kernel metrics. (2) A record written before this change with a cwd-relative source_path (relative --home) is not rewritten by an unchanged re-run. From another directory it reads skipped/exists plus source_untrusted until a changed source is re-imported with --apply --update, which records the absolute path. | <a id="h368"></a>H368 | Refuse to speak the wrong protocol to a lookalike host | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | A hostname-parsed protocol table guards every model backend at the wire. host_mandated_protocol (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:112) takes the hostname from hostname_of (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:85). hostname_of parses strings with httpx.URL, the parser the wire dials with (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:103). A fullwidth, ideographic or halfwidth-ideographic dot spelling of a vendor host is therefore refused at HybridRouter.detect() as well as at the wire. urlsplit remains the fallback for text httpx rejects, and httpx cannot dial that text either. The host is lower-cased with the trailing dot dropped, then matched exactly or as whole labels (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:62-69): api.anthropic.com -\> anthropic_messages; api.openai.com and one regional label -\> openai; bedrock-runtime\[-fips\].\<region\>.amazonaws.com\[.cn\] -\> bedrock_converse. Lookalike suffixes, path spoofs and userinfo spoofs mandate nothing. Each backend's ledger name declares its protocol (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:49). The declared llm_async_client callers on this head are anthropic, gemini (gemini_cache included), openai-responses, xai, openrouter, lm-studio (agents/core/llm/base.py:537), ollama (agents/core/llm/base.py:849), vlm and the approval judge's openai-compatible; the undeclared client names that also exist on this head (deepinfra in deepinfra.py and vision_deepinfra.py, nous and nous-metadata, models-dev, and vlm's provider_id variants) speak no declared protocol, so protocol_refusal refuses them on every mandated vendor host (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:141-146) and they can only reach unmandated hosts. The synchronous embedding clients (llm_sync_client and llm_sync_request_hook, agents/core/llm/egress.py:113-142: ollama, lm-studio) run the same protocol_refusal check. protocol_refusal (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:131) also refuses an undeclared backend on a mandated host. The check lives in the egress-ledger request hook that every backend's llm_async_client carries (agents/core/llm/egress.py:76-79), so it runs on every redirect hop. A mismatch records an allowed=False ledger row with the reason and raises before the transport is touched, so neither the body nor the auth header leaves. HybridRouter.detect refuses a compatible base URL on a host that mandates another protocol (agents/core/llm/hybrid_router.py:394); the refusal is logged and the backend is not built. HostProtocolRefused subclasses Exception, neither OSError nor httpx.HTTPError (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:73), so no connection-failure handler reads a refusal as 'backend down'. OllamaBackend.resolve_context_window logs a warning and returns unknown on a refusal (agents/core/llm/base.py:871), instead of raising out of compaction and route planning. Adaptation from Hermes: Nerva has no per-session api_mode that can go stale, so the mandate refuses a mismatched backend rather than re-picking a protocol. Nerva's OpenAI family admits both OpenAI dialects (chat completions and Responses) at api.openai.com. Base URLs remain env-only until the named-custom-endpoints row exists, and the wire guard covers any base URL unchanged. Coverage is tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py (43 tests): - vendor hosts, including the 5 IDNA-dot spellings (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:61); - 15 lookalike, path, userinfo and other hosts (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:82); - an OpenAI-protocol backend aimed at api.anthropic.com sends zero requests and leaves two blocked ledger rows (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:119); - the client raises before dispatch (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:141); - matching and unmandated hosts still flow (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:151); - a redirect onto a vendor host is refused per hop (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:164); - router refusals, including both IDNA spellings (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:204, tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:213, tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:234); - the default and custom endpoints are still built (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:222); - the refusal is neither an OSError nor an httpx.HTTPError (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:247); - the Ollama probe degrades and leaves a blocked ledger row (tests/test_host_mandated_protocol.py:254). All wire tests use the real client, with httpx.MockTransport injected through llm_async_client's public transport kwarg. Parameter-repair source review (2026-10-02): _post_chat still uses the existing declared LM Studio client and physical request hooks for every send. Model-unloaded recovery and one scoped temperature repair cannot change the host or wire protocol. Host-protocol and real auxiliary send regressions pass; no capability promotion. Independent #1233 review (historical scope): A hostname-parsed protocol table guards every model backend at the wire. host_mandated_protocol (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:112) takes the hostname from hostname_of (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:85). hostname_of parses strings with httpx.URL, the parser the wire dials with (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:103). A fullwidth, ideographic or halfwidth-ideographic dot spelling of a vendor host is therefore refused at HybridRouter.detect() as well as at the wire. urlsplit remains the fallback for text httpx rejects, and httpx cannot dial that text either. The host is lower-cased with the trailing dot dropped, then matched exactly or as whole labels (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:62-69): api.anthropic.com -\> anthropic_messages; api.openai.com and one regional label -\> openai; bedrock-runtime\[-fips\].\<region\>.amazonaws.com\[.cn\] -\> bedrock_converse. Lookalike suffixes, path spoofs and userinfo spoofs mandate nothing. Each backend's ledger name declares its protocol (agents/core/llm/host_protocol.py:49). Model-unloaded recovery and one scoped temperature repair cannot change the host or wire protocol. Host-protocol and real auxiliary send regressions pass; no capability promotion. Re-review on 2026-10-02: The local auxiliary temperature repair permission is now bound to the invoking asyncio task, preserving protocol selection and refusing inherited child-task authority. Collateral review (2026-10-02, e741f6bc): the shared source diff is confined to a backend-owned cache for typed temperature rejection on selected non-streaming local auxiliary calls. Existing cloud, tool-turn, stream, provider-selection and unrelated consumer branches are unchanged; the focused H277/H513/LM Studio union passes. This does not expand this row’s claimed capability or close its remaining gaps. Egress collateral review (2026-10-03, 08e29d4a): the owned async recorder receives an inert marker that lets the private SOUL draft prove it remains the final request hook. Nonstreaming producers, ordinary streams, cloud/Ollama adapters and unrelated task/kernel paths retain their existing branches. The row-specific cited tests are included in the 3433-case affected-row regression, which passes. Existing verdict preserved without new feature or live-provider credit. This preserves the existing verdict and limits from semantic review; no new completion or live acceptance credit. It is not a \`BACKEND_PROTOCOLS\` declaration, so the old exhaustive caller inventory is no longer true. \`protocol_refusal\` still refuses unknown backends on mandated vendor hosts, while \`deepinfra.py:36-49\` accepts only the canonical DeepInfra HTTPS base and \`deepinfra.py:79-105\` checks exact physical URL/auth/body. Auxiliary wording extends only local structured field classifiers, not host selection or egress. The existing protocol verdict is preserved without claiming general custom-endpoint or live coverage. Details are in the batch-04 runtime, surface and history collateral reviews. Existing verdicts and remaining limits are preserved; already-stale pins remain untouched. See the batch-05 integration and root collateral reports. | <a id="h373"></a>H373 | See how much provider quota is left before hitting the wall | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva now reads each provider's quota off every cloud response and shares a 429 with every process. agents/core/llm/quota.py holds the rules. llm_async_client, the one constructor every cloud backend's HTTP client goes through (Anthropic, Gemini, OpenRouter, xAI, the Responses dialect, VLM), now hangs two hooks (agents/core/llm/egress.py:99-100). response_hook (agents/core/llm/quota.py:338) reads the provider's own quota headers with parse_headers (agents/core/llm/quota.py:146): OpenAI-style x-ratelimit-{limit,remaining,reset}-{requests,tokens}; Anthropic's anthropic-ratelimit-{requests,tokens,input-tokens,output-tokens}-{limit,remaining,reset}; and retry-after as seconds or an HTTP date read as UTC. It understands resets given as durations ("6m0s", "20ms") or RFC 3339 times, and it stores the latest numbers per backend, key and model. On a 429 it blocks that backend, key and model (request_model, agents/core/llm/quota.py:78: Gemini's path, else the JSON body's model), or the whole key when the request names no model, until the provider's retry-after, else 30 s, never over 900 s (QuotaStore.block, agents/core/llm/quota.py:207). request_hook (agents/core/llm/quota.py:317) runs before the egress recorder on every request. It refuses a request whose model or whole key is held with ProviderRateLimited before it is sent, so a refused request never reaches the provider or the egress ledger. Another key in the pool is not held: on a refusal the Anthropic backend (_post_messages, and the stream, which retries a refused open on the next key) and the Gemini backend (generate, tool turn, stream) fail over to the next key as on a 429, and ProviderRateLimited carries retry_in, so AuthProfilePool.report_failure(key, cooldown=retry_in) keeps the held key cool for its whole hold (agents/core/llm/anthropic.py:98, agents/core/llm/gemini.py:204, agents/core/llm/auth_rotation.py:159). The H380 provider probe reports a held key as rate_limited, sends nothing, and caches that verdict no longer than the hold and at most 30 s (agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py:191). Retry-after and reset values must be finite ('inf', 'nan' and overflowing numbers are unreadable), a negative retry-after is unreadable so the 30 s default applies, and a reset time without a zone is read as UTC. The store drops its pre-model tables once. A local host is never tracked or held, even when the same backend is held in the cloud. The state lives in one SQLite file under the data folder (QuotaStore, agents/core/llm/quota.py:173), so the hub, the CLI, the scheduler and any worker see the same numbers and the same holds. A key is known only by a 12-hex fingerprint of its hash, and every failure is swallowed, so a quota reader never breaks a request. usage (agents/core/llm/quota.py:361) gives each backend's requests and tokens left, the fraction left (clamped, none for a missing or non-positive limit), time to reset and any hold. It is served by GET /api/llm/quota (agents/core/routers/models_llm.py:439, admin, 503 quota_unavailable when unreadable or when a row cannot be serialised; each row carries its model, which /usage and the panel name), the HUD Provider Quota panel (frontend/src/panels/provider-quota.tsx, Admin group: bars, numbers, reset times, a hold tag), and /usage (agents/core/commands.py:225, admin tier, rendered by render). The router is not fed a forecast; Hermes' own guard is likewise a refusal before the request, which is what this does. tests/conftest.py gives each test its own quota store, so a mocked 429 never holds a later test (tests/conftest.py:187). tests/test_h373_provider_quota.py (78 cases) covers durations, retry-after (seconds, dates, no zone under another host timezone), OpenAI and Anthropic headers, unreadable values, key fingerprints, and the store: latest numbers, clamping, a negative limit, blocks with retry-after, the default and cap, a hold before numbers, two processes, damaged data and the default path. It also covers the hooks on a real llm_async_client (recording, no headers, a 429 hold refused before dialling and absent from the egress ledger, another key, the default hold, other errors, a local model, a local request of a held backend, a failing store), the local-host test, the hook order, rendering, the admin route and the admin /usage command. The review round added the fail-over on a refusal for both backends and their streams, the cooldown floor, per-model holds, finite and signed header values, zone-less resets, the route's serialisation, and the probe's held key (tests/test_h380_provider_probe.py). frontend/src/test/provider-quota-panel.test.tsx has 3 cases. There are 50 mutants, all killed; the review round's 18 mutants, all killed (5 by tests added after a first pass). | <a id="h380"></a>H380 | Prove a configured cloud provider actually works | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | A configured cloud key is checked with its provider before a turn finds out. agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py asks each cloud provider once, with the key the hub would use (configured_key, agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py:87: for Anthropic and Gemini, the only providers with an AuthProfilePool (POOLED), the first of the \`\<VAR\>S\` pool split on commas and whitespace as the router splits it, else \`\<VAR\>\`; every other provider reads \`\<VAR\>\` only). The command center asks with the key the route's backend holds: the pool's current key for Claude and Gemini, the compatible backend's own api_key otherwise. It sends one authenticated GET (request_for, agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py:105): Anthropic /v1/models with x-api-key and anthropic-version, Gemini /v1beta/models with x-goog-api-key, OpenRouter \`\<base\>/key\` (its model list is public; the key record answers 401 to a key it does not know), and \`\<base\>/models\` with a bearer for the other OpenAI-shaped providers, honouring a configured base URL. The GET goes through llm_async_client, so it is in the egress ledger as \`llm:\<provider\>\`, and H368's host-protocol mandate refuses a mismatch before it leaves; the xAI and OpenAI Responses probes use trust_env=False, as their backends do (NO_ENV_TRUST, agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py:67). Nothing is generated. The verdict (probe, agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py:223) is one of: \`ok\` (any 2xx, with the listed model count); \`no_listing\` (404/405: nothing at that URL, so the key is not proven); \`auth_failed\` (401, or a 400 whose body names a bad key: Gemini API_KEY_INVALID/API_KEY_EXPIRED, xAI "Incorrect API key"; the body is read inside the probe and never returned); \`forbidden\` (403); \`rate_limited\` (429, or a key held by the shared 429 guard of H373, with nothing sent); \`error\` (any other status, which is named, or an unexpected failure); \`unreachable\` (any transport error); \`refused\` (the host mandate); \`not_configured\` (no key, nothing sent); or \`not_cloud\` (a local provider). Only \`ok\` proves the key (WORKING, agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py:61). The key and the response body are never in a result or a log line. A result is cached per provider, key fingerprint and probed URL, so a changed key or base URL is probed afresh: for TTL_SECONDS (300 s), and a transient verdict (rate_limited, error, unreachable) for TRANSIENT_TTL_SECONDS (30 s) or, for a guard-held key, no longer than its hold (agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py:53-56). Concurrent callers, forced ones included, share one in-flight request (_fresh, agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py:205). A forced re-check inside MIN_INTERVAL_SECONDS (30 s) answers the cached verdict marked \`throttled\`. last (agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py:263) reads the cache without a request. probe_all (agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py:301) asks every cloud provider concurrently, except one whose key variable belongs to a base URL on another host (_key_for_elsewhere, agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py:287): an OPENAI_API_KEY beside an OPENAI_BASE_URL on a gateway is not sent to api.openai.com unless llm.compatible_provider is openai-responses. POST /api/admin/llm/providers/probe (admin; admin_llm_providers_probe, agents/core/routers/admin.py:959) checks one provider or all, with \`force\`; an unknown provider is a 404. The Console's Provider Check panel (Admin; frontend/src/panels/provider-check.tsx) probes nothing until asked. It shows each verdict (\`key rejected\` with the HTTP status beside it; \`key not verified (nothing at this URL)\` in amber for no_listing), the model count and a named HTTP status, offers a forced re-check per provider, and says when a re-check was throttled. The command-center model block asks the provider for a selected cloud route (_cloud_probe, agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:224; agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:339) and waits at most CLOUD_PROBE_WAIT_SECONDS (4 s), after which the verdict reads \`unreachable\` and the probe finishes in the background. The route is ready (\`cloud_selected\`) on \`ok\`; \`rate_limited\` keeps it runnable (\`cloud_rate_limited\`); \`no_listing\`, \`error\` and \`unreachable\` make it unknown (ready=None, \`cloud_no_listing\` / \`cloud_error\` / \`cloud_unreachable\`); \`auth_failed\`, \`forbidden\`, \`refused\` and \`not_configured\` make it not runnable (CLOUD_PROBE_REASONS, agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:204). The block carries \`cloud_probe\` (provider, verdict, status code, time, cached; agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:370), and its hint names the provider, what it said and the HTTP status (_model_hint, agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:391). \`nerva doctor\`'s runtime_resolves and \`nerva status\`'s runnable line read that block, so a wrong or revoked key now fails them with its reason instead of passing on "a key is set". tests/test_h380_provider_probe.py (30 cases) covers the request per provider with the key only in a header, the key resolution, one read and its model count across list shapes, nine statuses, three transport failures and an unexpected one, the protocol refusal recorded as not sent, the egress row, no request without a key or for a local provider, no key or body in results or logs, the cache per key with expiry, the throttled and then allowed forced re-check, the cache-only read, probe_all, the admin route (admin-only, one, all, force, 404) and the command center's key choice. tests/test_h380b_provider_probe_review.py (35 cases) covers the review round: OpenRouter asked about its key, not its public list; a gateway key kept away from api.openai.com and OpenAI asked when the key is its own; a 400 that names a bad key; a missing listing that proves nothing; the key read as the router reads it and the compatible backend's own key; a guard-held key rate_limited for the hold; concurrent callers sharing one request; the bounded wait; readiness per verdict and the short cache of a transient one; trust_env matching the backends; the hint; and the cache slot per URL. tests/test_first_run_command_center.py covers every \`cloud_\*\` reason and \`cloud_probe\` in the model block. The panel has 3 vitest cases. The row's 38 mutants were all killed. The review round's 36 mutants: 29 caught at once, six after six cases were added to tests/test_h380b_provider_probe_review.py (a finished request is not joined again, a caller that stops waiting leaves the verdict cached, a request in flight on another event loop is not awaited, the cache-only read forgets a transient verdict, reset forgets a request in flight, the hint for an unknown provider), and one equivalent (\`ready is True\` and truthiness agree on True/False/None). HUD contrast collateral review (2026-10-09): foreground-only changes preserve this capability and its authority; changed source/test pins reviewed by gpt-6-sol/high, with full HUD and focused follow-up tests. Scope and browser evidence: docs/hud-muted-text-contrast-20261009.md. [d1](../agents/core/llm/provider_probe.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/admin.py) [d3](../agents/core/routers/onboarding.py) |

| <a id="h410"></a>H410 | Universal secret redaction on every log record | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Every log record, from every process, now goes through two filters: H495's shape scanner and a catalogue of credential-bearing names plus the Romanian identifiers. agents/core/log_catalogue.py (outside security/, additive) is a scanner over the names that make a value a credential whatever it looks like: query parameters (QUERY_PARAMS, agents/core/log_catalogue.py:54: access_token, refresh_token, id_token, token, auth, api_key, client_secret, secret, password, signature/sig, the AWS presign parameters, session, jwt), masked at any length up to the next separator; body, form and structured-log keys (SECRET_KEYS, agents/core/log_catalogue.py:62), quoted in JSON or a Python repr, assigned with a quoted value, or unquoted when the value is six characters or more; the Authorization header after any scheme (Bearer, Basic, Token, Digest, ApiKey, HMAC…), or without one when the value is twelve characters or more and carries a digit, so \`Authorization: denied\` stays; the credential headers X-Api-Key, X-Auth-Token, X-Access-Token, X-Goog-Api-Key, X-User-Token, X-Admin-Token, Api-Key and X-Webhook-Token (SECRET_HEADERS, agents/core/log_catalogue.py:68); and the whole value of Cookie and Set-Cookie. It also runs PIIScanner's own CNP and IBAN patterns (agents/core/security/scanner.py:287-288) with their checksum validators, and an IBAN written in spaced groups of four; the other PII patterns stay out (an e-mail or phone number in a log is often the point of the line; card and SSN shapes carry no checksum). A bare \`key\` parameter (settings routes use it for a setting's name) and a bare \`token:\` key (token counts) are not in the catalogue. The rules are in _rules (agents/core/log_catalogue.py:91); a value already masked is never re-masked, so a JWT the first filter named keeps its name. CatalogueScanner (agents/core/log_catalogue.py:126) offers redact, scan and the _compiled rows H495's prefilter is built from, and CatalogueRedactionFilter (agents/core/log_catalogue.py:161) runs it through an unchanged SecretRedactionFilter (agents/core/security/log_redaction.py:230): the arguments uvicorn's AccessFormatter unpacks are kept, a secret split between the format string and an argument masks the argument whole and is named after the finding that holds, tracebacks and stack info are masked, a failing scanner drops the record's content to \[redaction-unavailable\], and the one JARVIS_LOG_REDACTION switch, snapshotted at import, turns both filters off. It wraps rather than subclasses, so H495's installer, which skips a handler already carrying a SecretRedactionFilter, never mistakes it for its own. install_catalogue_redaction_everywhere (agents/core/log_catalogue.py:220) covers the root handlers and every logger's own (uvicorn's detached ones included), idempotently, and counts and names a handler that refuses the filter. core.log's _install_redaction (agents/core/log.py:69) installs it right after the H495 filter, so setup_logging (agents/core/log.py:108) gives every handler both, in that order; install_redaction (agents/core/log.py:102) is the same for a process that configures its own logging. The two entry points that called a bare basicConfig and wrote every record unredacted now call it: the headless coordinator (configure_logging, scripts/coordinator.py:145) and the MCP stdio bridge (configure_logging, scripts/nerva_mcp_stdio.py:149, still stderr only, since stdout is its protocol channel). Neither takes setup_logging's rotating file handler, which a second process would rotate under the hub (critic note 32). The H145 log reader runs the catalogue on every line it serves (agents/core/log_tail.py:167), for lines written before it or by a process it did not cover. The test isolation fixture strips the catalogue filter with the H495 one (tests/conftest.py:271). The H495 module's docstring (agents/core/security/, a protected path) still names the coordinator and the MCP bridge as uncovered and says no PII pass runs; it describes that module alone. tests/test_h410_log_catalogue.py (52 cases) covers the five probes the audit found unmasked and every other name in the catalogue, ordinary text that must stay (a failed authorization, a setting key, an exit code, a token count, a 13-digit order number, an IBAN-shaped string that fails its checksum, a short value), the exact line kept around a mask, CNP and IBAN compact and spaced, an access-log record keeping its five arguments, a traceback, a JWT keeping the first filter's name, a straddling IBAN, the switch, a failing scanner, both filters on every handler once and in order, a refusing handler counted and named, both entry points with a fresh root (the bridge writing nothing to stdout), and the log reader. 31 mutants: 30 killed (4 by cases added after a first pass) and 1 equivalent that removed a redundant \`(?:proxy-)?\` prefix (\`\\b\` holds after the hyphen). Test manual: SEC-216. [d1](../agents/core/log_catalogue.py) [d2](../agents/core/log.py) [d3](../agents/core/log_tail.py) |

| <a id="h428"></a>H428 | Pre-turn recall with a triviality gate, a hard timeout, and next-turn warm-up | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Orchestrator._recall_block (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3595) gates and bounds the pre-turn recall, skips it while an earlier one is stuck, and warms the next turn, the way Hermes' memory prefetch does. It is reached from the streaming and parallel turn paths. The 2026-09-24 review rounds fixed what had made the bound hollow, built the warm-up half, which had been missing, and made a delete reach every cached result and the store itself. (1) Triviality gate. is_trivial_prompt (agents/core/memory/recall_gate.py:67) returns true for: - empty input; - a command-shaped slash text (agents/core/memory/recall_gate.py:49). Nerva dispatches commands before recall, so a path such as "/etc/hosts changed, why?" is a question; - a bare acknowledgement or greeting followed only by punctuation or emoji: the Hermes list plus Romanian (_ACKS, agents/core/memory/recall_gate.py:31), with internal commas read as spaces; - punctuation alone ("?", "…?", "❓", "$?"), and an emoji-only reaction unless it comes with a question mark ("🦷?" is a question); only the "other symbol" category counts as an emoji (agents/core/memory/recall_gate.py:80). Text is compared after NFC normalisation with format characters removed (agents/core/memory/recall_gate.py:61). k8s, yolo, "ok, and what about X?" and any non-command text over 40 characters are never trivial. _recall_block returns '' before any embedding (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3616). (2) Job pins. A job-model-pinned turn never recalls, and the check runs before any handoff or warm context (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3620). (3) Hard timeout. _bounded_recall_hits (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3514) awaits _recall_hits (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3318) for at most memory.recall_timeout_s. _recall_hits covers the optional H433 rewrite, the query embedding and the fused vector ⊕ graph search. - The setting is seeded at 8 s like Hermes' _EXTERNAL_PREFETCH_TIMEOUT_S (agents/core/settings_db.py:323). - _recall_timeout_s (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3309) reads a non-number, bool, NaN, infinity or non-positive value as 8 and clamps the rest to 0.1–60 s. The settings write path refuses a non-finite number for every number setting (agents/core/settings_db.py:1046). - On expiry it logs a WARNING (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3551). - The living-memory rerank runs after the bound, off the event loop, only on the hits a turn freshly recalled (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3645); warm hits served again are not reranked again. The bound holds for the turn, not only for the await: - MemoryManager keeps a store lock for the vector store and the knowledge graph, separate from the conversation lock (agents/core/memory/manager.py:79-80; store_embedding at agents/core/memory/manager.py:639, hybrid_search at agents/core/memory/manager.py:667; a single vector remove at agents/core/routers/memory_kg.py:327). A search stuck on Qdrant or Neo4j does not block saving the reply or any other session's turn. - With MEMORY_EMBED_TURNS on, turn embeddings are written in the background, one at a time and oldest first, as Hermes' sync_all never writes a provider inline (_queue_turn_embedding, agents/core/memory/manager.py:500). Shutdown gives them 5 s to land and then drops what is still queued (agents/core/orchestrator.py:1561, agents/core/orchestrator.py:1565). The CLI REPL reads each line on a daemon thread, so the event loop and the queue keep running while the owner types, and it gives the queue 30 s to land on exit (_prompt, agents/run.py:57; agents/run.py:54). (4) Skip while stuck. A timed-out recall cannot be killed, because its embedding and search run on worker threads. It is kept as a straggler, and so is a recall whose turn was cancelled; none is overwritten or cancelled under its threads (_adopt_recall_straggler, agents/core/orchestrator.py:3559). While any straggler runs, later recalls are skipped with an INFO line (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3531), as Hermes skips a provider whose previous prefetch is still alive. (5) Next-turn warm-up. The hits a turn recalled, an empty result included, become its session's warm context (_keep_warm_recall, agents/core/orchestrator.py:3488, kept at agents/core/orchestrator.py:3646, capped at 256 sessions). When the next turn's own recall is unavailable (timed out, or skipped behind a stuck one), that warm context stands in (_warm_recall, agents/core/orchestrator.py:3502; agents/core/orchestrator.py:3639), with an INFO line naming it. It is never served: - to another session; - to a caller outside any turn (the autonomy worker, the nightly reflection, /api/context/compress), which neither keeps, serves nor consumes turn state, and whose stragglers leave no handoff (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3476). Work delegated inside a turn shares that turn's session; - after a purge or a single-memory delete; - past 10 minutes; - to a trivial or pinned turn; - when the new turn's recall ran and found nothing: that empty answer replaces it. This is Hermes' queue_prefetch_all adapted to one retrieval memory: - Hermes computes a background result from the finished turn's message and injects it next turn beside the new base context (Honcho's pending dialectic). - Nerva's recall of that same message already ran at the turn's start, so it reuses those hits instead of a second round-trip. - It uses them only when the new turn's own, fresher recall is unavailable, which is when a warmed result saves the turn. (6) Same-question handoff, a Nerva extension. Hermes throws a timed-out result away. Here a straggler that finishes cleanly leaves its hits for one handoff, kept per session: the session's next recall takes them, serving them only for a retry of the same question within 10 minutes of completion (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3536). Another session never receives them. (7) Deletes. Every recall reads the recall generation when it starts (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3630); its result is served, handed off or kept warm only while that generation is still current. A result a delete overtook is discarded when the search returns (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3553), before the rerank can reinforce it, or after the rerank (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3647), and nothing is kept under a stale generation. - A purge ("forget me", data_purge.clear_live_memory) drops queued turn embeddings and bumps the generation, clearing every handoff and warm context (_forget_in_flight, agents/core/data_purge.py:584; _recall_purged, agents/core/orchestrator.py:3578). It does so before the wipe and again after it, the second time in a finally (agents/core/data_purge.py:458, agents/core/data_purge.py:462). In between, recall serves nothing at all (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3626), so pre-wipe memory is never served during a purge either. - The vector store and the graph are wiped under the store lock (agents/core/data_purge.py:484), so a turn embedding already writing lands before the wipe. Queued embeddings and remember() carry the generation they started under and re-check it under that lock (agents/core/memory/manager.py:648). A search stuck on a dead backend, or a write slower than the 10 s wait, holds the lock: the wipe then goes ahead without it and never releases a lock it did not take. A write that lands after such a wipe removes its own record while it still holds the lock, so no search sees it (agents/core/memory/manager.py:651), and the forget reports the busy lock in not_erased instead of claiming a clean wipe (AUDIT-2). - A single delete does the same through _recall_forget (agents/core/routers/memory_kg.py:336): DELETE /api/kg/entities/{name} and DELETE /api/kg/relations, also when the call is cancelled mid-delete (agents/core/routers/memory_kg.py:696, agents/core/routers/memory_kg.py:757); an entity edit, which replaces its properties (agents/core/routers/memory_kg.py:670); and a consolidation apply that actually removed a vector, even one whose re-embedding then failed (agents/core/routers/memory_kg.py:434). - Known limit: a turn already running when the purge starts still saves its reply afterwards; the purge does not quiesce turns. Tests: tests/test_recall_gate.py has 135 cases. 22 cases from the first review rounds fail on the commits before them, 20 from the delete round fail on 890e80b6, and 22 from the second review fail on 3b4398d2 (two of those only because the handoff is now kept per session). They cover: - the trivial tables, including NFD, a zero-width space, a path, an emoji question and punctuation alone (tests/test_recall_gate.py:42, tests/test_recall_gate.py:53); - NaN and infinite timeouts (tests/test_recall_gate.py:185, tests/test_recall_gate.py:204); - a real MemoryManager whose search hangs while the reply, another session and the next recall proceed (tests/test_recall_gate.py:226); - slow turn embeddings (tests/test_recall_gate.py:265); - the pin before any handoff (tests/test_recall_gate.py:313); - concurrency and cancellation (tests/test_recall_gate.py:326, tests/test_recall_gate.py:337); - the TTL from completion (tests/test_recall_gate.py:352), a failed straggler (tests/test_recall_gate.py:363), and a handoff only to its own session (tests/test_recall_gate.py:975) and never from a caller outside a turn (tests/test_recall_gate.py:958); - the rerank off the loop (tests/test_recall_gate.py:381); - the warm-up served on a timeout and while skipped (tests/test_recall_gate.py:413), never across sessions (tests/test_recall_gate.py:427), replaced by an empty recall (tests/test_recall_gate.py:512), expiring (tests/test_recall_gate.py:445), not for trivial or pinned turns (tests/test_recall_gate.py:466) and not for internal callers (tests/test_recall_gate.py:535); - purges: queued embeddings (tests/test_recall_gate.py:282), a purge inside the bound (tests/test_recall_gate.py:483), between start and timeout (tests/test_recall_gate.py:497), a turn cancelled across one (tests/test_recall_gate.py:988), a recall finishing during it (tests/test_recall_gate.py:730), nothing served while it erases (tests/test_recall_gate.py:930), a failed purge still ending that window (tests/test_recall_gate.py:945), a write already in the store (tests/test_recall_gate.py:551), a write slower than the wait (tests/test_recall_gate.py:851, tests/test_recall_gate.py:878), remember() across a purge (tests/test_recall_gate.py:912), and a lock the purge did not take (tests/test_recall_gate.py:1006); - single deletes: during the search (tests/test_recall_gate.py:756), during the rerank (tests/test_recall_gate.py:768), through the KG routes, also cancelled mid-call (tests/test_recall_gate.py:784, tests/test_recall_gate.py:1066), an entity edit (tests/test_recall_gate.py:1038), a consolidation that removes a vector and one that removes nothing (tests/test_recall_gate.py:807, tests/test_recall_gate.py:1093), a failed recall left with no unretrieved exception (tests/test_recall_gate.py:1126), and a remove that does not take the conversation lock (tests/test_recall_gate.py:574); - the CLI and shutdown (tests/test_recall_gate.py:698, tests/test_recall_gate.py:686, tests/test_recall_gate.py:610). The guards added by the delete round and the second review were mutated out one at a time (21 mutants in the delete round, 23 in the second review, including the two the second reviewer found surviving), and a test failed each time. The memory, recall, purge, KG, consolidation, taint, settings and shutdown suites pass (696 cases, one known local-3.11 failure, identical on the base).
