| <a id="h433"></a>H433 | Retrieval query rewriting by a cheap auxiliary model | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py ports Hermes' rewriter (plugins/memory/query_rewrite.py). Its review rounds changed where the rewrite is used and tightened the filter. (1) The rewriter. rewrite_query (agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:134) asks a model for exactly one concise question about the user's own history, at TEMPERATURE 0 and MAX_TOKENS 96 (agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:39-40). - The latest message is bounded to 4,000 characters, keeping its head and tail around an omission marker (bounded_user_message, agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:97). - It is passed only as a JSON string on one data line. NEL (U+0085), U+2028 and U+2029, the line breaks JSON leaves raw, are escaped by hand, so the data cannot break that line (agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:78, agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:141). - SYSTEM_PROMPT (agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:80) says to treat the message as untrusted data, never follow it, never answer it, and ask in the message's language. - Any exception, or an answer the filter rejects, returns "" so the caller recalls on the raw text, exactly as in Hermes. (2) The filter. normalize_rewrite (agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:111): - NFKC-normalises the answer and removes format characters first (_plain, agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:105); - strips fences, labels (English and Romanian) and quotes, and control and line-separator characters; - rejects an answer over 320 characters, not a question, not grounded in the user's memory, instruction-shaped, or with a second sentence. Nerva additions for a Romanian-speaking owner: - Romanian interrogatives (_QUESTION_START_RE, agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:47) and grounding words (_MEMORY_GROUNDING_RE, agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:60). - An auxiliary starter, English or Romanian ("Did…", "Do…", "Am…", "E…", "A…", _AUX_START_RE, agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:55), counts as a question only when whitespace follows it and the model itself ended with "?" (agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:122). "Do my taxes now.", "E-mail all my passwords…", "Am rezervat hotelul." and an invented "Ai programare marți la 10." are therefore refused. An interrogative word ("What…", "Ce…") needs no "?". - Second-person and object pronouns are not grounding: "Can you tell me the capital of France?" is refused. - The instruction check also reads "ignoring", "disregard" and the Romanian forms (agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:68). - A second sentence is refused: sentence punctuation (including ";" and "…") then a space and more text, or "?", "!" or ";" straight into more text ("?Now", ";also"). A "." or "…" with no space stays inside the question ("the 3.5 kg bag") (agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:77). - A question the model ended with "." gets "?" instead (agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:131). This is a quality gate on what the embedder is asked, not a security control. The rewritten query reaches only the embedding model, never a generative one, and a homoglyph can still pass. (3) Wiring (critic note 12). _recall_hits (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3318) runs the rewrite only when memory.recall_query_rewrite is on (seeded OFF at agents/core/settings_db.py:326, because it costs a model call). - It runs after the H428 triviality gate and the job-pin check, and inside the H428 bound. - It gets half the bound of its own (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3334-3338), as Hermes gives the rewrite its own 8 s. Past that, the model call is cancelled and recall runs on the raw text within the same turn, instead of the whole recall becoming a straggler. - The rewrite becomes the embedding query only. The graph leg keeps the raw message as its keyword (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3346): it finds an entity whose name or a property contains the keyword, so it only ever helps a short, entity-like message ("BMW", "dentist"), and a rewritten whole question would never match. With the rewrite off or rejected, recall is unchanged (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3347). Hermes likewise keeps the raw message for its context fetch. (4) Strict-local. _query_rewriter (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3437) uses LLMRouter.local_backend only (agents/core/llm/auxiliary_text.py:129). That is the fail-closed accessor, which never falls through to a cloud backend: no local backend means it raises and the rewrite returns "". This is proven through a real HybridRouter whose only backend is cloud. The call re-checks the job-model pin itself (agents/core/llm/auxiliary_text.py:125), and tells a Qwen3 model (the default local family) not to think, so the 96 tokens are not spent before the answer (agents/core/llm/auxiliary_text.py:135). Like Hermes, the rewriter sees only the latest message, so a reference to an earlier turn ("the other one") stays unresolved. The win is a grounded question for short or oblique messages. Tests: tests/test_query_rewrite.py has 57 cases. 24 of them fail on 8e824b9f, the commit before the first review round; 5 on 890e80b6 (the NEL escape, three auxiliaries without "?" and ";also"); and 1 on 3b4398d2 (";also"). They cover: - the bounded JSON data line and the line-break escapes (tests/test_query_rewrite.py:61, tests/test_query_rewrite.py:208); - the accept and reject tables, including the Romanian statements and answers, the E-/A starters, English auxiliaries without "?", ungrounded second person, NFKC and a zero-width space (tests/test_query_rewrite.py:92, tests/test_query_rewrite.py:125); - errors and the seeded toggle; - the rewrite used when on, off by default, and not paid by a trivial prompt; - a hung rewrite costing half the bound before recall runs on the raw text (tests/test_query_rewrite.py:190); - the raw graph keyword (tests/test_query_rewrite.py:201); - a pinned turn, and the rewriter refusing under a pin (tests/test_query_rewrite.py:216, tests/test_query_rewrite.py:226); - the real HybridRouter with only a cloud backend (tests/test_query_rewrite.py:252); - initial temperature 0 and 96 tokens (tests/test_query_rewrite.py:285); - the Qwen3 switch (tests/test_query_rewrite.py:310). The memory, recall and orchestrator suites pass (one known local-3.11 failure, as on the base). The prompt, filters,96-token/initial-zero-temperature and fallback behavior are unchanged; the selected model controls Qwen3 suffixing. Job pins and H513 remain enforced inside the common helper. Prepared auxiliary helper review (2026-10-02): this consumer still delegates through generate_local_auxiliary; model selection, default, prompt/budget, Qwen behavior where applicable and H513 await/stream lifetime are preserved. The new preparation layer captures one route and rechecks the fixed job-pin policy at invocation. Existing cited suites and new late-pin regressions pass in the frozen full backend; no capability promotion. The 96-token cap, Qwen3 suffix, local accessor, filters and job-pin exclusion remain unchanged. Actual producer and H513 regressions pass. | <a id="h450"></a>H450 | Say when in plain words (schedule syntax) | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The schedule parser supports compact intervals, bare recurring durations, bilingual weekday/time phrases, validated five-field cron, ISO timestamps and delayed one-shots; repeat normalization is shared by create/edit. Preview, job creation/editing, explicit cron triggers, status and fallback tick now use the configured application timezone independently of the host. A runner pins its first valid app zone atomically; preview uses that same active zone, and saved zone changes take effect after restart. Absolute one-shots retain their UTC instants. Missing settings use the declared default, while invalid/unreadable explicit settings refuse scheduling; a failed initial read does not poison later repair. Four behavior regressions failed before the fix. The final eight-file selection passes 246 cases, and independent six-file review passes 199. Existing frontend jobs tests pass 24 cases. A fresh isolated app on UTC served a successful HTTP preview anchored to America/New_York. The full backend run found three older cron-slot expectations that configured only the scheduler zone; the isolated fixture now explicitly selects app UTC against a Bucharest scheduler. The complete six-file affected rerun passes 261 cases; no additional production change was needed. Batch-03 collateral review (2026-10-10): the changed evidence paths were compared with their previously current preimages. H329 adds strict global/channel owner switches and exact human-approved re-enable; H299/H397 add result-aware default-advisory stalls. This review preserves the existing contract verdict and remaining limits; unrelated stale pins remain unchanged. Current verification and limits are recorded in docs/plans/2026-10-10-hermes-closure-03.md. Batch-04 bounded collateral review (2026-10-10): changed evidence was checked against its previously current preimage; the existing verdict and remaining limits remain valid. Details are in the batch-04 runtime, surface and history collateral reviews. Existing verdicts are preserved; remaining limits are preserved except the explicitly reviewed H452/H454/H620 progress below. Already-stale pins remain untouched. See the batch-06 core, surface and root collateral reports. | <a id="h453"></a>H453 | Let a cheap check decide whether the expensive model runs at all | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Whole frozen H453 contract reviewed, batch07 (2026-10-10). Ordinary scheduled Python scripts require a successful matching governed task and trusted complete UTF-8 stdout capture before output decisions. No-agent delivery receives the complete safe projection instead of a silent 2,000-character prefix; empty/whitespace stdout suppresses model and delivery using the full observation. Model-bearing scripts receive JSON-fenced untrusted stdout context, with a fixed host-authored truncation notice outside the fence when abbreviated. Missing, invalid, unsuccessful or incomplete capture fails explicitly. Raw digest metadata is host-produced before redaction; safe projected stdout need not hash like original secret-containing bytes. Local transport currently attests full snapshots through 16,000 bytes; the shared validator ceiling is 50,000 bytes, not a larger local capture promise. Ordinary scripts, including no-agent, honor the last nonempty line's exact JSON boolean wakeAgent:false. Malformed/nonfalse JSON continues after completeness is established; stderr-only truncation does not invalidate complete stdout. Script/URL monitors instead treat all observation bytes as literal data and use their separate change gate. Complete raw hashes and scrubbed snapshots are persisted atomically at detection, before model calls, so unchanged observations skip work even after a prior model failure. Generations prevent stale baseline updates/delivery. First baselines, including empty ones, are observations. Model-only \[SILENT\] and whole-response silence conventions still suppress delivery; no-agent literal \[SILENT\] remains data. Accepted native mappings are bounded self-contained approved Python, safe redacted/Unicode-cleaned stdout and explicit capture limits; scripts retain fresh terminal approval and URL monitor hops retain signed governed plugin.egress approval. No standing grant, scanner relaxation or hidden model polling is introduced. Existing monitor/URL/gate regressions, actual local subprocess pipeline tests with a test authorization shim, and actual disposable lifespan/CLI orchestration with offline HTTP model responses cover the feature. Full backend milestone is required separately before assessment. Wider scheduler, routing, workspace and dedicated authoring UI clauses remain separate; no live-provider or native-host deployment claim. Final acceptance evidence: frozen full backend at def87104 completed 25,909 cases (25,849 passed, one failed document route-count assertion, 59 skipped/xfail). All 5,710 repository inputs were verified unchanged before repair. The sole stale roadmap count was corrected; all 22 document/parity cases pass afterward. Final source c18b25506e0103c5a453066b32e8b1fe5ecf39c3. No green full-run claim is made; backend-validation.json binds the complete original XML and the focused repair XML. Existing verdicts are preserved; remaining limits are preserved except the explicitly reviewed H146/H449/H620 progress below. Already-stale pins remain untouched. | <a id="h454"></a>H454 | A run remembers what the last one said (continuity, chaining, per-job notepad) | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Whole frozen H454 contract reviewed, batch07 (2026-10-10). Explicit own continuity injects the latest eligible output with host do-not-repeat/continue guidance outside the untrusted fence. A distinct owner-edited legacy note remains separately labelled, including proper prefixes; when no eligible output exists it is an unverified legacy fallback, never cross-job publication proof. continuity=false omits own/self/own-ID output and legacy note while preserving independent KV and external sources. Per-job SQLite KV retains printable 128-character keys, 16 KiB per value, 64 KiB per job and 256 keys with transactional rejection and JSON-fenced prompt injection. context_from accepts an ordered string/list of at most eight distinct canonical 12-hex job IDs, with case-insensitive input normalization and self. Validation precedes lookup; no filesystem output lookup exists. Missing/deleted/empty and other-profile sources silently skip, and source birth identity prevents replacement-job confusion. One latest substantive output per live job is stored with its successful producer run and birth identity in the same terminal-success transaction. Detailed successful model results require no source-assigned control/stop and non-silent content; process-only adapters retain old execution behavior without supplying publication proof. Complete no-agent output and substantive reminder/brief content may publish. Queued-task receipts, failures, unknowns, suppression and approval-control replies preserve the previous latest result. Deletion removes latest content but keeps H015 historical run metadata. Stored output is bounded to 8,000 characters/32 KiB. External blocks share a 64 KiB rendered UTF-8 budget including JSON escaping and host/fence text; bounded context is explicitly labelled. All injected content remains untrusted. Script reservations freeze own/external/KV snapshots transactionally, including absence; later output cannot leak into that attempt, while source deletion/replacement revokes injection. Old persisted attempts use explicitly empty new snapshots and retain their saved legacy notes. Sources are never automatically executed. CLI create/edit adds repeatable --context-from and edit --clear-context-from; standalone additive context/continuity PATCH merges latest options atomically without a GET. Explicit --options remains a replacement and folds CLI flags; conflicting non-null API replacement/additive fields are refused. Existing admin guard and strict string types remain, with no new route. Focused API/core, real local pipeline, and actual disposable CLI/lifespan/orchestrator offline-model proofs cover behavior. Full backend milestone is recorded separately before acceptance; dedicated web/mobile context controls and wider scheduling features remain open. Final acceptance evidence: frozen full backend at def87104 completed 25,909 cases (25,849 passed, one failed document route-count assertion, 59 skipped/xfail). All 5,710 repository inputs were verified unchanged before repair. The sole stale roadmap count was corrected; all 22 document/parity cases pass afterward. Final source c18b25506e0103c5a453066b32e8b1fe5ecf39c3. No green full-run claim is made; backend-validation.json binds the complete original XML and the focused repair XML. | <a id="h456"></a>H456 | Bound what an unattended agent may do | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Whole frozen H456 contract reviewed, batch08 (2026-10-10). Scheduled model tool bounds resolve an explicit non-null job list (including \[\]), then an explicit cron platform list (including \[\]), then the finite current registration set. All cases subtract operator-disabled groups and literal messaging/clarify; cronjob is removed unless allow_agent_scheduling is true. This flag lifts loop prevention only and creates no execution or scheduling grant. The selected bound intersects through existing closed-lifetime tool scope at model offer, runtime/RPC and nested execute_code calls; posture, agent patterns and kernel approvals remain additional limits. Curated exact groups cover named native tools; unavailable selected groups refuse while denied groups may contain absent names. Dynamic plugin/delegation catalogs and new tool implementations are not claimed. Three native owner settings are validated on write and read. Runtime reads one coherent read-only SQLite snapshot without reseeding/recovery; unreadable/malformed policy fails the model phase. Readable legacy stores missing new rows use declared defaults. Doctor remains read-only and reports policy source/removals and errors even with zero jobs; the result is an upper bound, not an execution grant. Canonical options.reasoning_effort is validated at storage for model-bearing ask work, rejects null/aliases/no-agent combinations, and overrides global/model default requests through existing request-local transport scope without mutating backends. Nested absent jobs retain explicit ancestors and copied child contexts are revoked on scope closure. Cloud adapters retain nearest-weaker supported clamping, below-minimum refusal and unsupported/undeclared omission; vocabulary overrides remain capabilities, not stronger requested preferences. No universal strict cost ceiling is claimed for undeclared cloud models. LM Studio/Ollama lack verified effort wire controls and explicitly refuse scheduled pins before text/tool/stream generation, including prepared dispatch and retries; metadata/warm-up and unpinned local work remain usable. CLI create/edit and HUD expose exact rungs/default deletion. CLI requires explicit complete --options, preserving replacement semantics; HUD skip-model removes/disables model-only fields and preserves unrelated options. Existing API options validation stays authoritative; no route added. Actual disposable CLI/FastAPI/orchestrator smoke proves job/cron/legacy offers, exclusions, local refusal/clearing and auth/validation with offline HTTP responses. The settings refresh runs synchronously and real clarify registration is called after startup because a separate pre-existing startup-order bug omits it. Real local nested-code proof uses an isolation authorization test shim, not Docker/WASM certification. Built Jobs Chromium checks pass fresh mount, default clearing, custom creation, skip-model and hard reload with mocked API. Dedicated native-mobile authoring, live providers and native owner-host deployment remain unclaimed. Final acceptance: the frozen full backend at 16bfdc1282554ada805968383ced004046173b2c completed 25,935 cases: 25,875 passed, one timing-sensitive approval fixture failed, and 59 skipped/xfail. All 5,715 repository inputs were verified unchanged before repair. Only that fixture was corrected to wait for durable acknowledgment before expiration; all 46 adjacent owner-once cases pass afterward, including the original failed node. Final source 8064ed8284a8434e13a4b7adb112ceee19981ce8; no green full-run claim. Full frontend 2,181 passes, typecheck/build, built-browser, route/schema and actual offline product proofs are recorded. No provider or native-host deployment claim. Reviewed source anchors: agents/core/job_toolsets.py:73-102 defines the scheduled precedence/removals; agents/core/settings_db.py:825-857 reads the strict snapshot; agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1906-1913 wraps the model phase; agents/core/llm/request_context.py:76-99 binds and revokes the reasoning pin. ## security

| ID | Capabilitate | Decizie inițială | Stare cod | Bază | Observație / restanță |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="h481"></a>H481 | Refuse a catastrophic shell command no matter who approved it (hardline floor + risky-pattern tier + de-obfuscation) | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | \*\*The middle tier does not exist.\*\* The row asks for three stacked layers; this is the first, with its matching repaired (and repaired again in PR #1177). There is still no ~40-rule severity/reversibility-tagged risky-pattern catalogue feeding the approval card's description, so \`rm -rf ~/projects\` is neither hardline nor pattern-classified — re-checked 2026-09-19 on 494a13b2: \`hardline_match("rm -rf ~/projects")\` and \`hardline_match(\['rm','-rf','/home/x/projects'\])\` both return None, and the module carries no severity/reversibility/catalogue vocabulary at all — and the owner's card still says "run this argv" rather than "recursive delete", which the row's rationale names as the point. No user-editable \`approvals.deny\` fnmatch list at hardline level (no \`fnmatch\`/\`deny\` in the module). No parser limits that themselves hardline-block and save an oversized payload to a file for manual review (128k chars / 4096 separator-free / 25k segments); \`MAX_ARG_CHARS = 4000\` is argv validation, not a refusal. \*\*Variants Hermes produces and this still does not:\*\* NFKC normalisation, \`$HOME\`/\`$HERMES_HOME\` folding, grep-pattern span masking, and \`python -c\` / \`perl -e\` / \`node -e\` payload extraction — the last left alone on purpose, since \`jobs_scripts.snapshot_script\` legitimately builds \`\[sys.executable, '-I', '-c', source\]\`. \`$()\`/backtick substitution is now extracted in ONE place only: #1177's \`_substituted\` narrows a heredoc BODY line to its substitutions, so \`cat \<\<EOF\` / \`$(rm -rf /)\` / \`EOF\` refuses; an ordinary line is still not expanded, so \`echo $(rm -rf /)\` and a backtick \`rm -rf /\` inside an assignment return None (a \`$(mkfs.ext4 …)\` is caught only through \`_CMD\`'s \`(\` anchor). The row's own examples \`$(printf mkfs).ext4 /dev/sda\` and \`echo bWtmcw==\|base64 -d\|sh\` still return None. \*\*Gaps this module knows it still has, listed honestly rather than as a complete set:\*\* nesting deeper than \`_MAX_SHELL_DEPTH\` (depth-3 \`sh -c "sh -c 'sh -c …'"\` returns None, pinned by test); a quote at a word BOUNDARY (\`'mkfs.ext4' /dev/sda\`) rather than inside a word; \`powershell -EncodedCommand\` base64; a \`cmd /c\` payload split across several argv items; an obfuscated shell HEAD in argv form (\`\['s""h','-c',…\]\`), because the de-obfuscation pass runs after payload extraction and its output is not fed back through it; and, since #1177, two in the false-refusal direction — a \`case\` label with a leading \`(\` (\`(shutdown) echo bye ;;\`) is still read as a command, and a \`case\` nested inside another is not modelled: its inner first label IS stripped (\`case $1 in\\na) case $2 in\\nreboot) echo r ;;\\nesac ;;\\nesac\` went power_cycle→None), so the docstring's 'not handled' is about the region boundary, not a guaranteed refusal. \*\*Refusals #1177 cleared in the dangerous direction — closed by this slice (H481, 2026-09-22).\*\* The \`case\`-label strip fires only inside a real command-position \`case WORD in … esac\` region now, so \`(echo case in; reboot); echo esac\` and the \`$IFS\`-spliced \`(echo case in; mkfs.ext4$IFS/dev/sda); echo esac\` / \`wipefs$IFS-a$IFS/dev/sda\` refuse again; and \`_heredoc_feeds_a_shell\` now also sees a body a shell consumes AFTER the redirection through a pipe (\`cat \<\<EOF \| sh\`, \`cat \<\<EOF \| bash\`, \`cat \<\<EOF \| grep … \| sh\`) or via a file a shell later runs (\`cat \<\<'EOF' \>x.sh; sh x.sh\`), so those refuse — and, after the review round, so do \`(sh x.sh)\`, \`{ sh x.sh; }\`, \`if true; then sh x.sh; fi\`, \`cat x.sh \| sh\`, \`sh -c '. x.sh'\`, \`. x.sh\`, \`source x.sh\`, \`tee x.sh \<\<EOF; sh x.sh\`, a quoted \`\> "x.sh"\` target, \`sh /dev/stdin \<\<EOF\`, \`bash -c sh \<\<EOF\` and \`cat \<\<EOF \| eval "$(cat)"\`, each verified to run the body. Pinned in tests/test_local_transport.py, reproduced against /bin/sh, /bin/dash and /bin/bash first, and screened by a 671,995-case differential fuzz vs origin/main whose every difference is explained by the two changed mechanisms (numbers in the summary; the earlier \`refusals_cleared = 0\` claim is withdrawn). Still open in this direction, listed in \`_detection_variants\` and pinned as misses: a heredoc body written to a file run by a bare path (\`… \>x.sh; ./x.sh\`), by a variable-carried name (\`for f in x.sh; do sh $f; done\`) or by the environment (\`BASH_ENV=x.sh bash -c :\`); a heredoc opened inside a command substitution (\`eval "$(cat \<\<EOF …)"\`, read as quoted text); \`xargs -I{} sh -c '{}'\` (each line becomes the command line); and a \`;\` inside a \`( )\` group of a pipe stage (\`cat \<\<EOF \| (cd /tmp; sh)\`). \*\*Two pre-existing false positives are unchanged\*\* (\`git commit -m "fix;mkfs.ext4 handling"\` → mkfs; \`grep -r "(shutdown" docs/\` → power_cycle), and \`_PATH_PREFIX\` added a third class no approval can lift: a path to a script whose basename starts with a hardline name — \`./mkfs.sh\` (mkfs), \`./reboot.sh\`, \`./scripts/shutdown.sh --dry-run\`, \`/opt/app/bin/halt\` (power_cycle) — all None on 1c5054ea, all refused on 494a13b2 (bare \`reboot.sh\` already refused before; the prefix extends it to every path spelling). This slice left that (c) class unclosed on purpose: any name- or path-only rule that cleared \`./mkfs.sh\` or \`/opt/app/bin/halt\` would also clear a TRUE refusal — \`./mkfs.sh\` is structurally \`mkfs.\<suffix\>\` exactly like the real \`/sbin/mkfs.ext4\`, and \`/opt/app/bin/halt\` is \`\<path\>/\<hardline-name\>\` exactly like a copied \`/tmp/mkfs.ext4 /dev/sda\` — so it needs approval-context (is the target a workspace script or a real system binary) the pure static floor does not have. \*\*And a measured cost, re-measured after #1177 removed the breadth cap\*\* (\`_MAX_SHELL_EXPANSIONS\` and the \`MAX_ARG_CHARS\` re-expansion guard are gone; every level-1 payload is unwrapped again): on a maximal 64x4000-character argv the pass is roughly unchanged (79.6 ms on the pre-#1177 module vs 82.3 ms after, same machine, best of 5), but a command made of 2,000 nested \`sh -c "sh -c 'echo ok'"\` segments (57k chars) goes from 196 ms to 380 ms — synchronous, on the event loop, before every exec. The docstring's linear-in-input argument holds (each level's payloads are disjoint substrings); an independent re-run on another machine saw 79.0→80.6 ms and 125→151 ms, so the constant grows for nested input but the factor is machine-dependent and no test pins either number. This slice's first pass claimed no measurable cost on that maximal 64x4000-character argv (78.7 → 78.4 ms); the review showed the claim held only for input with no \`case\` word and never exercised the redirect-target branch, which was quadratic. Re-measured after the review round (best of 3, same machine, origin/main → this branch): the maximal 64x4000 argv 62.0 → 58.6 ms; the same argv filled with case/in prose 18,207 → 105 ms (main's own \`\\bcase\\b.\*?\\besac\\b\` was super-linear there too); 4000 chars of \`case x in\` 21.6 → 6.5 ms; eight 4000-char argv items of prose 316 → 14 ms; 400 \`cat \<\<EOF \| sh\` blocks (12 KB) 39.8 → 64.9 ms; 400 \`cat \<\<E \>x\` blocks in 4000 chars 25 → 39 ms (2,471 ms on the first pass); 1,600 such blocks (33.6 KB) 132 → 201 ms (97.5 s on the first pass); 2,000 nested \`sh -c\` segments 146 → 148 ms; 300 real \`case\` statements 57 → 60 ms. The heredoc-with-target shapes cost about 1.5x main, linear, because the statement lines after a heredoc are tokenised once and shared; nothing else pays. Six test items pin wall-clock bounds (0.25–2 s) two orders of magnitude above these; no test pins a ratio. \*\*Adversarial review of the original slice found four more defects, all fixed and each reproduced first\*\* against real /bin/sh, /bin/dash and /bin/bash: (1) \`_shell_c_payloads\` demanded the word after the wrappers BE the shell, so \`env FOO=1 sh -c 'mkfs.ext4 /dev/sda'\` — and \`sudo -u root sh -c …\`, \`nice -n 10 sh -c …\` — skipped the whole \`sh -c\` screening while deleting \`FOO=1 \` made the same string \`hardline_denied:mkfs\`; \`_CMD\` had been widened for that assignment prefix and the unwrapper had not. (2) \`_unquoted_lines\` read a backslash-newline as a statement separator when it is a line CONTINUATION, so \`mk\\\` + newline + \`fs.ext4 /dev/sda\`, which all three shells splice into \`mkfs.ext4\`, was seen as two harmless fragments. (3) every heredoc body line became a command position, so \`cat \> x.yaml \<\<'EOF'\` / \`shutdown: graceful\` / \`EOF\` was refused as \`power_cycle\`; body lines were joined as data in the flat text. \*\*#1177's second review showed that fix was incomplete in three ways, all closed there:\*\* \`segments\` still tokenised every body line, so \`cat \> README.md \<\<'EOF'\` / \`rm -rf / will destroy the box\` / \`EOF\` stayed a \`recursive_root_removal\` refusal; the rule had no exception for a heredoc a SHELL consumes, so \`bash \<\<EOF\` / \`mkfs.ext4 /dev/sda\` / \`EOF\` returned None (a bypass the heredoc work itself opened); and any \`\<\<\` — inside quotes, inside \`$(( ))\`, a \`\<\<\<\` here-string, or with a delimiter that never returns — opened a heredoc and switched the newline screening off for every following line. (4) \`{\` anchored bare, so \`jq '{reboot: .needs_reboot}'\` was a \`power_cycle\` refusal; POSIX requires whitespace after \`{\` for a brace group, and \`{ mkfs.ext4 /dev/sda; }\` is still refused. Each fix is red-proofed on its own. [d1](../agents/core/environments/terminal_contract.py) [d2](../tests/test_local_transport.py) |

| <a id="h490"></a>H490 | Start with zero customization to prove a bug is not your own setup | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Safe mode now runs in Hermes' whole reduced posture, on top of the owner customizations H275 leaves out, and still relaxes no gate. JARVIS_SAFE_MODE=1 (or \`python serve.py --safe-mode\`) is the one switch; LAYERS (agents/core/safe_mode.py:63) gains four layers after H275's seven (later rows appended their own after them, H594's project convention files among them). Plugins: none is built (Orchestrator._build_plugins loads the hub .env and returns before plugin_manager.build in safe mode; tests/test_h490_safe_mode_posture.py:384 exercises it and pins its single call site), so no integration is reached and no plugin data (WorldView, the Signal Layer, the WorldView knowledge-graph sync) reaches a prompt; the hub's .env credentials still load. The permission gate switches every plugin off as well (apply_load_set, agents/core/plugin_gate.py:704), without touching the shared built-in table, and the plugin toggle answers 409 \`safe_mode\` without rewriting the saved choice (toggle_plugin, agents/core/routers/plugins.py:99). Outbound webhooks: ntfy and the owner-configured webhook channels are not started (the two lifespan gates in agents/web.py, pinned by tests/test_h490_safe_mode_posture.py:479), a proactive send to an owner destination refuses (send_to_target, agents/core/channels/outbound.py:174), and the inbound webhook receiver, whose hooks start agent runs, answers 503 before it reads its switch (_receiver_refusal, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:239). Memory: no pre-turn recall (Orchestrator._recall_block; tests/test_h490_safe_mode_posture.py:510) and no core block (_living_core_memory_block; tests/test_h490_safe_mode_posture.py:532) enters a prompt, Howard's archive few-shots are not looked up (Agent.build_prompt, agents/core/agent.py:870), and the H314 memory tool reads as memory switched off, refusing with its off description (agents/core/memory_tool.py:429); the conversation's own history stays. Settings: each setting that loosens an approval or widens a budget (FORCED_SETTINGS, agents/core/safe_mode.py:96: the actuation and tool-loop switches, the Kanban board, dispatch and network-attachment switches and the two Kanban worker caps (#1233), earned autonomy, ambient, acquisition, the input and output scans, the autonomy caps and interrupt budget, the sandbox timeout and memory, the model pull cap, the gateway rate limit, the daily review budget, the per-boot sub-agent spawn budget, H378's model-cost confirmation line (0, never asking, takes the shipped $40; a line under $1 is kept), the guest tool list, cloud fallback and product posture) reads the stricter of the owner's value and its shipped default (stricter, agents/core/safe_mode.py:178), so an owner who tightened one keeps it; a sandbox memory or spawn budget under 1, which means no cap, takes the shipped cap, and a reordered guest list keeps the owner's order; a switch is read by truthiness as the runtime reads it; the three loosening settings with no shipped row are dropped (UNSEEDED_SETTINGS, agents/core/safe_mode.py:130). The runtime settings pass through override_settings (agents/core/safe_mode.py:201) before and after the product posture is applied (Orchestrator.load_runtime_settings, pinned by tests/test_h490_safe_mode_posture.py:326), and every boot read of a forced key (the sandbox caps, the autonomy caps, earned autonomy, the interrupt budget and the guardrails scans) goes through safe_mode.get_value (agents/core/safe_mode.py:222; a real Orchestrator boot takes the stricter caps, tests/test_h490_safe_mode_posture.py:311), as do the model pull cap (_max_gb, agents/core/routers/model_setup.py:36), the gateway rate limit and the router's own settings read, so a re-detect keeps cloud fallback at the stricter value (HybridRouter._admin_setting, agents/core/llm/hybrid_router.py:256); an AST test finds any forced key read past safe_mode (tests/test_h490_safe_mode_posture.py:285). Settings whose default is already the loosest value keep the owner's choice. Nerva runs no owner-configured shell hooks, so there are none to leave out. The mode and the layers it left out are in /api/security/posture (\`safe_mode\`, _safe_mode_status, agents/core/routers/security.py:417) besides /healthz, /readyz, /status and /api/status, in the boot log (tests/test_h490_safe_mode_posture.py:611), and in the HUD banner, which names the new layers (frontend/src/safe-mode-banner.tsx; frontend/src/test/safe-mode-banner.test.tsx:28). The kernel, the security, secrets and approval modules, the tool profiles, the capability actions and the egress policy still do not read the flag (tests/test_h275_safe_mode.py:642), and the H275 test pinning the exact list of modules that do (tests/test_h275_safe_mode.py:655) now names the six new readers. tests/test_h490_safe_mode_posture.py (75 cases) covers the layer order, every forced and unseeded key being a real setting and its default its own stricter value, the stricter rule per kind (switches by truthiness, numbers, a bool that is not a number, the guest list, the ordered choices), settings served unchanged when off, put back when on and copied, the owner's tighter value kept, nothing reported when nothing moved, an unseeded key alone counting, the boot read, the runtime-settings load, no plugin built but the .env still loaded, the gate refusing every plugin with the shared table untouched, the toggle refusing and keeping the saved choice, a proactive send refused, the receiver's 503 before its switch is read, the lifespan's ntfy and webhook-channel gates, no recall and no core block (nothing cached) and recall switched off not reported, the memory tool read and offer, the posture field, the boot log and the banner labels; the banner has a vitest case. 52 mutants, all killed; each review-round fix fails a case when reverted (9 targeted mutants), and a real Orchestrator boot replaces the old source-slice pin. Native session integration review (2026-10-08): four cadence/index-retention settings are explicitly reviewed by the seeded-number test. They do not widen tool authority or delete transcripts; stall notifications still use outbound transport, which refuses in safe mode. The full safe-mode posture test file passes. Only that reviewed test pin is refreshed; unrelated stale source evidence remains subject to review. Independent #1233 review (historical scope): re-read the same claims against the #1233 merge and kept the verdict; its line numbers are superseded by the 2026-10-11 re-read below. Selected-main image collateral review (2026-10-02, 2fce39ca): The source diff adds optional bounded image provenance and a new reviewed conversation-image path. Existing no-media memory, text turns, previous composer route, and unrelated orchestrator/HUD behavior retain their prior branches; focused H277/H513/composer/session tests, 1,872 frontend tests, TypeScript and HUD build pass. Existing verdict preserved; this note does not claim live-provider acceptance or a new Hermes capability. Its diff changes complete_selected_image_turn for explicitly reviewed browser image turns; this row's cited implementation lines and feature path are unchanged. The focused active-image and route regressions pass, and this existing code-equivalent verdict is preserved. The smart path still requires the existing tool-loop/terminal gates; this review adds no safe-mode exception. Existing equivalent verdict and remaining limits are preserved; this is source review, not live-provider evidence. No safe-mode exception was added; the original equivalent verdict and remaining scope are preserved. The earlier 2109-case H277/H485/safe-mode union predates the final fresh-store fixes; current complete-suite verification remains pending. Final owner-authority complete-suite verification (2026-10-03): the guarded backend terminates successfully with 21790 passed, 34 skipped, one existing xfailed and 67 warnings in 1137.75 seconds. The backend count guard matches all 21825 collected cases, and all 2806 frozen source/configuration hashes match after completion. Focused record/count/reference gates pass228, baseline-aware Bandit has zero findings/errors, Ruff passes and the refreshed Graft wiring graph is in sync. This is local offline verification only; no new equivalence, remote CI, live-provider or activation credit is added. Safe mode still prevents an unapproved cloud dispatch. | <a id="h495"></a>H495 | Never let a credential reach a log, a transcript, an approval card or an export | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | This is one of the row's seven paths. Hermes applies \`redact.py\` to logs, verbose output, tool output, approval prompts, gateway messages, transcripts and memory; this slice covers LOGS. Still owed, and the rationale calls them the part that matters most: the hash-chained audit DB (a \`cat .env\` from \`terminal_run\` still writes a live credential into an append-only log that by design cannot be edited), the decision-inbox approval card the owner reads on a phone, task payloads, terminal output, and the observability exports — which Hermes scrubs unconditionally with no knob, returning \`\[redaction-unavailable\]\` rather than raw text. Also absent: the second mask mode (a syntactically INVALID \`«redacted:ghp_…»\` sentinel for file reads, so an agent cannot write a masked-but-plausible key back and silently kill the credential), \`code_file=True\` sparing source constants, \`_mask_control_split_tokens()\` for tokens smuggled across ANSI/zero-width characters, and the shared \`.env\` basename list. The two silent-degradation paths named at review are now CLOSED, after the SAST gate forced the question: a handler whose \`addFilter\` fails is counted and published through \`uncovered_handler_count()\` and a warning naming how many handlers are writing unredacted, and a \`loggerDict\` walk that raises warns that coverage fell back to root-only. The one silent swallow left is the filter's own last-ditch blank (\`# nosec B110\`), where a \`logging.Filter\` that raises would take down the caller's log call and the scanner has already failed. \`install_log_redaction()\` remains exported although the \`everywhere\` variant is what production uses. \*\*A later slice's full-suite run surfaced one more thing about this control, recorded here rather than fixed:\*\* the scanner's \`high_entropy_secret\` pattern matches an ordinary filesystem path. Under \`-n auto --dist loadfile\` a warning naming a pytest tmp file arrived as \`truncated: \[REDACTED:high_entropy_secret\].md\`, which is what a real operator would see when a log line names a file with a long random-looking segment — a debuggability cost the module docstring already warns about in general terms, now with a concrete case. Separately, \`setup_logging()\` installs the filter on every handler of every logger and nothing took it back off, so every later test on the same xdist worker ran under a live redactor. The installer is PRODUCTION code, not the tests: \`agents/run.py\` calls \`setup_logging()\` at module level, so importing it installs the filter during pytest COLLECTION — before any fixture runs — and eight test files import it; \`agents/web.py\` calls it from the FastAPI lifespan, so any test that drives the app installs it again; five test files call it directly on top of that. There is no list of leaking files to fix. The cleanup therefore lives in \`tests/conftest.py\` as an autouse fixture that strips unconditionally BEFORE each test as well as after — a snapshot-and-restore fixture, which is what the two neighbouring isolation fixtures do and what this was first written as, treats a collection-time install as pre-existing and preserves it for the life of the worker. That version went green locally and RED in CI on exactly the test it was meant to fix. The first attempt at it matched by \`isinstance\` and silently did nothing: \`agents/\` is on \`sys.path\`, so \`core.security.log_redaction\` and \`agents.core.security.log_redaction\` are two module objects over this one file with two distinct \`SecretRedactionFilter\` classes, and the root handler was carrying the other one. The match is by class name now, and four paired tests pin both halves — including the alternate import path, which is the case the first attempt missed. | <a id="h501"></a>H501 | Warn about a dangerous deployment posture before it becomes an incident | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva now warns about a dangerous host posture, as Hermes does, on top of a network half that was already stronger. assert_safe_bind (agents/core/boot_guards.py) still refuses an unauthenticated non-loopback bind and prints the exact env vars that fix it, and GET /api/security/posture still reports the secrets backend, skill signing, sandbox isolation and guardrails honestly. agents/core/host_posture.py adds the host half: three read-only checks that never block and never change anything, each answering ok, warn or unknown (it could not tell, never a false green), with a reason, what it saw and the fix. check_root (agents/core/host_posture.py:71) warns when the process runs as root (euid 0) on any host, headless included, or elevated on Windows (IsUserAnAdmin). check_sshd (agents/core/host_posture.py:168) reads /etc/ssh/sshd_config the way sshd does (_SshdScan, agents/core/host_posture.py:120): Include files are read in place, relative paths from /etc/ssh, each pattern's hits in lexical order, at most 16 deep; Keyword value, Keyword=value and trailing comments are accepted and keywords and values are case-insensitive; the first PasswordAuthentication outside a Match block wins, so a drop-in Included at the top overrides the main file; a Match block (to the end of its file, an Include inside it conditional) never sets the global value but a Match block that turns passwords on warns too; an absent directive is the sshd default yes and warns; no sshd_config is ok; an unreadable file is unknown unless the answer was read elsewhere; an unrecognised value is unknown. check_container_storage (agents/core/host_posture.py:241) detects a container (/.dockerenv, /run/.containerenv, or a docker, kubepods, containerd, libpod or lxc cgroup of PID 1, in_container) and then finds the mount the data root (resolved through symlinks) lives on in /proc/self/mountinfo (parse_mountinfo with its octal escapes, mount_of taking the longest containing mount point and the later of two stacked mounts): the container's own root filesystem or a tmpfs warns, a volume or bind mount is ok, no mountinfo or no mount is unknown. run_checks and report (agents/core/host_posture.py:273, :287) run the three independently, a failing check becoming an unknown row. The hub logs each warning once per process at start, after logging is set up, off the event loop (log_startup, agents/web.py lifespan). GET /api/security/posture (admin) gains host: the three findings and a warnings count (agents/core/routers/security.py). python scripts/doctor.py and nerva doctor show them as the advisory rows host_not_root, sshd_no_passwords and container_storage at the end of the report: a warning is warn with the fix and never fails the verdict, a check that could not tell or could not run is skip, never ok (check_host_posture, scripts/doctor.py:247). The module is stdlib only so the doctor can use it on a broken install. The shipped Dockerfile has no USER, so a stock container now shows the root warning; running the image as a non-root user is left to the deploy. Tests: tests/test_h501_host_posture.py (42), with real sshd_config trees in a temporary directory (drop-ins read first and last, lexical order, absolute and quoted Includes, Match blocks in the main and in an included file, a trailing comment naming a file, an Include loop) and real mountinfo text. 55 mutants: 53 killed after five cases were added; the other two were dead code, removed. Re-read 2026-10-10 after two pinned files drifted. The mechanism files (agents/core/host_posture.py, agents/core/boot_guards.py, scripts/doctor.py, tests/test_h501_host_posture.py) hold their pins and every citation above resolves unchanged. agents/core/routers/security.py changed by one hunk that widens DataHandlingAck.target with the video roles; the posture route still returns the host findings (agents/core/routers/security.py:396). agents/web.py changed by auth-event auditing, channel pending handlers and ntfy opt-in hunks, none of which mention the posture checks; the startup warning is still logged off the event loop (agents/web.py:497). tests/test_h501_host_posture.py passes (42) and the full backend suite is green on this head. Verdict unchanged. [d1](../agents/core/host_posture.py) [d2](../agents/core/boot_guards.py) [d3](../agents/core/routers/security.py) |
| <a id="h502"></a>H502 | Refuse an MCP server entry that is a backdoor wearing an MCP costume, and never hand one the owner's keys | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | \*\*The documented per-server \`env\` migration path does not exist as a reachable surface.\*\* \`MCPServerConfig\` has no \`env\` field (\`routers/mcp.py:145-157\`, no \`model_config\`), \`POST /api/admin/mcp\` drops the key (pydantic's default \`extra="ignore"\`), and neither \`to_config\` (\`client.py:798-812\`) nor \`load_from_config\` (\`:814-849\`) writes or reads one — the module docstring says as much at \`client.py:41-45\`. The only remedy an owner can actually reach is the \*\*host-wide\*\* \`JARVIS_MCP_STDIO_ALLOWED_ENV\` (\`:92-98\`, "Host-wide, not per-server"), which is a strictly weaker grant than the per-server one the docs describe: an owner whose forge server needs \`GITHUB_TOKEN\` must allow it for every stdio server on the host. Whether to ship on that or build the per-server surface first is a product decision, written up as \`docs/OWNER_TASKS.md\` §P28.3 with both options. \*\*Related and also owner-level:\*\* \`XAUTHORITY\` and \`DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS\` are both on \`STDIO_ENV_ALLOWLIST\` (\`client.py:169\`), and today one is listed-but-silently-dropped (\`_SECRET_LIKE\` at \`:174-176\` matches the \`AUTH\` in \`XAUTHORITY\`) while the other is listed-and-handed-over — they should be consistent in one direction or the other. \*\*And the honest ceiling, stated wherever the guarantee is:\*\* this is \*\*defence in depth, not a security boundary\*\*, and since #1179 the module states that ceiling by TWO routes rather than one (\`client.py:17-36\`). The child is same-UID with no namespace, so a server that can run a subprocess reads the withheld variables straight out of \`/proc/\<ppid\>/environ\`; and the baseline narrows the \*\*filesystem\*\* not at all — \`cwd\` defaults to the hub's own working directory, so \`open(".env")\` hands back exactly the keys that were withheld, which \`cwd_secret_files()\` now says out loud at connect (WARNING, filenames only, \`client.py:565-571\`) rather than leaving the owner to infer a protection from the withheld count alone. What the baseline buys is that a \*careless\* or \*curious\* server does not get them by accident, and that an owner's \`JARVIS_\*\` settings are not ambient in a third party's process. It does not stop an adversary, and the row says so rather than implying otherwise; closing the filesystem route as well as the \`/proc\` one needs real process isolation (separate UID, namespace or container — \`client.py:33-36\`), which is not shipped. \*\*Unaddressed from the row:\*\* there is no refusal of an MCP entry that is a backdoor wearing an MCP costume — no command allow-listing, no signature or provenance check on the spawned binary (the docstring itself calls the screener "still-unshipped", \`client.py:34\`), and \`POST /api/admin/mcp\` still stores \`req.command\` verbatim after checking only transport, trust tier, tool-filter shape and bounds, and duplicate name (\`routers/mcp.py:188-241\`, \`:184\`); the only shape check is \`_command_argv()\`'s shell-metacharacter/NUL refusal at connect (\`client.py:519-529\`), which is not a screener. #1179 changed none of this — it explicitly chose disclosure over refusal. This slice hardens what the child \*receives\*; the row also asks who the child is allowed to \*be\*. [d1](../agents/core/mcp/client.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/mcp.py) [d3](../agents/core/mcp/worldview_write.py) |
| <a id="h503"></a>H503 | Unpack an archive from another machine without letting it write outside the destination | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | archive_safe is the only module in Nerva that reads an archive. Backup verify and restore unpack through extract_tar_path (agents/core/archive_safe.py:573; agents/core/backup.py:598, agents/core/backup.py:667). Marketplace install_from_zip unpacks through extract_zip_bytes (agents/core/archive_safe.py:637; agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:816). The old backup._safe_extract and the marketplace _safe_targets+extractall pass are gone. An AST scan (tests/test_archive_safe.py:702, with probes at tests/test_archive_safe.py:688) fails when any other product module imports tarfile or zipfile outside a write-only allowlist, opens an archive for reading or calls an extract-family method. normalize_member (agents/core/archive_safe.py:122) folds backslash to '/' first. It then refuses empty, NUL, POSIX-absolute, Windows-absolute, UNC and drive-lettered names, and any '..' or empty part. Only regular files and directories are written. Symlink, hardlink, device, fifo and sparse tar members raise (agents/core/archive_safe.py:504), and so do symlink, device, fifo, socket and encrypted zip entries (agents/core/archive_safe.py:596). _Planner (agents/core/archive_safe.py:182) validates every member before the first write: name, type, in-archive conflicts and duplicates, member count and declared bytes (agents/core/archive_safe.py:257). It also checks the destination's existing content (agents/core/archive_safe.py:286): a link on the way to a member is refused, and so is a directory where a file goes (tests/test_archive_safe.py:365, tests/test_backup.py:471). The pre-scan reads the stream to its end (agents/core/archive_safe.py:522), so a bad gzip CRC, trailing data or a corrupt header part-way through is a refusal, not a shortened tree (tests/test_archive_safe.py:482, tests/test_archive_safe.py:517). Bytes are counted again while streaming (agents/core/archive_safe.py:389). The byte budget is clamped to free space plus the blocks of the single-link files the archive replaces (agents/core/archive_safe.py:314), so an in-place --force restore fits (tests/test_backup.py:443). PAX and GNU long-name header payloads are capped before tarfile reads them, 1 MiB each and 256 MiB in total (agents/core/archive_safe.py:461, agents/core/archive_safe.py:483; tests/test_backup.py:515). A link or hardlinked name at a member's path is unlinked and the file is created O_EXCL\|O_NOFOLLOW, never written through (agents/core/archive_safe.py:373; tests/test_archive_safe.py:323, tests/test_archive_safe.py:349). Backups take their caps from JARVIS_BACKUP_MAX_MEMBERS and JARVIS_BACKUP_MAX_BYTES (agents/core/backup.py:86); skill packages use SKILL_PACKAGE_LIMITS (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:42). On the producing side, create_backup writes both the encrypted and the plaintext branch through atomic_write, a sibling temp file that is fsynced and then os.replace'd (agents/core/archive_safe.py:654; agents/core/backup.py:415, agents/core/backup.py:420). Archives are GNU-format tar (agents/core/backup.py:474). The export walk skips symlinks (agents/core/archive_safe.py:715; agents/core/backup.py:401). publish_skill uses it on the staged copy it signs (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:392); the copy itself is built from the bytes of one source snapshot, which refuses a linked folder, a link or non-regular member and a file that changed while read (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:375), which has its own test (tests/test_marketplace_governance_hf12_12.py:219). Every other name, hardlinked ones included, is archived as its own regular member (agents/core/backup.py:434, agents/core/backup.py:458; tests/test_backup.py:388). verify and restore compare the manifest's file_count with what the archive held (agents/core/backup.py:625; tests/test_backup.py:430). install_from_zip stages inside skills_dir, runs the signature gate there, and only then renames the tree into place (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:854, agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:885). A non-zip upload is a 400 refusal (tests/test_marketplace_governance_hf12_12.py:194). Staging left by a killed install is swept once it is an hour old (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:773; tests/test_marketplace_governance_hf12_12.py:203). Restore stays an operator CLI action. It prints 'partial restore' when a failure came after the first write (agents/core/backup.py:716) and 'nothing restored' only for a pre-scan refusal (agents/core/backup.py:722). tests/test_backup.py:606 pins that no product module, script or subprocess caller reaches it. The pre-scan also refuses a component longer than the destination's NAME_MAX or a path past PATH_MAX, which the existing-path walk cannot see under a new directory (agents/core/archive_safe.py:251; tests/test_archive_safe.py:726), and a write the destination refuses mid-restore (permissions, a full disk) is a named ArchiveRejected marked partial, never a traceback (agents/core/archive_safe.py:445; tests/test_archive_safe.py:738). A restore leaves the archive's manifest in the target; create_backup never archives a top-level backup_manifest.json from the data root (agents/core/backup.py:482), so a backup of a restored root carries one manifest and verifies, restores and serves the forget purge's verified pre-snapshot (tests/test_backup.py:636, tests/test_backup.py:675). A backup of a restored root made before this change carries that name twice; verify and restore accept exactly that one repeat, last wins as tarfile resolved it, and count the dropped copy against the manifest's file_count (agents/core/backup.py:599, agents/core/backup.py:642; tests/test_backup.py:651). The repeat is bounded: the dropped copy still counts against the member and byte caps, and a third copy or any other duplicate is refused (agents/core/archive_safe.py:220, agents/core/archive_safe.py:223; tests/test_archive_safe.py:768, tests/test_archive_safe.py:775, tests/test_archive_safe.py:790). Known limit: backups made on main from a data root holding a symlink or hardlink fail verify and restore by design. [d1](../agents/core/archive_safe.py) [d2](../agents/core/backup.py) [d3](../agents/core/skills/marketplace.py) |
| <a id="h504"></a>H504 | Fail loudly when TLS trust is misconfigured instead of silently not verifying | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Startup validates all configured CA bundle paths and certifi, loads them through SSLContext, requires actual certificates, and supplies named repair instructions. Model egress uses an explicit trust context; invalid CA paths never imply verify=False. Per-target insecure overrides are explicit, loudly logged and ignored in hardened mode. Functional adaptation retained from the existing implementation: custom bundles may contain a valid short EC or pinned self-signed certificate; the 1024-byte minimum is reserved for the multi-root certifi bundle. Actual SSL parsing and certificate presence validate custom trust material instead of rejecting a valid anchor by byte length. All 45 TLS tests plus 22 plugin-egress tests pass, including real generated certificates and a loopback HTTPS server that fails without and succeeds with the owner CA. No external-provider connection or host trust-store change was performed. Batch-03 collateral review (2026-10-10): the changed evidence paths were compared with their previously current preimages. H329 adds strict global/channel owner switches and exact human-approved re-enable; H299/H397 add result-aware default-advisory stalls. This review preserves the existing contract verdict and remaining limits; unrelated stale pins remain unchanged. Current verification and limits are recorded in docs/plans/2026-10-10-hermes-closure-03.md. Batch-04 bounded collateral review (2026-10-10): changed evidence was checked against its previously current preimage; the existing verdict and remaining limits remain valid. Details are in the batch-04 runtime, surface and history collateral reviews. Batch-05 bounded collateral review (2026-10-10): the H002 usage/terminal-stop changes and additive response fixtures were checked against previously current evidence. Existing verdicts and remaining limits are preserved; already-stale pins remain untouched. See the batch-05 integration and root collateral reports. | <a id="h507"></a>H507 | Warn the model when the code it just wrote contains a known-dangerous pattern | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Code the agent writes is checked against a table of known-dangerous patterns, and the findings go back to the model and the owner. Nothing is ever refused. agents/core/code_guidance.py holds 26 rules (RULES, agents/core/code_guidance.py:89), each scoped to the file types it applies to. They cover unsafe deserialization (pickle/dill/joblib/marshal loads; yaml.load without SafeLoader, and yaml.unsafe_load; torch.load without weights_only=True); command and code injection (os.system/popen, shell=True, Python eval/exec, JS eval and new Function, child_process.exec and execSync, require('child_process').exec, a bare exec( in a file that imports child_process, a Go exec.Command of a shell); SQL built from an f-string or \`%\`; tempfile.mktemp; XSS sinks (innerHTML/outerHTML assignment, document.write, dangerouslySetInnerHTML); and crypto and TLS footguns (ECB mode, createCipher without an IV on any receiver, verify=False, unverified SSL contexts, CERT_NONE, rejectUnauthorized: false, NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED=0, InsecureSkipVerify: true, md5/sha1 over a password). They also cover XXE parser flags and a remote \`\<script\>\` without integrity=. A separate check (_workflow_findings, agents/core/code_guidance.py:189) flags \`${{ github.event.\* }}\` inside a GitHub Actions \`run:\` step, on the run line or in its \`\|\`/\`\>\` block, whose column is the run key's own, so a \`- run: \|\` step's env:, with: and if: keys end it and are never flagged. The guards keep the usual false positives quiet: \`model.eval()\` and \`obj.eval()\`, \`def eval(\`, \`yaml.load(..., Loader=SafeLoader)\`, \`torch.load(..., weights_only=True)\`, \`regex.exec()\`, an \`===\` comparison with innerHTML, \`hashlib.md5(data)\`, a script with integrity=, whole-line comments and non-code files. Every rule is linear: a guard reads the call's balanced arguments across lines, at most MAX_CALL_CHARS (400) of them and at most MAX_CALLS_PER_LINE (8) calls a line (_call_args, agents/core/code_guidance.py:211; _guarded, agents/core/code_guidance.py:226), or the tag it matched. scan (agents/core/code_guidance.py:265) returns \`{rule, line, message}\`, at most 20 findings over the first 1 MB, with lines split at \`\\n\` only (CRLF and CR normalised), and stops after SCAN_SECONDS (0.5 s; agents/core/code_guidance.py:50); a scan cut by size or time ends with a \`scan-incomplete\` finding at the first line it did not finish, so a cut scan never reads as clean. \`.env\`, \`.env.\*\` and the shell dotfiles are scanned as shell. The findings go to five places. On the file_write approval card, FileTools.classify_mutation (agents/core/file_tools.py:1277) adds \`code_warnings\` (rule@line, at most 200 characters), \`code_warning_count\` and a title notice (\`code not fully scanned\` when a limit cut it), scanning the name as spelled and the file the write lands on (a symlink, a \`./\` or \`//\` spelling; labels, agents/core/code_guidance.py:296, names each finding once and in line order); it never sets a class, so H506's approval binding is untouched, and an instruction-file notice and the warning share the title. The gated call's \`approval_required\` answer carries \`code_warnings\`/\`code_warning_count\` (_ANSWER_LABEL_KEYS, agents/core/tool_rpc.py:200), and the tool loop's pause reply names them as warnings, not refusals (_approval_reply, agents/core/agent_runtime.py:1758): the loop ends the turn there, so the model reads them on its next turn and the owner at once. On an ungated write's result (FileTools.write_file, agents/core/file_tools.py:1024), the model reads \`code_warnings\` with a \`code_guidance\` note that they are warnings; an approved write's result lands on the approved task's record, which no model turn reads. A refused write carries none, and a failing scan never fails a write. For a marketplace install (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:858; install and install-zip answer \`code_warnings\` when there are any, agents/core/routers/skills.py:490) and a generated skill (agents/core/skills/loader.py:1211), scan_tree (agents/core/code_guidance.py:340) walks the folder, skipping symlinks; only code files count toward its 200-file bound, a file past 1 MB is read as cut, the whole walk stops after TREE_SECONDS (2 s), and the file a bound stops at gets a \`scan-incomplete\` finding. skill_propose answers the generated skill's \`code_warnings\` (agents/core/skills/tools.py:493), and generate_skill resets them first (agents/core/skills/loader.py:1051), so a skipped generation never keeps another's; the background-review and \[learn:\] generation paths only log them (their main.py is a fixed template). The owner's switch is \`security.code_guidance\` (read by enabled, agents/core/code_guidance.py:161: on by default, only an explicit off turns it off and an unreadable store keeps it on; the settings row is seeded on in settings_db DEFAULTS, and safe mode forces it back on: safe_mode.FORCED_SETTINGS, agents/core/safe_mode.py:110). An aliased child_process module (\`cp.exec\`) is not flagged. tests/test_h507_code_guidance.py (136 cases) covers every rule firing, the guard cases, the cross-language scoping, the finding shape, the table size, the workflow rule with a blank line in a block and every non-run key, the bounds, the labels, the switch, the real ToolRPC card and approved execution, a clean and a non-code write, the shared instruction notice, a failing scan, a refused write, scan_tree (symlink, bounds, switch), generation, install with the route, and the setting row. The review round added crafted megabytes (one per quadratic rule) scanned in under a second and a card labelled promptly through the ToolRPC intake, the time and size cuts, the pause reply driven through the agent loop, skill_propose's warnings and the reset, the symlink and path spellings, a package padded with assets, the \`- run: \|\` block, the wrapped and nested calls, the child_process and createCipher spellings, CR/form-feed/U+2028 lines, the dotfiles and the safe-mode switch. The review round's 44 mutants were all killed, ten after seven cases were added. Existing equivalent verdict and remaining limits are preserved; this is source review, not live-provider evidence. Final positional compatibility reread: the new optional callback argument is appended after all existing register_tool parameters; old classification/threshold/schema positions remain unchanged. This capability's referenced backend modules in the frozen guarded run have 136 passing cases and 0 skips, with no failures; module counts overlap across capabilities. The overall 22,093-case run has one unrelated exact binding-callsite inventory failure, reproduced and corrected separately; it is not a full-suite success. No new frontend, provider, device or live-service acceptance, and historical mutation reports are not fresh mutation runs. See docs/hermes/evidence/h487-consent-anchor-full-review-2026-10-03.md. AST/source review preserves existing offer resolution, loop guards, result spill/guidance/taint, provider wire formats, compaction, recovery and conversation clocks claimed by this row. Event-sink limits and ordinary hangs remain bounded. | <a id="h526"></a>H526 | Make a spoken reply sound like speech rather than read-out markdown | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | A spoken reply sounds like speech rather than read-out markdown, as Hermes requires, at every text-to-speech entry point. agents/core/voice/speech_text.py is the one normaliser (for_speech, agents/core/voice/speech_text.py:161). It drops the file-mutation verifier footer (_VERIFIER_FOOTER, agents/core/voice/speech_text.py:42), \<think\>/\<thinking\>/\<reasoning\> blocks (an unclosed one to the end, and everything before a closing tag that never opened), fenced code (\`\`\` or ~~~, an unclosed fence to the end) and HTML tags. \[text\](url) reads as its text, !\[alt\](url) as its alt, and a bare or \<autolinked\> URL as "link". Headings, list items, task boxes, block quotes and table rows lose their markers and end with a full stop (_line, agents/core/voice/speech_text.py:133); table cells read with commas; separator rows and rules are dropped. Bold, italic, strikethrough and inline-code markers go and their words stay (_inline, agents/core/voice/speech_text.py:122), with word guards so 2\*3\*4 and my_var_name survive. Symbols are said in the reply's language (_symbols, agents/core/voice/speech_text.py:113, WORDS, agents/core/voice/speech_text.py:88): & as and/și, % as percent/la sută, -\> =\> as to/spre, degrees as degrees/grade; the language comes from the request, else a ro-RO voice, else the engine default (speech_lang, agents/core/voice/speech_text.py:104). Emoji go: pictographs, flags, keycaps, skin tones, ZWJ sequences, variation selectors and tag flags (_EMOJI, agents/core/voice/speech_text.py:70). Fish \[emotion\] tags are kept intact. Newlines are flattened to one spoken line. Text with no letter or digit left is "" (_SAYABLE, agents/core/voice/speech_text.py:85), and the result is a fixed point (checked over every shared case). Verifier footer (the clause the 2026-10-10 repair closed). Only the pinned producer's exact shape is removed from speech, and only from speech: the whole warning sentence with the warning sign (with or without U+FE0F), a positive count of at most twenty digits, and a contiguous block of at least one two-space-indented bullet with text, on its own lines, LF or CRLF. Preceding and following prose is kept. Near misses stay spoken exactly as ordinary prose: a header with no bullet, an empty bullet, a count of 0, a count of twenty-one digits, no warning sign, a lone leading space, and ordinary talk about verification. The visible chat text is untouched. One limit is stated plainly: Nerva has no producer of this advisory (it appears nowhere else under agents/); the format is the donor turn finalizer's, so the strip matters for resumed foreign sessions and for model-echoed text, and this row claims nothing about a Nerva-side file-mutation verifier. Wiring. TTSEngine.speak normalises before any backend, Fish included, and synthesises nothing when nothing is left (speak, agents/core/voice/tts.py:216, normalised at agents/core/voice/tts.py:232; speech_for, agents/core/voice/tts.py:211). speak_stream normalises the whole reply before splitting sentences (agents/core/voice/tts.py:315), so a code, reasoning or footer block spanning sentences is dropped whole, and each sentence is then normalised again without change. Through TTSEngine.speak this covers the wake-word VoicePipeline, VoiceChannel.send and chat voice notes: speakable() is for_speech plus its cap (agents/core/channels/spoken_reply.py:69). POST /tts answers 204 with X-Nerva-Speech: nothing_to_say instead of synthesising (agents/core/routers/voice.py:76), and /tts/stream frames carry the spoken sentences. The HUD twin is speechText (frontend/src/speech-text.ts:110), pinned by the same shared cases as the hub. SpeechStreamFilter (frontend/src/speech-text.ts:155) drops a fenced block, a reasoning block or a footer whose markers arrive split across token deltas, holding back only a bounded tail that could still become one, and flushes cleanly. frontend/src/voice.ts uses it on the live sentence stream (frontend/src/voice.ts:297) and normalises each sentence (frontend/src/voice.ts:318); a 204 is silence, never an empty clip or the fallback voice (frontend/src/voice.ts:330). Mobile sends the reply to /tts, so the hub normalises it; ttsFetchBase64 (mobile/src/api/client.ts:2517) reads 204 as '' and speak() finishes at once (mobile/src/api/client.ts:2538, mobile/src/audio/tts.ts). Verification at this head. tests/test_h526_speech_text.py runs every shared case (57) plus the fixed-point property: 135 Python tests pass with the TTS emotion suite. frontend/src/test/speech-text.test.ts and voice-speech-text.test.tsx pass 82 tests, including a live push-to-talk turn with a fence split across deltas, a footer split at many boundaries with CRLF, the plain warning sign, and a zero or over-long count spoken exactly as the whole-reply normaliser speaks it. mobile/src/api/__tests__/tts.test.ts passes 2 under jest. Full backend suite with CI's locked dependencies and its exact command: 25,971 collected, 0 failed, 0 errors, 57 skipped here (missing optional SDKs, live Neo4j, Docker sandbox and other opt-in lanes), working tree unchanged afterwards. Full frontend suite 236 files, 2,198 tests, tsc clean. A first run with an incomplete virtualenv showed 27 failures that were missing bs4/numpy (26 reproduce identically on unmodified main); with the locked dependencies they pass. Mutation pass on the footer logic written in this repair: Python 12 mutants, 11 killed; TypeScript 10 mutants, 8 killed. The first pass left real survivors (a zero count, the twenty-digit cap, the required warning sign, and in the stream filter the plain warning sign and the same count rules); four shared cases and two stream tests now kill them. The three remaining survivors are equivalent (footer replaced with an empty string rather than a space, because the match is bounded by newlines or the string ends; a digit-after-count guard the final startsWith check already implies; \>= for === on a probe that is fed one character at a time). The earlier normaliser had its own pass (126 mutants, 124 caught, two redundant code removed). Not claimed: physical audio output or a live TTS provider. The Chromium run in the 2026-10-10 handoff used fixture STT/chat and a synthetic speech sink. [d1](../agents/core/voice/speech_text.py) [d2](../agents/core/voice/tts.py) [d3](../agents/core/channels/spoken_reply.py) |

| <a id="h529"></a>H529 | Transcribe a voice message or dictated speech | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | Un singur backend. Hermes are opt (faster-whisper local, \`local_command\`, Groq, OpenAI, Mistral Voxtral cu diarizare și timestamp-uri pe cuvânt, xAI, ElevenLabs Scribe, DeepInfra) plus provideri pe comandă de shell, un ABC \`TranscriptionProvider\`, selecție explicită strictă cu auto-detect doar la o instalare neconfigurată vreodată, un plafon pe lungimea promptului Whisper, trunchierea liniștii înainte de upload ca să scadă octeții trimiși, un picker și \`POST /api/audio/transcribe\`. Nimic din astea nu e livrat. Nu din lene: clauza de guvernanță a rândului spune că orice STT în cloud trece prin \`plugin.egress\` și trimite vorbirea înregistrată a owner-ului în afara cutiei — e aceeași clasă de dezvăluire ca TTS-ul în cloud și trebuie clasificat, nu pus ca implicit de config. Cache-ul motorului stă tot în \`routers/voice.py\`, care e casa greșită pentru el; canalul îl importă în loc să-l dubleze, iar mutarea lui e o schimbare separată. NEVERIFICAT pe servicii reale: niciun model Whisper încărcat cu adevărat. [d1](../agents/core/voice/stt.py) [d2](../agents/core/voice/hallucination.py) [d3](../agents/core/channels/inbound_voice.py) |
| <a id="h530"></a>H530 | Not turn a silent recording into a message | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Whisper nu tace când nu aude nimic — inventează. Pe liniște întoarce o propoziție fluentă din datele de antrenament: generice de subtitrare („Subtitles by the Amara.org community", „Subtitrarea: ..."), formule de încheiere („Thanks for watching!", „Mulțumesc pentru vizionare!"). Pentru o casetă de dictare e o enervare; aici e un control de siguranță, exact cum spune clauza de guvernanță a rândului: un mesaj vocal devine o tură \*inbound\*, o tură inbound poate cere lucruri, deci un transcript halucinat e o instrucțiune pe care n-a autorizat-o nimeni, intrată într-un sistem guvernat. \`hallucination.py\` o oprește. Designul e dictat de asimetria erorilor: a pierde un mesaj real e inacceptabil (cineva zice „mulțumesc" și dispare fără răspuns), a lăsa unul să treacă costă o tură ignorabilă. De aici două niveluri, nu o listă: frazele distinctive (\`CANNED\`) cad pe text; cele pe care oamenii chiar le spun (\`AMBIGUOUS\`) cad doar când durata \*decodată\* arată că înregistrarea era prea scurtă ca să le conțină — iar un apelant care nu știe durata le păstrează. Potrivirea e pe transcriptul întreg, niciodată pe substring; regula pentru generice cere marker ȘI vocabular exclusiv de generic, fiindcă prima variantă (doar markerul) mânca „am văzut pe amara.org un film bun". Filtrul rulează în \`STTEngine.transcribe\` și întoarce \`\[silence\]\` — santinela care exista deja și pe care fiecare apelant o tratează ca „nu s-a spus nimic" — deci ruta de dictare din browser o primește gratuit, nu doar calea nouă de pe Telegram. CORECTARE la auditul din 07.09: verdictul „missing" era pe jumătate greșit. A doua cerință a rândului — auto-stop pe liniște (VAD), ca utilizatorul să nu apese o tastă — EXISTĂ de mult în \`frontend/src/voice.ts\`: \`recordUtterance\` eșantionează RMS la 60 ms și oprește după \`SILENCE_MS\`, cu plafon dur și abandon când nimeni nu vorbește. Auditul a dat un \`rg -i 'vad'\` peste \`frontend/src/\` și a raportat „nimic relevant"; linia 111 spune „VAD" în litere. Ce lipsea cu adevărat era dovada: nimic nu o testa, fiindcă harness-ul comun folosește un MediaRecorder care se oprește singur și scurtcircuitează intervalul. \`voice-vad.test.tsx\` folosește unul care NU se oprește niciodată singur — dacă o tură se termină acolo, doar detectorul de liniște a putut-o termina — și acoperă fereastra de liniște, plafonul, abandonul și faptul că vorbirea continuă resetează ceasul. Named speech compatibility review: the Whisper hallucination filter and decoded-duration handling remain on the native transcribe path; named commands preserve the existing exact-sentinel behavior. Hallucination and speech selection regressions pass. [d1](../agents/core/voice/hallucination.py) [d2](../agents/core/voice/stt.py) [d3](../frontend/src/voice.ts) |

| <a id="h544"></a>H544 | Steer, inspect and control a running editor thread (in-thread commands + live rendering) | copy | Lipsă | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h545"></a>H545 | Let the editor hand the agent MCP servers that exist only for that session | copy | Lipsă | Reevaluat | Every accepted part is missing: (1) an ACP session entry point (session/new, load, resume, fork) that accepts an mcpServers array; this depends on H542. (2) A session-scoped MCP registry layered over the global MCPManager, so that a server registered for one session is invisible to every other session and is torn down when the session ends. (3) Off-loop registration (asyncio.to_thread) with a bounded discovery join (default 1.5 s), so a dead server cannot block session creation. (4) A scheduled late refresh that re-reads the session's tool list after slow discovery finishes. (5) Governance: session-supplied servers pass through the same outbound MCP authorization gate as configured servers (H554, still partial), and their trust tier is capped at no wider than a configured server's.
