| <a id="h551"></a>H551 | Choose which of a server's tools the model may see | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Filtrele allow/deny pe nume și glob (\`tools_allow\`/\`tools_deny\`, potrivite cu \`fnmatchcase\` pe numele original al uneltei) restrâng atât listarea, cât și apelarea uneltelor MCP; deny prevalează (\`tool_visible\`, agents/core/mcp/client.py:603-610). \`_list_tools\` nu oferă o unealtă ascunsă (:596-601), iar \`MCPServer.call_tool\` răspunde \`tool_filtered\` înainte să plece vreun frame (:616-617), deci scoaterea unei unelte din filtru o revocă imediat, și la apel, nu doar la înregistrare. Un filtru malformat îngustează, nu lărgește: un allow care nu e listă devine \`\[\]\` (\`_clean_tool_patterns\`, :124-135). Limitele (64 de pattern-uri / 128 de caractere, :120-121) sunt fail-closed. Ruta de add (\`admin_mcp_add\`, agents/core/routers/mcp.py:214-223) refuză cu 400 \`{"error": "invalid_tool_filter", "field": ...}\` un \`tools_allow\` sau \`tools_deny\` peste limite, în loc să-l taie (\`tool_patterns_over_bounds\`, client.py:138-154), la fel ca pe o valoare care nu e listă sau pe o intrare goală. Un deny care nu poate fi păstrat (peste 64 de pattern-uri, un pattern peste 128 de caractere, sau care nu e listă) venit dintr-un config editat manual prin \`load_from_config\` îngustează serverul la nimic (\`tools_allow = \[\]\`) și loghează un avertisment (:407-418), pentru că un deny tăiat ar face din nou vizibilă unealta pe care o numea. Filtrele fac round-trip prin \`to_config\`/\`load_from_config\` (:808-809, :852-853). Teste (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py, 13/13 verzi): listare și deny prevalează (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:47-57), unealtă filtrată refuzată la apel pe nume (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:60-70), filtru malformat → nimic și round-trip de config (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:73-84), ruta primește filtre și refuză formele malformate (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:98-109), ruta refuză 129 de caractere / 65 de intrări pe ambele câmpuri (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:121-129) și acceptă un filtru exact la limită (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:132-138), un deny imposibil de păstrat ascunde toate uneltele cu avertisment (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:141-147), iar un config editat manual cu deny peste limite se încarcă fail-closed fără să atingă serverul vecin (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:150-159). Resursele/prompts sunt urmărite separat în H552. MCP transport audit collateral review (2026-10-09): the route adds only fixed-field authentication telemetry at the existing RPC gate. This row's admin/listing, validation or transport-auth behavior and remaining limitations are unchanged; its previously current mcp.py pin and explicit coordinates were reviewed. MCP token audit collateral review (2026-10-10): the six-line insertion follows successful inbound token signing and only submits fixed-field telemetry. Existing admin configuration, stdio environment, outbound filters/trust and absence of ACP session-scoped servers or outbound approval/audit are unchanged; cited coordinates precede the insertion. Independent source review and all five cited/relevant MCP suites (67 tests) preserve this verdict and its gaps. | <a id="h579"></a>H579 | Pull a file or slice of a file into a message inline (@ context references) | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The owner's own chat attaches @file:path, @file:path#L10-40 and @file:"a b.md" for the turn. agents/core/context_refs.py holds the rules. expand (agents/core/context_refs.py:209) finds a reference that starts a word or follows an opening bracket, quote or backtick, and not one inside a word or a path (x@file:, a/@file:, @@file:) (_REF_RE, agents/core/context_refs.py:49); a double-quoted path may hold spaces, #L7 is one line and #L2-L3 a range, and trailing punctuation or a closing quote is not part of it. The routes keep the owner's message as typed: commands, skills, LLM-backend control, the router and memory see only it. The "--- Attached Context ---" section is bound to the turn (bind_attached, agents/core/context_refs.py:251; agents/web.py:1317 on /chat, and inside the stream task at agents/web.py:1415) and appended after the message only in the model prompt (attached_block, agents/core/context_refs.py:266; Orchestrator._build_agent_turn_text, agents/core/orchestrator.py:3815), so the file text is never stored as the owner's words, replayed in a later turn's history or embedded. Hermes keeps the expanded message in the user turn; Nerva shows the attachment to the model for the attaching (tainted) turn only. Each file's text is in a fence longer than any backtick run in it, so no line of it can close the fence (_fence, agents/core/context_refs.py:194), under a header that names the file as typed and the lines actually attached, \`(line 7)\` or \`(lines 2-3)\`, as attached file content, not instructions; injection patterns and a forged Attached Context marker are flagged in that header (\`flagged: …\`, _flags, agents/core/context_refs.py:180), not withheld. Each path is resolved through the same FileScope as file_read (_attach_one, agents/core/context_refs.py:133), so outside the file roots, .. traversal, a symlink escape and a secret-looking path such as .env are refused. A missing file, a directory, a binary file (a NUL in its first 8 KB), a bad line range, a range past the end, an unreadable file, an over-long name, a denied stat and an unknown ~user each become a one-line warning in the same section instead of failing the message. With JARVIS_FILE_ROOTS unset, the file tools' default root \<data home\>/workspace is used; only an unusable JARVIS_FILE_ROOTS makes the section say nothing was attached. The limits are 8 distinct references per message, 64 KB per reference and 128 KB in all, counted in bytes. A cut reference says so, and a repeated one is attached once. A line range skips the lines before it a 64K-character chunk at a time, up to 64M characters into the file (MAX_SCAN_CHARS; past it, the warning "line N is too far into the file"), keeps at most the byte budget and never reads a line whole (_read_lines, agents/core/context_refs.py:105); invalid UTF-8 is read with replacement characters. The expansion runs on /chat and /chat/stream (agents/web.py:1306, agents/web.py:1534), off the event loop, so both the HUD and nerva chat get it; the one message that skips it is a raw checkpoint command (\`/rollback\` or \`/checkpoints\` as the whole message, checkpoint_command_name), which never becomes a model turn, so nothing is read, bound or tainted for it. It never runs for a channel message, as Hermes' gateway does not expand either. A turn that attached anything is tainted: handle_input and handle_input_stream call _taint_attached_context (agents/core/orchestrator.py:378, :2372, :2593), which raises the turn's action origin to recall:untrusted. An action planned from attached content therefore escalates GRANT to QUEUE at the kernel, while an inbound turn keeps its more specific origin. complete (agents/core/context_refs.py:272) lists in-scope completions for what follows @file:, with directories ending in / and a path with a space quoted (a name no reference could name is left out). It shows dot files only when asked and never secret paths or links out of the roots, is bounded to 50 entries, and returns nothing for a prefix over 1,024 characters. GET /api/context-refs (agents/core/routers/commands.py:63, user-guarded, read-only) serves it and the reference types, and answers 503 file_roots_unusable when the roots cannot be used. The HUD composer (frontend/src/cockpit.tsx:229, :284, frontend/src/context-refs.tsx) offers the reference types while \`@\` or \`@fi\` is typed (Tab takes \`@file:\`), then the in-scope paths; a list is shown only for the text it was fetched for. tests/test_h579_context_refs.py (70 cases) covers the bounds, parsing, whole files, line ranges (single, L-form, past the end), every refusal and warning, a symlink, punctuation, word starts, dedupe, the byte cut, the shared budget, the reference limit, an unreadable file, invalid UTF-8, the roots, an explicit scope and the marker; completion (prefixes, dot files, secrets, roots, a long prefix, a file or missing folder, the bound); both routes, the completion route, the turn taint on both paths, and an inbound turn. The review round added attached text never read as the owner's command (a slash command, a skill, a backend switch) and never stored or carried to a later turn, through the real routes; a file that cannot close its fence; injection and a forged marker flagged; a range in one huge line bounded in memory and a range of many short lines linear; a range too far into a file; quoted paths and their completion; references after brackets and quotes and not inside words; the distinct-reference limit; the header's path and lines; paths the system refuses to look at; and the roots. frontend/src/test/context-refs.test.tsx has 6 cases. The row's 53 mutants predate this code; the review round's 34: 28 caught at once, five after five cases were added, and one equivalent (a kept read stops at the budget either way, since readline(0) answers ""). Local auxiliary diff review (2026-10-02,aa4865b5): the claimed behavior is outside the four changed producer methods. Parent AST comparison and re-read source/citations confirm unchanged mechanisms; the cited backend test modules pass in the frozen full run. Existing frontend evidence remains tied to unchanged source. No new capability or mutation claim is added. Re-review on 2026-10-02: InputBar now passes its active agent into the image hook; inline file-reference completion and insertion remain unchanged. Selected-main image collateral review (2026-10-02, 2fce39ca): The source diff adds optional bounded image provenance and a new reviewed conversation-image path. Existing no-media memory, text turns, previous composer route, and unrelated orchestrator/HUD behavior retain their prior branches; focused H277/H513/composer/session tests, 1,872 frontend tests, TypeScript and HUD build pass. Existing verdict preserved; this note does not claim live-provider acceptance or a new Hermes capability. Collateral active-image review (2026-10-03, a30603e0): cockpit submit readiness changed only for explicit image selections and orchestrator added a referenced-image provenance marker. The @file context reference parser, completion and insertion branches are unchanged at their pinned citations; the existing equivalent verdict is preserved. Collateral actuation review (2026-10-03, Task3 working tree): The orchestrator edit is limited to the post-answer approval-observation acknowledgment. Attached-file expansion, taint and prompt insertion retain their cited paths. Existing equivalent verdict and remaining limits are preserved; this is source review, not live-provider evidence. Collateral review (2026-10-03, fresh owner authority): @file parsing, taint insertion and prompt attachment still use unchanged branches. The owner-principal edit changes who is an admin in Telegram, not the web-only attachment path or its bounds. The prior equivalent verdict is retained from claim-specific source review; this is no new parity or live acceptance claim. Re-read (2026-10-11, PR #1249 head f9e130b5): agents/web.py now skips the expansion (and the notes prefix) for a raw checkpoint command, which never reaches the model; every other message still expands on both routes, is bound to the turn and reset after it. The orchestrator and cockpit drift moved the cited lines without touching the taint, the attached block or the composer's completion. The test file's hub fixture now binds a native checkpoint session (the routes' owner guard) and the off-the-event-loop test observes the expander's thread through the real /chat and /chat/stream functions instead of reading their source; still 70 cases, all passing, with the 6 frontend cases. settings_db.py is no longer pinned: no claim of this row lives there. | <a id="h594"></a>H594 | Have the agent pick up a project's conventions automatically (context files) | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva reads a project's convention files into the turn, as Hermes reads its context files and subdirectory hints. agents/core/project_context.py walks from the nearest git root (never above the file root) down to the working directory (llm.project_dir when it is a folder inside the roots, H218; else the first JARVIS_FILE_ROOTS root; since #1233 a bound kanban workspace's cwd instead, read inside that workspace's scope), and reads AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, .cursorrules and .cursor/rules/\*.mdc in each directory in that order (CONVENTION_NAMES, agents/core/project_context.py:59; candidates, agents/core/project_context.py:168). A directory that the model's file_read, file_list or file_search touches is noted for the session (note_tool_path, agents/core/project_context.py:408, bounded to 8 directories and 256 sessions, agents/core/project_context.py:70-71); its chain is read on every later turn, and the tool's own result carries the files the turn had not seen yet, marked tainted so the loop fences it (attach, agents/core/project_context.py:427; _with_project_context, agents/core/file_tools.py:653, called by the three read tools at agents/core/file_tools.py:841, agents/core/file_tools.py:897 and agents/core/file_tools.py:1020). A script's file call (execute_code, no tool turn) brings none. A local terminal_run runs only after approval, by the worker from the queue: the queuing turn's session is recorded at intake (note_task, agents/core/project_context.py:439) — on ToolRPCServer.handle's generic gated path (agents/core/tool_rpc.py:638) and, since #1233 gave terminal_run its own trusted intake, in _terminal_intake (agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:1192, the call at agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:1257; registered with gated_intake and gated_review at agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:1284-1285) — and when the approved command ran locally, note_terminal (agents/core/project_context.py:451; keyed by the task id, not by a turn) notes its cwd for that session, so its next turn reads the chain (_rpc_terminal_run, agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:1189). Normally the command runs outside any turn and its result goes to the task row, not to the model; with Smart review or a live owner-once/consent reply, the registrar's gated_review (review_terminal_task, agents/core/autonomy/terminal_review.py:22-24) awaits the worker's own run of that task and hands the queue-verified result back to the model in the same turn — the directory is noted either way. A project SOUL.md, HEARTBEAT.md or IDENTITY.md is never read, not even through a symlink (HUB_FILES, agents/core/project_context.py:63; _admit, agents/core/project_context.py:183). Every file is resolved through FileScope (outside the roots, symlink escape and secret paths refused), must be a regular file (a FIFO is never opened), and is bounded to 20 KB, with 40 KB and 12 files a turn and 8 rules a directory (agents/core/project_context.py:65-68), shared by parallel tool calls under the turn's lock; a cut says how much was shown, a file past the budget is named, and files past a count cap are counted (load_file, agents/core/project_context.py:220; collect, agents/core/project_context.py:239; render, agents/core/project_context.py:274). Each file's text and name are scanned with detect_injection_normalized, plus Unicode TAG characters (decoded and scanned) and the fence's markers (_detect, agents/core/project_context.py:122). A flagged file becomes a \[BLOCKED ...\] line naming the rules (injection_flag_names) with none of its text, and a flagged name is withheld; other names have non-printable characters escaped and undecodable bytes replaced. The caveated block, which holds the files in the untrusted-data fence, sits after the core-memory block in _build_agent_turn_text (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3831-3837); _begin_project_context (agents/core/orchestrator.py:388-416) builds it off the event loop at turn start in both turn paths (agents/core/orchestrator.py:2436 and agents/core/orchestrator.py:2664) and marks the turn recall-tainted when any file was given, so an action planned from it escalates GRANT to QUEUE. A noted directory's files are in every later turn of the session, so those turns are tainted too: web_search, web_extract and memory writes refuse until the setting is off or the hub restarts. A guest's turn is given none of them. It is off with llm.project_context_files (agents/core/project_context.py:74) and in safe mode (layer project_context, agents/core/safe_mode.py:77, named on the HUD banner; enabled, agents/core/project_context.py:312). tests/test_h594_project_context.py: 66 cases. The original build's 51 mutants were all killed; the review round's fixes each come with a case that fails without them. Independent #1233 review (historical scope): Project convention files and noted file-tool directories still feed the later turn through project_context. The terminal intake still calls note_task, and the approved terminal handler still calls note_terminal with the actual cwd/result; the new signed owner-once continuation reaches that same handler only after a live owner reply and private worker claim. The source/guest taint and admission rules remain. The prior equivalent code verdict remains supported as code behavior, without claiming a live project run. The frozen local checkpoint selected 1,346 tests: 1,338 passed and 8 skipped, with no full-suite, provider, or live-service claim. Claim-specific provenance review (2026-10-03, c70e0407): matched the stale coordinator/ToolRPC pins to local historical Git contents and inspected their complete diffs. Terminal consent registration metadata preserves this capability's accepted code contract; every other evidence pin already matches current bytes. This capability's referenced backend modules in the frozen guarded run have 85 passing cases and 1 skips, with no failures; module counts overlap across capabilities. The overall 22,093-case run has one unrelated exact binding-callsite inventory failure, reproduced and corrected separately; it is not a full-suite success. No new frontend, provider, device or live-service acceptance, and historical mutation reports are not fresh mutation runs. See docs/hermes/evidence/h487-consent-anchor-full-review-2026-10-03.md. Telegram/CLI collateral review (2026-10-04, base42840e3d): reviewed the bounded source diff and this row's claimed entry points. Changes are confined to approval consent, reserved callback lanes and terminal wiring; existing unrelated producer/consumer branches and this verdict are retained. Current citation/source review and separate regression evidence are recorded in the Telegram milestone; no new completion or fresh live/mutation acceptance credit. Owner-once outcome collateral review (2026-10-04): The sole changed actuation-test expectation reports the actual authenticated owner denial instead of the earlier guardian label. Its CAS and zero-effect assertions and the underlying project convention ingestion/approved terminal note implementations are unchanged. Existing equivalent verdict and limits are preserved; no new completion credit. Batch collateral review (2026-10-04, base9bfa085c): The manual Docker continuation reaches the established terminal note path only after current mediation; project_context and file-tool directory note behavior are unchanged. H011 file-history collateral review (2026-10-04, dfc26172): claim-specific source diff/AST and affected regressions preserve this verdict and its limits. Only snapshot/index actuation changes; detailed evidence is in docs/hermes/evidence/h011-file-history-collateral-review-2026-10-04.json. No new parity or live acceptance credit. Restamp re-read (2026-10-11, HEAD f9e130b5, PR #1249): five pins had drifted, all through #1233 (332b16ca) and the Codex sprint. agents/core/file_tools.py (H011 checkpoint history; the registered handlers now pick the instance per call through file_tools_for_workspace, whose kanban worker subclass overrides only _resolve_read and _spill_file, so read_file, list_dir and search_files still end in _with_project_context) and agents/core/safe_mode.py (kanban keys added to FORCED_SETTINGS) touch no claim. agents/core/tool_rpc.py and agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py moved the queuing-session record: terminal_run now registers a trusted gated_intake and gated_review, so note_task runs in _terminal_intake rather than on handle's generic path, and the review can hand the worker's queue-verified result to the model in the same turn; the terminal sentences above were corrected and agents/core/autonomy/terminal_review.py pinned for that claim. agents/core/orchestrator.py added a bound kanban workspace's cwd and scope to _begin_project_context; block order, the guest refusal and the recall taint are unchanged. agents/core/project_context.py and both pinned tests are byte-identical to their pins. Run in this session (python3.12 -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider, JARVIS_HOME and TMPDIR under the scratch directory): tests/test_h594_project_context.py 66 passed, tests/test_h485_owner_once_actuation.py 20 passed, both exit 0; test_terminal_run_approved_from_the_queue_notes_its_cwd drives the real rail at HEAD (_wire_agent_tool_runtime, handle, _terminal_intake, accept, worker tick, note_terminal). No live project run, no fresh mutation run and no full-suite claim. [d1](../agents/core/project_context.py) [d2](../agents/core/file_tools.py) [d3](../agents/core/orchestrator.py) |

| <a id="h659"></a>H659 | A retried external request returns the original run instead of starting a second one | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The three native external intakes reserve an authenticated caller scope before enqueue or execution: owner action intake, webhook ID and verified A2A peer. agents/core/idempotency.py:147 uses SQLite BEGIN IMMEDIATE with a unique (scope,key), a length-framed method/path/body fingerprint and bounded public references, never bodies or credentials. The visible-ASCII key grammar, durable file-store property, restart/racing-worker behavior, conflict/in-progress/replay handling, attempt fencing, bounded expiry and webhook heartbeat remain implemented. Actions reserve before q.request (agents/core/routers/actions.py:76); A2A reserves after peer verification and webhooks after token/signature and enabled checks. Ninety focused regressions passed. These are native single-owner/webhook/peer scopes, not a claim that Hermes RoomLink exists. Reservations expire after 24 hours and stale pending attempts after 600 seconds; outcome-write exhaustion can allow a later retry to run again. This is bounded retry deduplication, not an unconditional exactly-once distributed transaction. The existing target-specific failed-turn recognition limitation is unchanged. | <a id="h661"></a>H661 | Oversized tool output is paged from a file instead of being thrown away | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | execute_code spools stdout/stderr while one-shot and resident-session output arrives, keeping 5,000,000 bytes per stream by default and appending one explicit cap marker. Inline output retains head/tail. Successful spills carry the exact file_read arguments with byte offset/max_bytes and next_offset paging; the real reader reconstructs the captured output without rerunning the privileged tool. Failed open/write/finalization omits the spill reference while preserving the ordinary tool response. Paths use the native managed data root, and paging uses byte offsets rather than upstream line offsets. A reader recipe is withheld when file_read is outside the current offer or cannot reach that exact spill; owner retention remains explicit. Re-read/search results retain taint. No code changes were required for this review. Independent source review found no concrete contract gap. All 215 cases in the five focused code/spill/output modules pass in the current backend milestone. | <a id="h670"></a>H670 | The default identity prompt is a behavior contract, and it lives in one file | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The shared identity band exists. agents/_identity/IDENTITY.md is one behaviour contract: the reply-sizing rule (match length to the weight of the ask), earned depth, the named prohibitions (no filler, no restating the request, no re-summarizing, no narrating visible tool calls), the anti-sycophancy line ('agree because it is right, not because the owner said it') and 'say when you do not know'. Its reply rules govern the agent's own replies to the owner: text drafted in someone else's voice (an email, a post, a message written as the owner) keeps that voice, greetings and sign-offs included, and a persona's warmth or curiosity lives inside the answer (review-H670 m-5); its honesty rules hold for everything an agent writes, drafted text included (review-H670b m-1). Its leading maintainer note (stripped before the model sees it, _strip_maintainer_note, agents/core/agent.py:453) says never to add 'be targeted and efficient in your exploration', and why. The repo-root SOUL.md mirrors the text, so agents working on the repo read the contract the product ships; a test keeps the two equal. identity_path (agents/core/agent.py:438) resolves it as soul_path_for does a persona: the data home's souls/IDENTITY.local.md, then a gitignored agents/_identity/IDENTITY.local.md, then the shipped file. read_identity (agents/core/agent.py:481) runs it through the same H387 helpers as a SOUL (the injection scan on the uncapped body, then the cap), announces a flagged or truncated contract (its blocked and quarantined stubs name the contract, not a persona), caps it at its own 4,000 characters (IDENTITY_MAX_CHARS, never above the persona's cap, so the two bands stay within the persona cap plus 4,000), and reads it once per settled file signature for the whole process. An override that cannot be read (not UTF-8, a directory, no permission) is reported at ERROR and the shipped contract used instead, cached against the shipped file's signature so an edit of it is seen; a contract that cannot be located or read at all leaves the agent without it; none of these stops an agent from being built. Agent.system_prompt (agents/core/agent.py:572) is the contract, then the agent's persona (either may be empty; an agent without a SOUL still gets the contract). Every place a system prompt is built uses it: Agent.process (agents/core/agent.py:1090) and the synthesis path in agent.py, and the orchestrator's two sites (agents/core/orchestrator.py:2789, agents/core/orchestrator.py:4642) through _system_prompt_of (agents/core/orchestrator.py:512). At a compaction boundary refresh_soul (agents/core/agent.py:741) re-reads the contract as it re-reads the persona (_refresh_identity, agents/core/agent.py:610): a few stats when unchanged; when the file that resolves is absent, vanishes before the read or cannot be read, the contract in force is kept, reported at the boundary as failed-open and warned once per episode (an override absent for an instant resolves to the shipped file for that boundary, as a persona's does); and a moved contract reported as rebuilt, so an edit reaches running agents without a restart. The contract in force at start is committed to the prompt VC (SoulVersionStore) as _identity (_record_identity_version, agents/core/orchestrator.py:494), a no-op when unchanged or empty, so its history is recorded and diffable through /api/admin/prompts/_identity/... beside the personas; what is served is the file (a rollback in the VC does not change it), and an edit picked up at a boundary is recorded at the next start. The prompt-size report counts it as a shared component every call pays (identity_component, agents/core/prompt_size.py:136), and the packaged build ships it through its agents/\*/\*.md glob (overrides, \*.local.md, never ship). IDENTITY.md and its .local overlay are in the H506 instruction-file class (INSTRUCTION_BASE_NAMES, agents/core/file_tools.py:260), so a file write to them asks as an instruction file; a test derives the class from every name the contract's and a persona's loaders can resolve. The data home's README lists souls/IDENTITY.local.md. Persona SOULs and Persona.prompt_block (agents/core/cognition/persona.py:142) stay the character layer on top. tests/test_identity_contract.py (18 cases) covers every rule's presence, the banned line's absence and the kept warning, the mirror, the scan verdict, every active agent's system prompt starting with the contract (and exactly contract then persona), an agent without a persona, the orchestrator's helper and all four sites, both overrides and their order, the scan and the cap on an override, an edit reaching a running agent at the boundary, the one-stat unchanged path, a failed re-read keeping the last-good contract, the version record (once, and a failing store never stopping a start) and one read shared across agents; tests/test_h670b_identity_review.py (21 cases) covers the first review: the instruction-file class, a contract absent or vanishing at a boundary, a non-UTF-8 or unreadable override, the contract's own cap, no empty version, the start-time record, the contract's scope, a mid-file comment, the prompt-size component, the ERROR on a flagged contract, the stubs' wording and CHT-117; tests/test_h670c_identity_review.py (14 cases) covers the second review: honesty unscoped, a shipped contract that stops being UTF-8, a vanish before the real read, the hub's start recording the contract, one warning per episode and a second one, a reader that raises, the fallback following a shipped edit, a directory override, a permission error at load and at a boundary, the fallback rules once when both bands are blocked, and the docs. The first review (one major: the contract was outside the instruction-file class; seven minors: a missing contract emptied at a boundary, a non-UTF-8 override stopped every agent, the two bands exceeded the cap, the VC's rollback and A/B claim, the contract's unscoped rules against drafting personas and trait directives, six untested survivors, CHT-117 naming a retired agent; six nits) is answered; its six real survivors are pinned. The feature's 17 mutants were caught but one equivalent; the first review round's 15 were caught, one only against a stand-in (the vanish case, now driven through the real read). The second review (no major; three minors: the scope lifted the honesty rules from drafted text, a shipped contract that stopped being UTF-8 emptied the contract at a boundary, three real survivors; eight nits) is answered; its 31 mutants' three real survivors are pinned, and this round's 10 were all caught, two after their cases were added (a reader that raises, a second warning episode). Known limits: the prompt-size report measures the shipped contract, not an override in force (as it measures shipped personas); PromptRefresh.text is still the persona alone; the contract's scan verdict is reported only in the log (a persona's also reaches GET /api/agents/{id}/soul); the data home's README is written once, so an existing install does not get its new row. | <a id="h677"></a>H677 | Boot warm-up before accepting work, and every shutdown wait has a short explicit budget | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The bounded warm-up gate, status reporting, per-step shutdown budgets and ChatLanes ordering/8-turn concurrency retain their existing implementations. Ordinary Telegram batched turns, generic callbacks, group observations and rejection reasons enter the chat lane. Reserved current aut1 callbacks have a separate post-flush reply lane; reserved autc callbacks intercept before flush, and only registered current offers use their independent bounded32-task lane. Stale or malformed autc callbacks refuse promptly outside the ordinary lane. Stop revokes both registries, cancels every reserved fast-task set (five since the pending-input lane landed) and waits on their combined set for at most LANE_DRAIN_BUDGET before ordinary lane drain. Offline lifecycle/owner/consent regressions preserve the accepted boot/shutdown verdict; this does not establish live shutdown or renew older mutation receipts. Batch collateral review (2026-10-04, base9bfa085c): Native reusable-consent \`/deny\` joins aut1/autc as a reserved fast-lane exception: original direct replies to current delivered cards use a bounded private task set; stop cancels and drains it. Ordinary ChatLanes ordering, warm-up, status and shutdown budgets remain unchanged. This does not prove live Telegram shutdown. Owner-once denial collateral review (2026-10-04, base 22005244): Telegram now has a fourth bounded fast-task set for native owner-once \`/deny\` replies. \`stop\` revokes both registries, cancels and waits on the union of owner-once callbacks, owner-once denials, reusable-consent callbacks and reusable-consent denials before the ordinary lane drain (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:379-404). The existing warm-up, status, per-step budget and ChatLanes ordering/concurrency code is unchanged. The owner-once offline test exercises the occupied-lane dispatch; the four-set shutdown claim was from direct source review, not a fresh live shutdown or mutation proof. Re-read (2026-10-11, base f9e130b5, PR #1249): the drift since the pins is the pending-input (H067) Telegram transport and forum-topic replies; no lifecycle claim regressed. \`stop\` now cancels five reserved fast-task sets (owner-once callbacks, owner-once denials, reusable-consent callbacks, reusable-consent denials and pending-input \`h067:\` callbacks) and waits on their union for at most LANE_DRAIN_BUDGET (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:396-404), then drains the ordinary lanes and closes the ephemeral system-notice deletes within the same budget (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:421-425). \`h067:\` callbacks are intercepted ahead of the consent parsing into their own bounded 32-task set and answer 'Not applied.' when the set is full or the transport is stopped (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:883-898). Fresh, directly authored, un-forwarded text that answers a delivered clarification may be consumed by the pending-reply handler before batching and the chat lane (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:1047-1050); that handler is awaited inline in the poll loop and only a \`True\` result skips the ordinary path (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:1071-1080); its gateway and orchestrator halves are documented as pending-only ingress with no model fallback (read, not pinned). The stop-cancel of the fifth set is exercised offline (tests/test_pending_input_native_transport.py:303-322) and the Telegram lane drain stays within budget (tests/test_h677_boot_and_shutdown.py:756-770). The warm-up gate waits at most the configured budget and opens either way (agents/core/lifecycle_budget.py:102-119); the lifespan still runs it between load_agents and start_channels (tests/test_h677_boot_and_shutdown.py:165-171); /api/status and /readyz still report it (agents/core/routers/status.py:119-120; agents/core/routers/ops.py:142-143); every channel stop is bounded by CHANNEL_STOP_BUDGET (agents/core/channels/manager.py:121-127) and the orchestrator's teardown waits stay bounded in the source (tests/test_h677_boot_and_shutdown.py:481-489); lanes still run 8 turns at once (agents/core/channels/chat_lanes.py:21) and drain within a budget (agents/core/channels/chat_lanes.py:66-78). Drift notes outside the pins: the orchestrator's new stop_channels waits (session-lifecycle stall-watcher close, 2 s default; embeddings flush, 5 s) and the lifespan's new Hermes-runtime stop (process STOP_TIMEOUT 7 s then kill, websocket close_timeout 3 s, bridge thread join 2 s; a no-op unless that runtime was started) are internally bounded, though the latter is not wrapped in bounded(). agents/web.py, agents/core/orchestrator.py and agents/core/settings_db.py are no longer pinned: their H677 code is unchanged and the lifespan-ordering, source-scan and setting-declaration tests carry those claims. Offline verdict (165 + 32 tests passed on this base); not a live shutdown proof. Immutable adapter snapshots isolate declarations; Anthropic empty controls, budget mappings and compatible/Gemini shaping use the shared rule. The ultra/xhigh inversion is regression-tested on the native generic provider rail. Current focused registry/adapter/invocation verification passes 207 cases with fake HTTP; no live provider acceptance is claimed. | <a id="h684"></a>H684 | A read-only scheduler health check that exits non-zero when jobs are silently not firing | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Whole frozen H684 doctor contract reviewed, batch 06 (2026-10-10). Read-only doctor groups job last-run and persisted delivery failures, active jobs missing registration/next-run and overdue/invalid next-run times. It detects no_agent without a script, missing/escaping/nonregular native scripts through metadata only, and unavailable configured workdirs under approved terminal roots. CLI unhealthy or malformed responses exit nonzero; API is an admin-guarded GET. Inspection does not execute/read script bytes or secrets, mutate jobs, repair, restart or control the gateway. Direct-attempt recovery lives exclusively on registration/execution paths, never doctor. DB total_changes/state and diagnostic exclusion regressions cover this boundary. The exact frozen clause requires configured-path availability, not unsupported model workspace context or wider job options. Native scripts root and approved script-only workdir are the accepted mapping. Broader options and production-host observation remain independently open. Focused doctor/scheduler/script/workdir/CLI health regressions passed; the root route/doctor selection passed 43 cases and the 476-case core selection includes relevant job modules. Full backend milestone is recorded separately; no native-host execution claim. | <a id="h689"></a>H689 | One stable identity for this install, and per-profile isolation of its runtime artifacts | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | One durable 32-hex installation ID now belongs to the validated unprofiled root and is shared across default/named profiles; data, credentials and hub locks stay profile-local. Locked atomic publication adopts only one unambiguous legacy identity, preserves canonical/legacy bytes and refuses conflicting or damaged state. Activation migration requires the selected profile's matching legacy proof; foreign records require reconciliation. Missing ID refuses node grants/deeplinks with fixed 503; unbound old links cannot approve senders. Windows PID updates preserve the lock inode and byte. Verification: 199 implementer and 169 independent focused tests passed (overlapping), including process races and simulated Windows reacquisition; native Windows acceptance not claimed.
